# Rashi on II Kings

רש"י על מלכים ב

Link: [https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings)

הפירוש הנפוץ והמוכר ביותר לתורה, המבאר את פשטי המקראות בתוספת הרחבות פרשניות; חובר במאה ה-11.

Most widely-read biblical commentary, compiled in the 11th-century, explaining the simple meaning of the text with interpretive elaborations.

Sections: Chapter-Verse-Comment

Location: Tanakh/Rishonim on Tanakh/Rashi/Prophets

Originally from [Sefaria](https://www.sefaria.org) via [TorahLM.org](https://torahlm.org) — see [licensing](https://torahlm.org/sefaria_license).

Version (He): The Metsudah Tanach series, Lakewood, N.J (Segments: 503, License: CC-BY)

Version (En): The Metsudah Tanach series, Lakewood, N.J (Segments: 503)


## Chapter 1


### Verse 2

###### Rashi on II Kings 1:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/1:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/1:2:1)


**בְּעַד הַשְּׂבָכָה.** גראד"ה בְּלַעַ"ז. אוֹמֵר אֲנִי שֶׁרְגִילִים לְאָרְגוֹ שְׁתִי וָעֵרֶב בִּנְסָרִים אֲרֻכִּים וּקְצָרִים שֶׁקּוֹרִין קנטינל"י בְּלַעַ"ז, וְעוֹשִׂין נְקָבִים נְקָבִים כְּעֵין שְׂבָכָה, וְקָרוּי פרדונ"י בְּלַעַ"ז. וַאֲנִי שָׁמַעְתִּי 'שְׂבָכָה' ווי"ץ בְּלַעַ"ז, וּבִלְשׁוֹן אַשְׁכְּנַז שווינדל שטיג כְּמוֹ שֶׁפֵּרַשְׁתִּי וּבְלוּלִּים יַעֲלוּ.


**Through the lattice.** *Gradil*, in Latin. I say that they were accustomed to weave it as a warp and a woof with long and short boards, which is called (*lates*) *cantineli*, in Latin, and it was made full of holes like sort of a net, and it is called *prodne*, in O.F. But I heard that שְׂבָכָה is *wiz*, in O.F., and in German, *schwindel* *steg*, as I explained [in], “and by a winding stairway they went up.”¹

footnotes:
¹ Melochim 2:1


###### Rashi on II Kings 1:2:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/1:2:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/1:2:2)


**וַיָּחַל.** לְשׁוֹן חֹלִי.


**And he became ill.** An expression of sickness.²

footnotes:
² IMelochim 6:8.



### Verse 17

###### Rashi on II Kings 1:17:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/1:17:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/1:17:1)


**וַיִּמְלֹךְ יְהוֹרָם תַּחְתָּיו.** אָחִיו הָיָה.


**And Yehorom reigned in his place.** He was his brother.


###### Rashi on II Kings 1:17:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/1:17:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/1:17:2)


**בִּשְׁנַת שְׁתַּיִם לִיהוֹרָם בֶּן יְהוֹשָׁפָט.** אֶפְשָׁר לוֹמַר כֵּן, וַהֲלֹא מָלַךְ בִּשְׁנַת תְּשַׁע עֶשְׂרֵה לִיהוֹשָׁפָט, שֶׁהֲרֵי אֲחַזְיָה מָלַךְ בִּשְׁבַע עֶשְׂרֵה, וּמָלַךְ שְׁנָתַיִם, וּבְסָמוּךְ נָמֵי כְּתִיב: בִּשְׁנַת שְׁמוֹנֶה עֶשְׂרֵה לִיהוֹשָׁפָט מָלַךְ יְהוֹרָם, וִיהוֹשָׁפָט מָלַךְ עֶשְׂרִים וְחָמֵשׁ שָׁנִים קֹדֶם שֶׁמָּלַךְ יְהוֹרָם בְּנוֹ, אֶלָּא רָאוּי הָיָה יְהוֹשָׁפָט לֵהָרֵג בְּרָמוֹת גִּלְעָד, וּבִשְׂכַר שֶׁזָּעַק, תָּלָה לוֹ שֶׁבַע שָׁנִים, וּמֵאוֹתָהּ שָׁעָה הוּא מוֹנֶה לִבְנוֹ.


**In the second year of Yehorom, son of Yehoshophot.** Is it possible to say this? Did he not reign in the nineteenth year of Yehoshophot? For Achazyah reigned in the seventeenth year [of Yehoshophot] and he reigned for two years,³ and also, below it is written, “In the eighteenth year of Yehoshophot,”⁴ that Yehorom reigned, and Yehoshophot reigned twenty-five years before Yehorom, his son, reigned.⁵ But, Yehoshophot deserved to be killed in Ramos Gilad, but as a reward for [his] crying out, his [death] was suspended for seven years,⁶ and from that time [Scripture] counts [the years] to his son.⁷

footnotes:
³ The root of the word is חלה (=to become ill).
⁴ He did not reign for a full two years. He reigned during two different years, and his total reign might have been less than one year, covering only the last few months of one year and the first few months of the following year.—Ralbag
⁵ Below 3:1.
⁶ According to Radak, the seven years discrepancy is resolved as follows. Yehoshophot gave over the throne to Yehorom during his lifetime because he wanted to establish Yehorom as his successor and to prevent conflict among the brothers. This occurred at the end of Yehoshophot’s reign. The first seven years of Yehorom’s reign were while his father was alive. The second year of Yehorom’s reign corresponds with the year that Yehorom son of Achov became king over Yisroel, as stated below in 3:1.
⁷ Tosefta Maseches Sotah 12:2.



## Chapter 2


### Verse 1

###### Rashi on II Kings 2:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/2:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/2:1:1)


**בַּסְעָרָה.** בְּרוּחַ סְעָרָה.


**In a mighty wind.** In a stormy wind.¹

footnotes:
¹ See Yechezkeil 1:4, where a “רוּחַ סְעָרָה” is part of the vision of the מַעֲשֶׂה מֶרְכָּבָה that is described by Yechezkeil.



### Verse 2

###### Rashi on II Kings 2:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/2:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/2:2:1)


**שֵׁב נָא פֹה.** חָפֵץ הָיָה לִדְחוֹתוֹ מֵחֲמַת עֲנָוָה, שֶׁלֹּא יִרְאֶה בְּהִלָּקְחוֹ.


**Please remain here.** He [Eliyahu] wished to drive him away because of his humility, so that he [Elisha] would not see him being taken away.²

footnotes:
² Alternatively, he did not want Elisha to be frightened by seeing the manner by which he was being taken away.—Ralbag



### Verse 3

###### Rashi on II Kings 2:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/2:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/2:3:1)


**אֶת אֲדֹנֶיךָ.** וְלֹא אֲדוֹנֵנוּ, מְלַמֵּד שֶׁהָיוּ שְׁקוּלִים כְּאֵלִיָּהוּ.


**Your master.** But [Scripture does] not [state,] “our master.” This teaches [us] that they were equal to Eliyahu.³

footnotes:
³ I.e., their level of prophecy was equal to Eliyahu’s. Tosefta Maseches Sotah 12:5.



### Verse 8

###### Rashi on II Kings 2:8:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/2:8:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/2:8:1)


**וַיִּגְלֹם.** כְּרָכָהּ, כְּדֵי שֶׁתְּהֵא נוֹחָה לְהַכּוֹת בָּהּ.


**And [he] rolled it up.** He rolled it up in order that it would be easy to strike with it.


###### Rashi on II Kings 2:8:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/2:8:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/2:8:2)


**וַיִּגְלֹם.** וְלֹא כְּרִיכָה מַמָּשׁ, אֶלָּא קוֹלֵט, שֶׁקּוֹרִין פייגד"ו בְּלַעַ"ז.


**And [he] rolled it up.** Not exactly rolling, but gathering together,⁴ which is called *cueilir*, in O.F.

footnotes:
⁴ I.e., he did not fold it neatly on its creases, but he bunched it up together.



### Verse 10

###### Rashi on II Kings 2:10:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/2:10:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/2:10:1)


**הִקְשִׁיתָ לִשְׁאוֹל.** אִי אֶפְשָׁר לָתֵת לְךָ יוֹתֵר מִמַּה שֶּׁיֵּשׁ לִי בְּיָדִי.


**You have made a difficult request.** It is impossible [for me] to give you more than I possess.⁵

footnotes:
⁵ According to Rashi, Elisha apparently asked for a double portion of Eliyahu’s spirit. Alternatively, Elisha requested to attain two thirds of Eliyahu’s spirit and for all other prophets together to attain one third. -Radak Or, Elisha requested double the spirit of any other prophet.—Ralbag


###### Rashi on II Kings 2:10:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/2:10:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/2:10:2)


**אִם תִּרְאֶה אֹתִי לֻקַּח מֵאִתָּךְ.** אָז אוּכַל לַעֲשׂוֹת לְךָ יוֹתֵר וְיוֹתֵר.


**If you will witness my departure from you.** Then I will be able to do more and more.⁶

footnotes:
⁶ I.e., if you are spiritually elevated enough to observe my ascent, it would be an indication that your request would be granted.


###### Rashi on II Kings 2:10:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/2:10:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/2:10:3)


**יְהִי לְךָ כֵן וְאִם אַיִן לֹא יִהְיֶה.** שֶׁאֵין הַיְכֹלֶת בְּיָדִי.


**It will be so for you, and if not, it will not be.** That I am unable [to do so].



### Verse 12

###### Rashi on II Kings 2:12:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/2:12:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/2:12:1)


**אָבִי אָבִי רֶכֶב יִשְׂרָאֵל.** רַבִּי רַבִּי דְּטָב לְהוֹן לְיִשְׂרָאֵל בִּצְלוֹתֵהּ מֵרְתִיכִין וּפָרָשִׁין.


**My father, my father! Chariot of Yisroel.** My endeared teacher⁷ who is better for Yisroel with his prayers than chariots and horsemen.⁸

footnotes:
⁷ He referred to Eliyahu as “father,” because one’s disciples are considered like one’s own children, and conversely, the teacher is considered a father.—Ralbag See Rashi in Devarim 6:7.
⁸ I.e., his prayers are more effective for Yisroel than chariots and horsemen.—Targum



### Verse 14

###### Rashi on II Kings 2:14:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/2:14:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/2:14:1)


**אַיֵּה ה' אֱלֹהֵי אֵלִיָּהוּ.** שֶׁהֻבְטַחְתִּי שֶׁתִּשְׁרֶה בִי פִּי שְׁנַיִם בְּרוּחוֹ אֵלַי.


**Where is Adonoy, the God of Eliyahu?** That I was promised that a double portion of his spirit would rest upon me.⁹

footnotes:
⁹ Alternatively, where is God who does not show me His sign as He showed Eliyahu is the splitting of the water?”—Ralbag


###### Rashi on II Kings 2:14:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/2:14:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/2:14:2)


**אַף הוּא וַיַּכֶּה אֶת הַמַּיִם.** אַף הוּא, בְּפִרְקֵי רַבִּי אֱלִיעֶזֶר, בְּנוֹ שֶל רַבִּי יוֹסֵי הַגְּלִילִי: גְּדוֹלָה חֲצִיַּת אֱלִישָׁע כִּפְלַיִם כְּשֶׁל אֵלִיָּהוּ, שֶׁבִּתְחִלָּה זְכוּת שְׁנֵיהֶם, וְעַכְשָׁיו זְכוּתוֹ יְחִידִי.


**Then he also struck the water.** He too. In the Chapters of Rabbi Eliezer, son of Rabbi Yose Haglili, [it states that] Elisha’s splitting [the Yardein] was twice as great as Eliyahu’s, for in the beginning there was the merit of them both, and now there was his merit alone.¹⁰

footnotes:
¹⁰ See Rashi 3:1 below. Thus, Elisha’s request for twice Eliyahu’s spirit had been fulfilled.



### Verse 16

###### Rashi on II Kings 2:16:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/2:16:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/2:16:1)


**פֶּן נְשָׂאוֹ רוּחַ ה'.** אֶפְשָׁר אֶמֶשׁ אָמְרוּ לוֹ: הֲיָדַעְתָּ כִּי הַיּוֹם ה' לוֹקֵחַ אֶת אֲדוֹנֶיךָ, וְעַכְשָׁיו נֶעְלָם מֵהֶם הֵיכָן הוּא, מְלַמֵּד, מִיּוֹם שֶׁנִּגְנַז אֵלִיָּהוּ הָלְכָה וְנִסְתַּלְּקָה רוּחַ הַקֹּדֶשׁ מִן הַנְּבִיאִים, וְשׁוּב לֹא הָיְתָה רוּחַ הַקֹּדֶשׁ מְרֻבָּה בְּיִשְׂרָאֵל.


**Perhaps the wind of Adonoy carried him up.** Is it possible that yesterday they said to him, “Do you know that today Adonoy is taking your master?”¹¹ and now they did not know where he was?¹² This teaches [us] that since the day when Eliyahu was hidden,¹³ the Holy Spirit departed from the prophets, and the Holy Spirit was no longer widespread throughout Yisroel.¹⁴

footnotes:
¹¹ Above, v. 5.
¹² They thought Eliyahu’s spirit returned to God, but his body was cast to the ground, and they sought to find it and bury it.—Metzudas Dovid
¹³ Some say that Eliyahu’s body remained whole and unharmed. He was carried to גַּן עֵדֶן where he remains hidden from mankind except when he is sent on special missions or to appear to the righteous. Radak Alternatively, they did not know that he would be removed from the world. Rather, they thought that God would take Eliyahu away to some remote location for a period of time. This was not an unusual occurrence. See I Melochim 18:12.
¹⁴ See Tosefta Maseches Sotah 12:5.



### Verse 17

###### Rashi on II Kings 2:17:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/2:17:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/2:17:1)


**עַד בּשׁ.** נִתְבַּיֵּשׁ מֵהֶם, שֶׁלֹּא יֹאמְרוּ אֵינוֹ רוֹצֶה לְהַקְבִּיל פְּנֵי רַבּוֹ, לְפִי שֶׁנָּטַל גְּדֻלָּתוֹ אֵינוֹ רוֹצֶה שֶׁיָּשׁוּב, כָּל זֶה רָאִיתִי בַּתּוֹסֶפְתָּא דְּסוֹטָה.


**Until he was ashamed.** He was ashamed¹⁵ [on account] of them, lest they say that he does not want to go to meet his teacher.¹⁶ Because he took over his high position, he does not want him to return. All this I saw in Tosefta of [Maseches] Sotah.¹⁷

footnotes:
¹⁵ I.e., “to the point of embarrassment.” The root of בש is בוש [=shame].
¹⁶ I.e., he was afraid that they would think that he wanted Eliyahu to stay away so that he could keep his newly acquired position of leadership. Alternatively, the root of בש is בשש [=delay], i.e., “he delayed them considerably” from sending out people to search for Eliyahu. -Metzudas Dovid Also, see below 8:11 and Shemos 32:1.
¹⁷ 12:5.



### Verse 19

###### Rashi on II Kings 2:19:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/2:19:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/2:19:1)


**וְהַמַּיִם רָעִים.** וּמֵחֲמַת הַמַּיִם הָאָרֶץ מְשַׁכֶּלֶת אֶת יוֹשְׁבֶיהָ לְקָבְרָם, אִם כֵּן מַהוּ: וּמוֹשַׁב הָעִיר טוֹב, מִכָּאן אָמְרוּ: חֵן מָקוֹם עַל יוֹשְׁבָיו.


**But the water is bad.** And because of the water, the land causes people to die, and send them to their graves. If so, what is the meaning of, “the city is a good place to live”?¹⁸ From here [we learn] that a place’s charm is perceived by its inhabitants.¹⁹

footnotes:
¹⁸ Above, at the beginning of this verse.
¹⁹ Maseches Sotah 47a. Rabbi Yochonon said that there are three types of charm. 1. The charm a place that is perceived by its inhabitants. 2. The charm a woman holds for her husband. 3. The charm of a newly purchased item for its buyer.



### Verse 21

###### Rashi on II Kings 2:21:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/2:21:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/2:21:1)


**אֶל מוֹצָא הַמַּיִם.** שֶׁהָיוּ נוֹבְעִים מִשָּׁם.


**To the source of the water.** From where the water flowed.


###### Rashi on II Kings 2:21:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/2:21:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/2:21:2)


**וַיַּשְׁלֶךְ שָׁם מֶלַח.** וַהֲלֹא מֶלַח דָּבָר הַמְקַלְקֵל אֶת הַמַּיִם הוּא, אֶלָּא נֵס בְּתוֹךְ נֵס.


**And threw salt there.** Now is not salt a thing that spoils water? This was a miracle within a miracle.²⁰

footnotes:
²⁰ This miracle was similar to the one performed by Moshe in Marah. After traveling through the wilderness for three days without water, the Bnei Yisroel finally came to a spring only to find the water undrinkably bitter. Moshe threw a tree whose wood was bitter into the water and the water became sweet and drinkable. See Shemos 15:22-26.—Radak



### Verse 23

###### Rashi on II Kings 2:23:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/2:23:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/2:23:1)


**וּנְעָרִים קְטַנִּים.** הַמְנֹעָרִין מִן הַמִּצְוֹת.


**Little boys.** Void of *mitzvos*.²¹

footnotes:
²¹ The literal meaning of נְעָרִים קְטַנִּים is “small children,” but obviously this is not the true meaning, because Elisha would never have cursed small children. The word נְעָרִים can also be interpreted as נְעוּרִים [=shaken out or empty] from good deeds. The word קטנים [=little] alludes to how small their faith was. Nonetheless, Elisha was punished for treating them so harshly. See Maseches Sotah 47a. Radak Alternatively, the boys came from a town called נַעֲרָן, which is mentioned in I Divrei Hayomim 7:28 and in Yehoshua 16:7. See below, 5:2.


###### Rashi on II Kings 2:23:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/2:23:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/2:23:2)


**עֲלֵה קֵרֵחַ.** עֲלֵה מִכָּאן, שֶׁהִקְרַחְתָּ עָלֵינוּ אֶת הַמָּקוֹם, שֶׁעַד עַכְשָׁיו הָיִינוּ מִשְׂתַּכְּרִין לְהָבִיא מַיִם מְתוּקִים מֵרָחוֹק, וּמִתְפַּרְנְסִין בְּכָךְ, וּמִשֶּׁהִמְתִּיקוּ אִבְּדוּ פַּרְנָסָתָן, כָּךְ מְפֹרָשׁ בְּסוֹטָה.


**“Go away, baldy!”** “Go away from here, for you have made the place bald for us, [i.e.,] for until now we would be hired to bring sweet water from a distance, and we would earn our livelihood thereby.” But when [the water] became sweet, they lost their livelihood. Thus it is explained in [Maseches] Sotah.²²

footnotes:
²² 46b, and see Rashi there.



### Verse 24

###### Rashi on II Kings 2:24:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/2:24:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/2:24:1)


**וַיִּרְאֵם.** רָאָה שֶׁאֵין בָּהֶם וְלֹא בְּזַרְעָם לַחְלוּחִית שֶל טוֹבָה.


**And saw them.** He saw that neither in them nor in their descendants would there be any sap of good deed.²³²⁴

footnotes:
²³ See Shemos 2:12 and Rashi there.
²⁴ Some say that Elisha saw that their hair was cut in the style of the heathens. He believed that they were degenerate and depraved and foresaw that they would never return to the ways of the Torah. See Maseches Sotah 46b.



## Chapter 3


### Verse 1

###### Rashi on II Kings 3:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/3:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/3:1:1)


**וִיהוֹרָם בֶּן אַחְאָב.** אַף פָּרָשָׁה זוֹ, בִּשְׁבִיל לְהַגִּיד נֵס שֶל מֵישָׁע מֶלֶךְ מוֹאָב שֶׁנַּעֲשָׂה עַל יְדֵי אֱלִישָׁע נִכְתְּבָה כָּאן, שֶׁמָּנָה בְּאֵלִיָּהוּ שְׁמוֹנָה נִסִּים, וּבֶאֱלִישָׁע שִׁשָּׁה עָשָׂר, לְקַיֵּם: פִּי שְׁנַיִם בְּרוּחֲךָ אֵלַי, וּכְתָבָן כֻּלָּם זוֹ בְּצַד זוֹ, כָּךְ רָאִיתִי בִּשְׁלשִׁים וּשְׁתַּיִם מִדּוֹת דְּרַבִּי אֱלִיעֶזֶר בְּנוֹ דְּרַבִּי יוֹסֵי הַגְּלִילִי, וַחֲצִיַּת יַרְדֵּן שֶׁל אֱלִישָׁע, כִּשְׁתַּיִם דְּשֶׁל אֵלִיָּהוּ.


**Yehorom, son of Achov.** This section too [in order] to tell the miracle of Meisha the king of Moav, which was performed through Elisha, was written here, for [Scripture] enumerated for Eliyahu eight miracles and for Elisha sixteen, to fulfill “a double amount of your spirit upon me,”¹ and he wrote them all, one juxtaposed to the other. I saw this in the “Thirty-two Methods of Rabbi Eliezer, son of Rabbi Yose Haglili.” And the dividing of the Yardein by Elisha, is [counted] like two of Eliyahu’s miracles.²

footnotes:
¹ Above, 2:9.
² See above 2:14 and Rashi there.



### Verse 2

###### Rashi on II Kings 3:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/3:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/3:2:1)


**רַק לֹא כְאָבִיו וּכְאִמּוֹ.** שֶׁהוֹסִיפוּ אֶת הַבַּעַל, לְהַכְעִיס.


**But not [as bad] as his father and mother.** Who added the [worship of the ] Baal in order to provoke [God].³

footnotes:
³ Yehorom abolished worship of the Baal but not the Asheiroh because is mother, Izevel, was still alive at the time and she supported Asheiroh worship.—Ralbag



### Verse 3

###### Rashi on II Kings 3:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/3:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/3:3:1)


**רַק בְּחַטֹּאות יָרָבְעָם וְגוֹ' דָּבֵק.** וְכֵן כָּל מַלְכֵי יִשְׂרָאֵל, מִדְּאָגָה שֶׁלֹּא תָשׁוּב הַמַּמְלָכָה לְבֵית דָּוִד אִם יַעֲלוּ לָרֶגֶל, לְכָךְ עָבְדוּ אֶת הָעֲגָלִים.


**However, to the sins of Yorovom, etc. he cling.** And so did all kings of Yisroel, out of fear, so that the kingdom does not return to the House of Dovid if they were to perform the pilgrimage on the [Three] Festival[s]. They, therefore, worshiped the calves.



### Verse 4

###### Rashi on II Kings 3:4:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/3:4:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/3:4:1)


**נֹקֵד.** מָרֵי גֵיתִין.


**Owned many flocks.** [Targum Yonoson renders,] “owner of livestock.”⁴

footnotes:
⁴ Sheep are usually spotted [= נְקוּדִים], therefore a sheep owner is called נַקָּד.—Radak


###### Rashi on II Kings 3:4:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/3:4:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/3:4:2)


**אֵילִים צָמֶר.** דִּיכְרִין דְּרָעֲיָא, עִם צִמְרָם.


**Wooly rams.** [Targum Yonoson renders,] “pasture-fed rams,” with their wool.



### Verse 8

###### Rashi on II Kings 3:8:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/3:8:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/3:8:1)


**דֶּרֶךְ מִדְבַּר אֱדוֹם.** כִּי גַּם מֶלֶךְ אֱדוֹם יֵלֵךְ עִמָּנוּ.


**Through the road of the wilderness of Edom.** For the king of Edom,⁵ too, will go with us.

footnotes:
⁵ He was not a king, but actually a governor that was appointed by the king of Yehudah.—Metzudas Dovid



### Verse 10

###### Rashi on II Kings 3:10:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/3:10:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/3:10:1)


**לָתֵת אוֹתָם וְגוֹ'.** שֶׁיָּמוּתוּ בַּצָּמָא.


**To hand them over, etc.** For they will die of thirst.⁶

footnotes:
⁶ The three kings will be handed over to Moav because either they will die of thirst, or if they scatter across the countryside in search for water, they will be in an unprotected and vulnerable position.—Radak



### Verse 11

###### Rashi on II Kings 3:11:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/3:11:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/3:11:1)


**אֲשֶׁר יָצַק מַיִם עַל יְדֵי אֵלִיָּהוּ.** בְּהַר הַכַּרְמֶל, וּבִיצִיקָתוֹ נַעֲשׂוּ אֶצְבְּעוֹתָיו כְּמַעְיְנוֹת מַיִם, עַד שֶׁנִּתְמַלְּאָה הַתְּעָלָה, וְרָאוּי הוּא לֵעָשׂוֹת לָנוּ נֵס עַל יָדוֹ בְּמַיִם.


**Who poured water on the hands of Eliyahu.** On Mount Carmel,⁷ and with his pouring, his fingers became like springs of water, until the ditch filled up, and it is [therefore] fitting for a miracle to be performed for us through him concerning water.⁸⁹

footnotes:
⁷ See I Melochim 18:34 and Rashi there.
⁸ See Yalkot Shimoni 247:214.
⁹ Alternatively, this is an expression to indicate Elisha’s total devotion to Eliyahu to the extent that he even poured water on Eliyahu’s hands. From here we derive that those who serve Torah scholars are greater than those who only study the Torah. See Maseches Berachos 7b.



### Verse 13

###### Rashi on II Kings 3:13:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/3:13:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/3:13:1)


**אַל כִּי קָרָא ה' וְגוֹ'.** בְּבָעוּ לָא תִּדְכַּר חוֹבִין רִשְׁעָתָא הַהִיא, בָּעֵי עֲלָנָא רַחֲמִין, אֲרֵי זַמִּין ה' וְכוּ'.


**Do not [say that], for Adonoy has called, etc.** [Targum Yonoson renders,] “Please [אל]¹⁰ do not mention the sins of that wicked woman. Beg mercy for us, for Adonoy has summoned, etc.”

footnotes:
¹⁰ Alternatively, Gא means “do not,” i.e., “do not tell us these things now, etc.”—Radak



### Verse 15

###### Rashi on II Kings 3:15:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/3:15:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/3:15:1)


**קְחוּ לִי מְנַגֵּן.** מִפְּנֵי הַכַּעַס נִסְתַּלְּקָה שְׁכִינָה מִמֶּנּוּ.


**Bring me a musician.** Because of his anger, the Divine Presence departed from him.¹¹. See Maseches Pesachim 117a.

footnotes:
¹¹ See Maseches Pesachim 66b. Alternatively, Elisha was unable to prophesy because he was mourning Eliyahu’s departure. The Divine Presence does not reside with people who are sad. It rests on prophets only when they experience joy in their fulfillment of a mitzvah



### Verse 16

###### Rashi on II Kings 3:16:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/3:16:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/3:16:1)


**גֵּבִים גֵּבִים.** בּוֹרוֹת מְלֵאִים מַיִם.


**Many pools.** Ditches full of water.



### Verse 18

###### Rashi on II Kings 3:18:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/3:18:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/3:18:1)


**וְנָקַל זֹאת.** וְעוֹד נֵס זֶה נָקֵל וּמוּעָט בְּעֵינֵי ה', וְהוֹסִיף לְהַפְלִיא אֶתְכֶם נֵס אַחֵר, וְנָתַן אֶת מוֹאָב בְּיֶדְכֶם.


**This is easy.** And furthermore, this miracle is slight and insignificant in the eyes of God, and He will proceed to act wondrously with you and [perform] another miracle, and He will deliver Moav into your hand.



### Verse 19

###### Rashi on II Kings 3:19:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/3:19:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/3:19:1)


**וְכָל עֵץ טוֹב תַּפִּילוּ.** אַף עַל פִּי שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר: לֹא תַשְׁחִית אֶת עֵצָהּ, כָּאן הִתִּיר לָכֶם, שֶׁהִיא אֻמָּה בְּזוּיָה וְנִקְלֵית לְפָנָיו, וְכֵן הוּא אוֹמֵר: לֹא תִדְרשׁ שְׁלוֹמָם וְטוֹבָתָם, אֵלּוּ הָאִילָנוֹת הַטּוֹבִים שֶׁבָּהֶם.


**And every valuable tree you will chop down.** Even though it is stated, “Do not harm any of its trees,”¹² here He permitted it for you, for this is a contemptible and insignificant nation before Him. And so Scripture states, “You shall not pursue their peace and their benefit,”¹³ [i.e.,] these are the good trees that are among them.

footnotes:
¹² Devarim 20:19.
¹³ Devarim 23:7.


###### Rashi on II Kings 3:19:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/3:19:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/3:19:2)


**הַחֶלְקָה הַטּוֹבָה.** שָׂדֶה זְרוּעָה, קומצ"י בְּלַעַ"ז.


**Fertile field.** Sown field, *koumici*, in O.F.



### Verse 20

###### Rashi on II Kings 3:20:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/3:20:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/3:20:1)


**וַיְהִי בַבֹּקֶר** כַּעֲלוֹת הַמִּנְחָה. הַמַּיִם הִתְחִילוּ לֵירֵד מִן הַשָּׁמַיִם בַּבֹּקֶר בְּאֶרֶץ אֱדוֹם, וְכַעֲלוֹת הַמִּנְחָה בָּאִין שׁוֹטְפִין וּבָאִין לְאוֹתוֹ הַנַּחַל, לְפִי שֶׁאָמַר: לֹא תִרְאוּ רוּחַ וְלֹא תִרְאוּ גֶשֶׁם, בְּמִדְרַשׁ רַבִּי תַּנְחוּמָא.


**At the time of the morning** when the meal-offering was brought up. The water began falling from heaven in the morning¹⁴ in the land of Edom,¹⁵ and at the [time of] offering up the meal-offering, it came surging into that valley, because Elisha had said, “You will not see wind and you will not see rain.”¹⁶ [This is stated] in the Midrash of Rabbi Tanchuma.

footnotes:
¹⁴ Rashi is explaining the apparent contradiction between the expressions “morning” and “when the meal-offering was offered up” which refers to the afternoon services. Rashi explains that two expressions refer to the beginning and end of the miracle.
¹⁵ Alternatively, God caused one of the rivers in Edom to overflow its banks causing the valley near the camp to fill with water.—Radak
¹⁶ Above v. 17.



### Verse 21

###### Rashi on II Kings 3:21:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/3:21:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/3:21:1)


**עַל הַגְּבוּל.** עַל הַסְּפָר, שֶׁקּוֹרִין מרק"א.


**At the border.** At the border, that is called *marche*, in O.F.



### Verse 22

###### Rashi on II Kings 3:22:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/3:22:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/3:22:1)


**אֲדֻמִּים כַּדָּם.** כֵּן דֶּרֶךְ הַמַּיִם, כְּשֶׁהַשֶּׁמֶשׁ זוֹרַחַת עֲלֵיהֶם בַּבֹּקֶר, מַאֲדִימוֹת.


**Red as blood.** Such is the nature of water; when the sun shines on it in the morning, causes it to appear red.


###### Rashi on II Kings 3:22:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/3:22:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/3:22:2)


**מִנֶּגֶד.** מֵרָחוֹק, וְהֵם לֹא רָאוּ בְּאוֹתוֹ הַנַּחַל מַיִם מֵעוֹלָם, לְפִיכָךְ הָיוּ סְבוּרִין שֶׁהוּא דָם.


**From a distance.** At a distance, but because they had never seen water in that valley, they, therefore, thought that it was blood.



### Verse 25

###### Rashi on II Kings 3:25:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/3:25:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/3:25:1)


**וְהֶעָרִים יַהֲרֹסוּ.** הָיוּ הוֹרְסִין.


**They demolished the cities.** They would destroy the cities.


###### Rashi on II Kings 3:25:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/3:25:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/3:25:2)


**יַשְׁלִיכוּ אִישׁ אַבְנוֹ.** שֶׁהָיוּ נוֹטְלִין הָאֲבָנִים מִן הַחוֹמוֹת, עַד הִשְׁאִיר כָּל אַבְנֵי הַקִּירוֹת בַּקִּיר לַעֲמֹד עַל חַרְסִית שֶׁלָּהֶן הוּא הַטִּיט, כְּלוֹמַר שֶׁלֹּא הָיְתָה עוֹד אֶבֶן בַּחוֹמָה, וְאַחֲרֵי זֹאת וַיָּסֹבּוּ הַקַּלָּעִים וַיַּכּוּ אֶת הַנִּשְׁאָר בַּחֶלְקָה, שֶׁלֹּא נִשְׁבְּרָה הַחוֹמָה, וְהִפִּילוּהָ.


**Each man threw his stone.** For they would take stones from the walls until they left all the stones of the walls in the wall, with only their clay,¹⁷ [i.e.,] their mortar; i.e., there were no more stones in the wall. Afterwards, “and the catapulters surrounded it,” and destroyed that which remained in the fields where the wall was not broken, and tore it down.

footnotes:
¹⁷ This is Rashi’s explanation of בַּקִּיר חֲרָשֶׂת [=the clay in the wall]. Alternatively, קִיר חֲרָשֶׂת was the name of a mighty fortified city in Moav. Generally, enemy cities would be destroyed after their capture. But, because קִיר חֲרָשֶׂת was a mighty fortified city, they needed catapulters to destroy its wall in order to capture it.—Radak



### Verse 27

###### Rashi on II Kings 3:27:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/3:27:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/3:27:1)


**וַיִּקַּח אֶת בְּנוֹ הַבְּכוֹר.** בִּפְסִיקְתָּא דְּפָרָשַׁת שְׁקָלִים נִדְרָשׁ: שֶׁשָּׁאַל אֶת עֲבָדָיו מַה טִּיבָהּ שֶל אֻמָּה זוֹ שֶׁנַּעֲשָׂה לָהֶם נִסִּים כָּאֵלֶּה, אָמְרוּ לוֹ: אֲבִיהֶם אַבְרָהָם בֵּן יָחִיד הָיָה לוֹ, אָמַר לוֹ הַקָּבָּ"ה הַקְרִיבֵהוּ לְפָנַי, וְרָצָה לְהַקְרִיבוֹ לְהַקָּבָּ"ה, אָמַר לָהֶם: אַף אֲנִי בֵּן בְּכוֹר יֵשׁ לִי, אֵלֵךְ וְאַקְרִיבֶנּוּ לַעֲבוֹדָה זָרָה.


**And then took his firstborn son.** In the Pesikta of the section of Shekalim it is expounded: He asked his servants, “What is the character of this nation, that miracles such as these were performed for them?” They replied, “Their forefather, Avrohom, had an only son. The Holy One, Blessed Is He, said to him, ‘Offer him before me,’ and he wanted to sacrifice him to the Holy One, Blessed Is He.” He said to them, “I too have a firstborn son. I will go and sacrifice him to the gods.”¹⁸

footnotes:
¹⁸ There are different opinions whose firstborn son was offered as a burnt offering. Some say that the king of Edom’s firstborn son was in the custody of Moav’s king, and he was burnt by the king of Moav. Others maintain that Meisha, the king of Moav, sacrificed his own son. In Maseches Sanhedrin 39b there are also different opinions whether the firstborn was offered to God or to a heathen deity.—Radak


###### Rashi on II Kings 3:27:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/3:27:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/3:27:2)


**עַל הַחֹמָה.** חָסֵר וָא"ו, שֶׁהָיָה עוֹבֵד לַחַמָּה.


**On the wall.** The ‘*vav*’ [in חֹמָה] is missing,¹⁹ for he worshiped the sun [=חַמָּה].

footnotes:
¹⁹ I.e., it should have been spelled חוֹמָה.


###### Rashi on II Kings 3:27:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/3:27:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/3:27:3)


**וַיְהִי קֶצֶף גָּדוֹל.** שֶׁנִּזְכְּרוּ עֲוֹנוֹתֵיהֶם, שֶׁאַף הֵם עוֹבְדִין לַעֲבוֹדָה זָרָה, וְאֵינָן רְאוּיִין לְנֵס.


**This caused a terrible anger.** For their iniquities were remembered, that they too worship pagan deities and are not worthy of a miracle.²⁰

footnotes:
²⁰ See Maseches Sanhedrin 39b.



## Chapter 4


### Verse 1

###### Rashi on II Kings 4:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/4:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/4:1:1)


**מִנְּשֵׁי בְנֵי הַנְּבִיאִים.** אֵשֶׁת עוֹבַדְיָה הָיְתָה. כָּל 'בְּנֵי הַנְּבִיאִים' שֶׁבַּמִּקְרָא, תַּרְגּוּמוֹ: תַּלְמִידֵי נְבִיַּיָּא.


**A wife of one of the disciples of the prophets.** She was the wife of Ovadyoh. Every expression of ‘בְּנֵי הַנְּבִיאִים’ [lit., “the sons of the prophets”], in Scripture, is translated [by Targum Yonoson] as, “the disciples of the prophets.”¹

footnotes:
¹ See Targum above 2:3, 2:7 and 2:15.


###### Rashi on II Kings 4:1:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/4:1:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/4:1:2)


**וְהַנּשֶׁה.** הוּא יְהוֹרָם בֶּן אַחְאָב, שֶׁהָיָה מַלְוֵהוּ בְּרִבִּית מַה שֶּׁזָּן אֶת הַנְּבִיאִים בִּימֵי אָבִיו, בְּמִדְרַשׁ רַבִּי תַּנְחוּמָא. לְכָךְ נֶאֱמַר: וַיַּךְ אֶת יְהוֹרָם בֵּין זְרוֹעָיו, שֶׁפָּשְׁטוּ לִטֹּל רִבִּית.


**Now the creditor.** He was Yehorom, son of Achov, who would lend him with interest, enabling him to sustain the prophets in his father’s time [according to] Midrash Rabbi Tanchuma. Therefore it is stated, “And struck Yehorom between his arms,”² which he stretched out to take interest.

footnotes:
² Below 9:24.



### Verse 2

###### Rashi on II Kings 4:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/4:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/4:2:1)


**אָסוּךְ שָׁמֶן.** כְּדֵי סִיכַת שֶׁמֶן, וְהָאָלֶ"ף בַּתֵּבָה מִן הַיְּסוֹד, כְּמוֹ אָלֶ"ף שֶל וְאַחְוָתִי בְּאָזְנֵיכֶם, וְהָאָלֶ"ף שֶׁל אִבְחַת חָרֶב.


**A jar of oil.** Enough oil for anointment [=אָסוּךְ], and the ‘א’ is part of the root,³ like the ‘א’ of, “and my speech [=וְאַחְוָתִי] in your ears,”⁴ and the ‘א’ of, “the dread [=אִבְחַת] of those struck down by the sword.”⁵

footnotes:
³ Alternatively, the root is סוך, and the ‘א’ is an added prefix.—Radak
⁴ Iyov 13:17.
⁵ Yechezkeil 21:20.



### Verse 4

###### Rashi on II Kings 4:4:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/4:4:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/4:4:1)


**וְסָגַרְתְּ הַדֶּלֶת.** כְּבוֹד הַנֵּס הוּא לָבֹא בְּהֶצְנֵעַ.


**And close the door.** [It affords] respect to the miracle [if] it comes about in secret.


###### Rashi on II Kings 4:4:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/4:4:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/4:4:2)


**וְהַמָּלֵא תַּסִּיעִי.** מִלְּפָנַיִךְ, וְתִתְּנִי כְּלִי אַחֵר בִּמְקוֹמוֹ לְמַלּאוֹתוֹ, וּצְלוֹחִית הַשֶּׁמֶן לֹא תָזִיזִי מִמְּקוֹמוֹ, לְפִי שֶׁהַקָּבָּ"ה עוֹשֵׂהוּ כְּמַעְיָן, וְאֵין דֶּרֶךְ מַעְיָן לְזוּזוֹ מִמְּקוֹמוֹ, מִדְרַשׁ אַגָּדָה שָׁמַעְתִּי.


**And the full one[s] you shall remove.** From before you, and you shall place another vessel in its place to fill it, but you must not move the jug of oil from its place, for the Holy One, Blessed Is He, is making it as a spring, and it is not customary to move a spring from its place.⁶ I heard this Midrash Aggadah.

footnotes:
⁶ Alternatively, she must not move the jug of oil because a miracle continues only in the place where it started.—Radak



### Verse 5

###### Rashi on II Kings 4:5:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/4:5:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/4:5:1)


**הֵם מַגִּישִׁים אֵלֶיהָ.** הַכֵּלִים.


**They would bring [the containers] to her.** The vessels.⁷

footnotes:
⁷ They even brought her broken vessels and miraculously they became whole. These too were filled with oil.—Radak



### Verse 6

###### Rashi on II Kings 4:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/4:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/4:6:1)


**וַיַּעֲמֹד הַשָּׁמֶן.** מִלָּבֹא עוֹד, וּמִדְרַשׁ אַגָּדָה: הוֹקִיר שַׁעַר הַשֶּׁמֶן.


**Then the oil stopped.** From coming anymore. [According to] Midrash Aggadah, the price of oil went up.⁸

footnotes:
⁸ Bereishis Rabbah 35:3.



### Verse 7

###### Rashi on II Kings 4:7:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/4:7:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/4:7:1)


**וַתָּבֹא וַתַּגֵּד וְגוֹ'.** בָּאת לִטֹּל עֵצָה, אִם לִמְכֹּר אִם לְהַמְתִּין עַד שֶׁיּוּקַר עוֹד, אָמַר לָהּ לְכִי מִכְרִי, כִּי יֵשׁ דַּי לְכָל נִשְׁיֵךְ, וְלִחְיוֹת אַתְּ וּבָנַיִךְ בַּנּוֹתָר, עַד שֶׁיִּחְיוּ הַמֵּתִים.


**She came and told, etc.** She came for advice, whether to sell [the oil] or to wait until it would become even more expensive. He said to her, “Go sell, for there is enough [to pay] all your debt, and for you and your sons to live with the remainder, until the dead are resurrected.”⁹

footnotes:
⁹ He blessed them with eternal life, i.e., life until the Resurrection of the Dead.—Malbim



### Verse 8

###### Rashi on II Kings 4:8:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/4:8:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/4:8:1)


**וְשָׁם אִשָּׁה גְדוֹלָה.** חֲשׁוּבָה, וְרָאִיתִי בְּפִרְקֵי דְרַבִּי אֱלִיעֶזֶר: אֲחוֹתָהּ שֶל אֲבִישַׁג הַשּׁוּנַמִּית הָיְתָה.


**There [lived] a prominent woman.** [I.e.,] an important [woman]. And I saw in Pirkei of Rabbi Eliezer,¹⁰ that she was the sister of Avishag the Shunamis.¹¹¹²

footnotes:
¹⁰ Chapter 23.
¹¹ See I Melochim 1:1-4.
¹² She is also identified as the mother of Iddo the prophet.—Radak See I Melochim 13:1 and Rashi there.


###### Rashi on II Kings 4:8:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/4:8:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/4:8:2)


**וַיְהִי מִדֵּי עָבְרוֹ.** בְּאוֹתָהּ הָעִיר, יָסוּר אֶל בֵּיתָהּ לֶאֱכֹל לָחֶם.


**Thereafter, whenever he passed through.** In that city, he would stop at her house to eat a meal.


###### Rashi on II Kings 4:8:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/4:8:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/4:8:3)


**יָסֻר.** לְשׁוֹן הוֶֹה, הָיָה סָר שָׁם.


**He would go.** [יור is] the present tense, [i.e.,] he would go there.



### Verse 9

###### Rashi on II Kings 4:9:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/4:9:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/4:9:1)


**הִנֵּה נָא יָדַעְתִּי.** שֶׁלֹּא רָאֲתָה זְבוּב עַל שֻׁלְחָנוֹ, וּקֶרִי עַל סְדִינוֹ.


**“Behold, now I know.”** Because she never saw a fly at his table or semen on his sheet.¹³

footnotes:
¹³ Maseches Berachos 10b. From here we derive that a woman recognizes her guests better than her husband does.



### Verse 11

###### Rashi on II Kings 4:11:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/4:11:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/4:11:1)


**וַיְהִי הַיּוֹם.** וַיְהִי יוֹם אֶחָד.


**And the day came.** And it was one day.



### Verse 13

###### Rashi on II Kings 4:13:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/4:13:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/4:13:1)


**חָרַדְתְּ אֵלֵינוּ.** בִּשְׁבִילֵנוּ.


**You have busied yourself with us.** On our account.


###### Rashi on II Kings 4:13:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/4:13:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/4:13:2)


**אֶת כָּל הַחֲרָדָה הַזֹּאת.** לָשׂוּם אֶל לִבֵּךְ אֶת הָעֵסֶק הַזֶּה, כְּמוֹ: וְחָרֵד עַל דְּבָרִי, זָהִיר עַל הַדָּבָר שֶׁיְּהֵא עָשׂוּי, וְנוֹתְנוֹ אֶל לִבּוֹ.


**With all this attention.** To put your heart into this task, as in, “and shudders [=וחרד] concerning My word,”¹⁴ [i.e.,] is diligent concerning the task that it be accomplished, and puts his heart into it.

footnotes:
¹⁴ Yeshayahu 66:2.


###### Rashi on II Kings 4:13:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/4:13:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/4:13:3)


**מֶה לַעֲשׂוֹת לָךְ.** מָה אַתְּ צְרִיכָה שֶׁנַּעֲשֶׂה לָךְ, שֶׁבִּשְׁבִילֵנוּ עָסַקְתְּ כָּל זֶה.


**What can be done for you?** What do you need that we do for you, for you busied yourself on our account.


###### Rashi on II Kings 4:13:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/4:13:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/4:13:4)


**בְּתוֹךְ עַמִּי.** בְּתוֹךְ קְרוֹבַי, אֵין אָדָם מַזִּיקֵנִי, אֵינִי צְרִיכָה לְמֶלֶךְ וְלֹא לְשַׂר הַצָּבָא.


**“In the midst of my people.”** Among my relatives, [i.e.,] no one harms me. I have no need for the king or for the general of the army.¹⁵

footnotes:
¹⁵ Alternatively, “I dwell among my relatives and they can speak to the king on my behalf if necessary.”—Radak



### Verse 14

###### Rashi on II Kings 4:14:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/4:14:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/4:14:1)


**וַיֹּאמֶר אֱלִישָׁע.** לְגֵיחֲזִי.


**And he said.** Elisha [said] to Geichazi.


###### Rashi on II Kings 4:14:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/4:14:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/4:14:2)


**וּמֶה לַעֲשׂוֹת לָהּ.** נֶגֶד הַטּוֹבָה הַזֹּאת.


**“Then what is there to do for her?”** To reciprocate for this favor.



### Verse 16

###### Rashi on II Kings 4:16:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/4:16:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/4:16:1)


**לַמּוֹעֵד הַזֶּה** כָּעֵת חַיָּה. כְּמוֹ שֶׁאַתְּ קַיֶּמֶת הַיּוֹם וּלְשָׁלוֹם, כָּךְ תְּהִי קַיֶּמֶת לַמּוֹעֵד הַזֶּה, וְחוֹבֶקֶת בֵּן.


**“At this season [next year]** when the time comes to give birth.” Just as you are alive and well today, so will you be alive at this season [next year]¹⁶ at this time and embracing a son.

footnotes:
¹⁶ I.e., Elisha was guaranteeing her future health by comparing it to her present state of health. Alternatively, חַיָּה refers to a woman who gives birth and כָּעֵת חַיָּה refers to a nine month span, the normal period of pregnancy. Elisha told her that the baby will be born after fully developing in the womb.—Metzudas Dovid


###### Rashi on II Kings 4:16:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/4:16:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/4:16:2)


**אַל אֲדֹנִי.** אַל תֹּאמַר חוֹבֶקֶת בֵּן, מַה לִּי חִבּוּקוֹ אִם סוֹפִי לְקָבְרוֹ, וְזֶה שֶׁאָמְרָה לּוֹ כְּשֶׁמֵּת הֲלֹא אָמַרְתִּי לֹא תַשְׁלֶה אוֹתִי.


**“Do not my master.”** Do not say, “embracing a son.” Why do I need to embrace him if I will end up burying him?¹⁷ This is what she said to him when he died, “Did I not say, ‘Do not mislead me’?”

footnotes:
¹⁷ She suspected that her son would survive for only a short period because the prophet used the expression “you will be hugging a son” instead of “you will have a son,” the expression used by the angels when they told Avrohom that Sarah will bear him a son. See Bereishis 18:10.—Malbim


###### Rashi on II Kings 4:16:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/4:16:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/4:16:3)


**אַל תְּכַזֵּב.** אַל תַּרְאֵנִי דָּבָר שֶׁיִּפְסֹק, יֵשׁ בְּיָדְךָ לְבַקֵּשׁ רַחֲמִים וְיִנָּתֵן לִי בֵּן, אַךְ בְּבַקָּשָׁה מִמְּךָ אַל תִּתֵּן לִי אֶלָּא בֵּן שֶׁל קַיָּמָא.


**Do not deceive.** Do not show me something that will fail. You have power to beg mercy, so that a son will be given to me. But I beg you, do not give me [any child], rather a child who will live.


###### Rashi on II Kings 4:16:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/4:16:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/4:16:4)


**אַל תְּכַזֵּב.** כְּמוֹ: לֹא יְכַזְּבוּ מֵימָיו.


**Do not deceive.** Like “whose waters will not fail.”¹⁸

footnotes:
¹⁸ Yeshayahu 58:11.



### Verse 17

###### Rashi on II Kings 4:17:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/4:17:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/4:17:1)


**כָּעֵת חַיָּה.** כָּעֵת הַזֹּאת שֶׁהָיְתָה בְחַיִּים וּבְשָׁלוֹם, וּלְכָךְ נָקוּד כָּעֵת.


**When the time was for her to give birth.** Like this time when she was alive and well. Therefore, the word is voweled [to mean], like “this” time.¹⁹

footnotes:
¹⁹ I.e., the ‘כ’ is voweled with a קָמַץ instead of a שְׁוָא.



### Verse 19

###### Rashi on II Kings 4:19:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/4:19:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/4:19:1)


**רֹאשִׁי רֹאשִׁי.** הִנְנִי חוֹלֶה בְּרֹאשִׁי.


**“My head! My head!”** I am sick in my head.²⁰

footnotes:
²⁰ It is common for those in pain to repeat their cries; see Yirmiyahu 4:19.—Radak


###### Rashi on II Kings 4:19:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/4:19:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/4:19:2)


**וַיֹּאמֶר.** אָבִיו אֶל אֶחָד מִן הַנְּעָרִים, שָׂאֵהוּ אֶל אִמּוֹ.


**He said.** His father said to one of the servants, “Carry him to his mother.”



### Verse 24

###### Rashi on II Kings 4:24:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/4:24:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/4:24:1)


**נְהַג וָלֵךְ.** מַהֵר.


**“Drive and go on.”** [I.e.,] hurry.


###### Rashi on II Kings 4:24:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/4:24:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/4:24:2)


**אַל תַּעֲצָר לִי.** אַל תְּעַכֵּב עַל יָדִי אֶת הָרְכִיבָה.


**Do not hold back from riding because of me.** Do not hold back the riding [pace] because of me.²¹

footnotes:
²¹ Some say she walked alongside the donkey and others say she rode the donkey.—Radak



### Verse 26

###### Rashi on II Kings 4:26:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/4:26:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/4:26:1)


**וַתֹּאמֶר שָׁלוֹם.** הֲרֵי זֶה מִקְרָא קָצָר, שֶׁהֲרֵי הָיָה לוֹ לִכְתֹּב, וַיִּשְׁאַל לָהּ וַתֹּאמֶר שָׁלוֹם.


**And she said, “[All is] well.”** This is an abbreviated verse, for it should have been written, “And he asked her, and she said, ‘We are well.’”



### Verse 28

###### Rashi on II Kings 4:28:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/4:28:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/4:28:1)


**הֲלֹא אָמַרְתִּי.** לְךָ, אַל תְּכַזֵּב בְּשִׁפְחָתֶךָ.


**Did I not say.** To you, “Do not deceive your maidservant”?²²

footnotes:
²² Below v. 16.


###### Rashi on II Kings 4:28:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/4:28:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/4:28:2)


**לֹא תַשְׁלֶה.** לֹא תַשְׁגֶּה אוֹתִי עַל דְּבַר טָעוּת.


**“Do not mislead”?** Do not mislead me concerning a mistake.²³

footnotes:
²³ Alternatively, Gא תַשְׁגֶּה means “do not grieve me.”—Targum



### Verse 29

###### Rashi on II Kings 4:29:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/4:29:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/4:29:1)


**לֹא תְבָרְכֶנּוּ.** לֹא תִּשְׁאַל לְשָׁלוֹם, וְכָל זֶה שֶׁלֹּא יַרְבֶּה דְבָרִים וְיִשְׁאָלֵהוּ לְהֵיכָן אַתָּה הוֹלֵךְ, וְהוּא אוֹמֵר לְהַחֲיוֹת אֶת הַמֵּת, וְאֵין זֶה כְּבוֹד הַנֵּס לְהִתְהַלֵּל בּוֹ מִי שֶׁבָּא עַל יָדוֹ. וְהוּא לֹא עָשָׂה כֵּן, אֶלָּא לְכָל הַשּׁוֹאֲלוֹ הוּא אוֹמֵר רַבִּי שְׁלָחַנִי לְהַחֲיוֹת אֶת הַמֵּת.


**Do not greet him.** Do not inquire about his welfare. All this [was done] to discourage conversation, where one might ask him, “Where are you going?” and he would say, “To revive the dead.” It is not respectful for the miracle when the maker brags about it. But, he did not obey.²⁴ When anyone asked him [where he was going], he responded, “My master sent me to revive the dead.”²⁵

footnotes:
²⁴ Geichazi took his mission very lightly and considered it a joke. He was therefore unsuccessful in his mission. Radak
²⁵ Alternatively, Elisha initially thought that the boy had merely fainted and therefore his personal presence would not be necessary.—Malbim



### Verse 34

###### Rashi on II Kings 4:34:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/4:34:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/4:34:1)


**וַיִּגְהַר עָלָיו.** תִּרְגֵּם יוֹנָתָן: וְאַלְהֵי עֲלוֹהִי, הוּא לְשׁוֹן עֲיֵפוּת, יֵשׁ דֻּגְמָתוֹ בְּבָרַיְיתָא דְּהַאֲזִינוּ וּבְסִפְרִי. וּמְנַחֵם פָּתַר: 'וַיִּגְהַר' פִּתְרוֹן הַמִּלָּה כְּפִי עִנְיָנָהּ, נִשְׁתַּטַּח עָלָיו.


**And he spread himself upon him.** [Targum] Yonoson rendered ‘וְאַלְהֵי עֲלוֹהִי’, an expression of weariness.²⁶ There is a similar word in the Beraisa of Ha’azinu and in Sifrei. But Menachem rendered ‘וַיִּגְהַר’, an interpretation of the word according to its context, “he prostrated himself upon him.”²⁷

footnotes:
²⁶ I.e., “he laid exhausted upon him.”
²⁷ See I Melochim 18:42.



### Verse 35

###### Rashi on II Kings 4:35:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/4:35:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/4:35:1)


**וַיְזוֹרֵר.** נִתְעַטֵּשׁ.


**[The boy] sneezed.** He sneezed.²⁸

footnotes:
²⁸ According to the Zohar, the boy that Elisha revived was Chavakuk the prophet. This is alluded to in v. 16 above, where the prophet tells the woman “you will be hugging [=חֹבֶקֶת] a son.”



### Verse 38

###### Rashi on II Kings 4:38:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/4:38:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/4:38:1)


**שְׁפֹת הַסִּיר.** הוֹשִׁיבָהּ עַל הַכִּירָה.


**Put up the large pot.** Place it on the stove.


###### Rashi on II Kings 4:38:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/4:38:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/4:38:2)


**נָזִיד.** תַּבְשִׁיל.


**A porridge.** A cooked dish.


###### Rashi on II Kings 4:38:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/4:38:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/4:38:3)


**לִבְנֵי הַנְּבִיאִים.** לְתַלְמִידֵי נְבִיַּיָּא.


**For the disciples of the prophets.** For the disciples of the prophets.



### Verse 39

###### Rashi on II Kings 4:39:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/4:39:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/4:39:1)


**לְלַקֵּט אֹרֹת.** גַּרְגִּיר שֶׁקּוֹרִין אוֹרְגָּא, שֶׁמֵּאִיר אֶת הָעֵינַיִם. וְיֵשׁ שֶׁפּוֹתְרִין 'אֹרֹת' יְרָקוֹת, כְּמוֹ: כְּחֹם צַח עֲלֵי אוֹר.


**To gather greens [herbs].** [‘אֹרֹת’ is] field-rocket which is called *oruge*, and it enlightens [=מֵאִיר] the eyes.²⁹ Others interpret ‘אֹרֹת’ as herbs, as in, “like a clear heat on herbs [=אוֹר].”³⁰

footnotes:
²⁹ See Maseches Yoma 18b.
³⁰ Yeshayahu 18:4.


###### Rashi on II Kings 4:39:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/4:39:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/4:39:2)


**וַיִּמְצָא גֶּפֶן שָׂדֶה.** גּוּפְנָא בְּחַקְלָא.


**And he found a vine in the field.** A vine in the field.


###### Rashi on II Kings 4:39:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/4:39:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/4:39:3)


**וַיְלַקֵּט מִמֶּנּוּ פַּקֻּעֹת שָׂדֶה.** צָמְחוּ מִמֶּנּוּ פַּקּוּעוֹת שָׂדֶה, הוּא בוליי"ן בְּלַעַ"ז, סַם הַמָּוֶת. וּבְשֵׁם רַבִּי מְנַחֵם שָׁמַעְתִּי: וַיְלַקֵּט לְבַד מִמֶּנּוּ, בִּלְעָדָיו, פַּקּוּעוֹת שָׂדֶה.


**And gathered wild gourds from it.** Wild mushrooms sprouted from it, *boleiz*, in O.F., which are poisonous.³¹ And in the name of Rabbi Menachem I heard, “and he gathered in addition to the fruit that grew from it, wild gourds.”

footnotes:
³¹ They did not realize that the mushrooms were poisonous.


###### Rashi on II Kings 4:39:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/4:39:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/4:39:4)


**וַיְפַלַּח.** וַיְבַקַּע.


**And sliced [them].** Split them.



### Verse 42

###### Rashi on II Kings 4:42:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/4:42:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/4:42:1)


**מִבַּעַל שָׁלִשָׁה.** שֵׁם מְדִינָה. וְיוֹנָתָן תִּרְגֵּם: מֵאַרְעָא דָּרוֹמָא. 'בַּעַל' לְשׁוֹן 'מִישׁוֹר', וְהַרְבֵּה בַּמִּקְרָא.


**From Ba’al-Shalishah.** The name of a province. But [Targum] Yonoson rendered, “from the land toward the south.” “בַּעַל” is an expression meaning “a plain.” There are many [such expressions] in Scripture.


###### Rashi on II Kings 4:42:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/4:42:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/4:42:2)


**לֶחֶם בִּכּוּרִים.** בְּפֶסַח, שֶׁהַתְּבוּאָה מְבַכֶּרֶת.


**Bread from the first crop.** It was during Pesach, when the grain ripens.³²

footnotes:
³² Although the word בִּכּוּרִים usually refers to the new crop offering that was brought in the Beis Hamikdosh [see Shemos 34:26], here it merely means “bread from the first crop,” without reference to the new crop offering.


###### Rashi on II Kings 4:42:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/4:42:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/4:42:3)


**בְּצִקְלֹנוֹ.** בִּלְבוּשֵׁיהּ.


**In their shells.** In its wrapper.³³

footnotes:
³³ I.e., the grains were still in their husks. Alternatively, after grain is toasted and broken up, it is placed in a vessel known as צִקְלוֹן.—Ralbag


###### Rashi on II Kings 4:42:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/4:42:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/4:42:4)


**לָעָם.** לַתַּלְמִידִים שֶׁהָיָה זָן.


**To the people.** To the disciples [of the prophets] whom he sustained.



### Verse 43

###### Rashi on II Kings 4:43:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/4:43:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/4:43:1)


**מַה אֶתֵּן זֶה.** כָּל לֶחֶם וְלֶחֶם.


**“How will I present this.”** Each bread.



## Chapter 5


### Verse 1

###### Rashi on II Kings 5:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/5:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/5:1:1)


**וְנַעֲמָן שַׂר צְבָא.** נִסִּים שֶׁנַּעֲשׂוּ עַל יְדֵי אֱלִישָׁע, מְסַדֵּר וְהוֹלֵךְ.


**Na’amon the general.** He goes on and lists the miracles that were performed through Elisha.


###### Rashi on II Kings 5:1:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/5:1:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/5:1:2)


**תְּשׁוּעָה לַאֲרָם.** הוּא מָשַׁךְ בַּקֶּשֶׁת לְתֻמּוֹ, וְהָרַג אֶת אַחְאָב.


**Victory to Arom.** He drew back his bow innocently and killed Achov.¹

footnotes:
¹ See I Melochim 22:34 and Rashi there.



### Verse 2

###### Rashi on II Kings 5:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/5:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/5:2:1)


**יָצְאוּ גְדוּדִים.** כְּשֶׁהוֹלְכִין מֵאָה אוֹ מָאתַיִם מֵעַצְמָן לִשְׁלֹל כַּאֲשֶׁר יִמְצְאוּן, הוּא קָרוּי גְּדוּד.


**Bands of marauders [from Arom] went out.** When they go [in groups of] one hundred or two hundred by themselves, to plunder whatever they find, that is called a גְּדוּד [=band].


###### Rashi on II Kings 5:2:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/5:2:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/5:2:2)


**נַעֲרָה קְטַנָּה.** רִיבָה קְטַנָּה, מֵעִיר נַעֲרוֹן.


**A little girl.** A young girl from the city of נַעֲרוֹן.²

footnotes:
² Two apparently contradictory words, נַעֲרָה [=a twelve year old girl] and קְטַנָה [=a young child] describe the girl. Rashi therefore explains that נַעֲרָה does not mean girl, but one who comes from the city of נַעֲרוֹן, which is mentioned in I Divrei Hayomim 7:28. Alternatively, נַעֲרָה can mean a girl of any age, and when modified by קְטַנָה, it means “a small girl.”—Ralbag


###### Rashi on II Kings 5:2:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/5:2:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/5:2:3)


**וַתְּהִי לִפְנֵי.** וַהֲוַת מְשַׁמְּשָׁא קֳדָם אִתַּת נַעֲמָן.


**She became [a servant] to [Na’amon’s wife].** [Targum Yonoson renders,] “and she served Na’amon’s wife.



### Verse 3

###### Rashi on II Kings 5:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/5:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/5:3:1)


**אַחֲלֵי אֲדֹנִי** לִפְנֵי הַנָּבִיא. לְשׁוֹן וַיְחַל משֶׁה, בַּקָּשׁוֹת כָּל הַמִּתְפַּלְּלִים עָלָיו יִהְיוּ, שֶׁיָּבוֹא הַנֵּס לִפְנֵי הַנָּבִיא.


**“My master should plead** [for curing his illness] before the prophet.” An expression [similar to], “and Moshe prayed [וַיְחַל].”³ The supplications of all those who pray for him, will be that the miracle shall come before the prophet.

footnotes:
³ Shemos 32:11. See also Tehillim 119:5.


###### Rashi on II Kings 5:3:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/5:3:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/5:3:2)


**אַחֲלֵי.** שוהיידמנ"ס בְּלַעַ"ז, כְּלוֹמַר זוֹ הִיא בַּקָּשָׁה שֶׁהוּא צָרִיךְ לָהּ.


**Plead.** *Soheidement*, in O.F., i.e., this is the supplication that he needs.



### Verse 4

###### Rashi on II Kings 5:4:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/5:4:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/5:4:1)


**וַיָּבֹא.** נַעֲמָן.


**He came.** Na’amon.


###### Rashi on II Kings 5:4:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/5:4:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/5:4:2)


**וַיַּגֵּד לַאדֹנָיו.** מֶלֶךְ אֲרָם.


**And told his master.** The king of Arom.



### Verse 5

###### Rashi on II Kings 5:5:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/5:5:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/5:5:1)


**וַיִּקַּח בְּיָדוֹ.** מִנְחָה לַנָּבִיא.


**And took in his hand.** A present for the prophet.


###### Rashi on II Kings 5:5:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/5:5:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/5:5:2)


**וְשֵׁשֶׁת אֲלָפִים זָהָב.** זְהוּבִים.


**Six thousand gold [pieces].** Gold pieces.



### Verse 6

###### Rashi on II Kings 5:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/5:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/5:6:1)


**לֵאמֹר וְעַתָּה כְּבוֹא הַסֵּפֶר הַזֶּה וְגוֹ'.** הַסֵּפֶר אָמַר לוֹ, וְעַתָּה כְּבוֹא הַסֵּפֶר הַזֶּה וְגוֹ'.


**Saying, “Now when this letter reaches, etc.”** The letter said to him, “And now, when this letter reaches, etc.”


###### Rashi on II Kings 5:6:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/5:6:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/5:6:2)


**וַאֲסַפְתּוֹ מִצָּרַעְתּוֹ.** 'אֲסֵפָה' בִּמְצֹרָע הִיא לְשׁוֹן רְפוּאָתוֹ, כִּי בְּהִתְרַפּאוֹתוֹ הוּא נֶאֱסָף אֶל תּוֹךְ בְּנֵי אָדָם, וּבְחָלְיוֹ הַכֹּל בְּדֵלִין הֵימֶנּוּ.


**Arrange to cure him of this *tzora’as*.** ‘אֲסֵפָה’ [=gathering], pertaining to a metzora, is an expression of his cure, because when he is cured, he is [once again] gathered in among people, but during his illness, everyone stays away from him.⁴ to dwell within the camp of Yisroel. See Vayikra 13:46. However, even among the other nations a *metzora* is excluded from the general community until he is cured.

footnotes:
⁴ The Torah forbids a metzora



### Verse 7

###### Rashi on II Kings 5:7:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/5:7:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/5:7:1)


**הַאֱלֹהִים אָנִי.** הֵ"א נָקוּד פַּתָּ"ח, לְלַמֵּד שֶׁהוּא בִּלְשׁוֹן תְּמִיהָה.


**“Am I God?”** The [first] ‘ה’ is voweled with a ‘פתח’ to indicate that it is a question.


###### Rashi on II Kings 5:7:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/5:7:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/5:7:2)


**מִתְאַנֶּה.** מְבַקֵּשׁ תּוֹאֲנָה לְהִתְגָּרוֹת בִּי.


**He is looking for an excuse.** He is looking for a pretext to attack me.⁵ He is looking for an excuse.

footnotes:
⁵ Because he was so involved in his evil ways, it did not occur to Yehorom to seek help from Elisha the prophet.—Metzudas Dovid


###### Rashi on II Kings 5:7:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/5:7:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/5:7:3)


**מִתְאַנֶּה.** לְשׁוֹן עֲלִילוֹת דְּבָרִים.


**[מִתְאַנֶּה is] an expression of** “false accusation [=עֲלִילוֹת דְּבָרִים].”⁶

footnotes:
⁶ Devarim 22:14.



### Verse 11

###### Rashi on II Kings 5:11:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/5:11:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/5:11:1)


**הִנֵּה אָמַרְתִּי.** סָבוּר הָיִיתִי, אֵלַי יֵצֵא הַנָּבִיא וִידַבֵּר עִמִּי וְיִרְאֶה אֶת הַחֹלִי.


**“Behold, I thought.”** I thought the prophet would come out and speak with me, and see the ailment.


###### Rashi on II Kings 5:11:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/5:11:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/5:11:2)


**וְהֵנִיף יָדוֹ אֶל הַמָּקוֹם.** הַצָּרַעַת.


**And wave his hand toward the spot.** Of the *tzora’as*.⁷

footnotes:
⁷ Alternatively, אֶל הַמָּקוֹם refers “to the place where he worships God, i.e., towards the Beis Hamikdosh.—Ralbag



### Verse 13

###### Rashi on II Kings 5:13:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/5:13:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/5:13:1)


**אָבִי.** כְּמוֹ אֲדוֹנִי.


**“My father [master].”** אָבִי [lit., my father, means] the same as אֲדוֹנִי [=my master].⁸

footnotes:
⁸ We find instances where אָב is a title that is used for prominent people. See Bereishis 45:8.


###### Rashi on II Kings 5:13:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/5:13:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/5:13:2)


**הֲלוֹא תַעֲשֶׂה.** וְכִי לֹא תַעֲשֵׂהוּ, אֲפִלּוּ אָמַר לְךָ דְּבַר טֹרַח.


**Would you not do it?** Wouldn’t you do it, even if he told you to do something requiring exertion?


###### Rashi on II Kings 5:13:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/5:13:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/5:13:3)


**וְאַף כִּי.** קַל וָחֹמֶר שֶׁאָמַר לְךָ דָּבָר קַל, רְחַץ וּטְהָר.


**Certainly since.** By a fortiori conclusion, for he said to you [to do] an easy thing, “immerse yourself and become clean.”



### Verse 15

###### Rashi on II Kings 5:15:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/5:15:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/5:15:1)


**בְרָכָה.** מִנְחַת שָׁלוֹם שֶׁל הַקְבָּלַת פָּנִים, שֶׁשּׁוֹאֵל תַּלְמִיד אוֹ עֶבֶד בִּשְׁלוֹם הָרַב, שולד"ו בְּלַעַ"ז.


**A blessing [a gift].** A gift of peace, upon greeting, [e.g.,] when a student or a servant greets [his] master,⁹ *salud*, in O.F.¹⁰

footnotes:
⁹ See Bereishis 33:11.
¹⁰ Na’amon lived as a righteous gentile, keeping the seven commandments that God gave to Noach and his descendants. However, he did not convert to Judaism. See Maseches Gittin 57b.



### Verse 16

###### Rashi on II Kings 5:16:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/5:16:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/5:16:1)


**אִם אֶקָּח.** שֶׁדְּמֵי עֲבוֹדָה זָרָה מְעֹרָבִין בּוֹ.


**I will not accept.** Because monies pertaining to idols are included in it.



### Verse 17

###### Rashi on II Kings 5:17:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/5:17:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/5:17:1)


**וָלֹא.** לְשׁוֹן בַּקָּשָׁה, לְשׁוֹן הַלְוַאי.


**“Please.”** An expression of a request, an expression of הַלְוַאי [=were it only so].¹¹.

footnotes:
¹¹ Na’amon would not even take a small amount of earth with asking permission. Radak


###### Rashi on II Kings 5:17:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/5:17:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/5:17:2)


**יֻתַּן נָא.** מֵאֲדָמָה זוֹ מֵאֶרֶץ יִשְׂרָאֵל שֶׁהִיא קְדוֹשָׁה, מַשָּׂא שְׁנֵי פְּרָדִים, וְאֶשָּׂאֶנָּה לְעִירִי וְאֶעֱשֶׂה אוֹתָהּ מִזְבֵּחַ.


**“May there be given.”** From this soil of Eretz Yisroel, which is holy, a load [of earth] carried by a team of mules, and I will carry it off to my city, and I will make it [into] an altar.



### Verse 18

###### Rashi on II Kings 5:18:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/5:18:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/5:18:1)


**בֵית רִמּוֹן.** שֵׁם עֲבוֹדָה זָרָה.


**The house of Rimon.** The name of a pagan deity.


###### Rashi on II Kings 5:18:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/5:18:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/5:18:2)


**וְהִשְׁתַּחֲוֵיתִי.** עַל כָּרְחִי, כְּשֶׁשּׁוֹחֶה אֲדוֹנִי וְהוּא נִשְׁעָן עַל יָדִי.


**To bow down there.** Against my will, when my master bows, because he leans on my hand.



### Verse 19

###### Rashi on II Kings 5:19:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/5:19:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/5:19:1)


**כִּבְרַת אָרֶץ.** שֵׁם מִדָּה שֶל קַרְקַע, כְּמוֹ: צִמְדֵי כֶרֶם, ארפינ"ט בְּלַעַ"ז.


**A distance.** כִּבְרַת אֶרֶץ is the name of a land measure,¹² as in, “portions [צִמְדֵי] of vineyard,”¹³ *arpent*, in O.F.

footnotes:
¹² The distance of a mile.—Radak
¹³ Yeshayahu 5:10.



### Verse 20

###### Rashi on II Kings 5:20:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/5:20:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/5:20:1)


**מְאוּמָה.** חָסֵר אָלֶ"ף, לְפִי שֶׁהָיְתָה לְקִיחָה זוֹ לְמוּם.


**Something.** The ‘א’ is missing,¹⁴. because this taking was [responsible for his being stricken with] a flaw [=[מום.

footnotes:
¹⁴ The word “מְאוּמָה” is written “מוּמָה,” without the ‘א.’ מוּם means a flaw. As a result of this taking, he was stricken with Na’amon’s flaw, that of tzora’as



### Verse 21

###### Rashi on II Kings 5:21:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/5:21:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/5:21:1)


**וַיִּפֹּל.** וְאִתְרְכִין.


**And he jumped off.** [Targum Yonoson renders,] “and he leaned over.”¹⁵

footnotes:
¹⁵ This expression is similar to the one in Bereishis 24:64 pertaining to Rivkah.



### Verse 23

###### Rashi on II Kings 5:23:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/5:23:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/5:23:1)


**הוֹאֵל.** הִשָּׁבַע שֶׁשְּׁלַחֲךָ.


**“Please.”** Swear that he sent you.¹⁶

footnotes:
¹⁶ Na’amon asked him to swear because he did not believe that Elisha changed his mind. Alternatively, הוֹאֵל means “agree.”—Metzudas Tzion


###### Rashi on II Kings 5:23:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/5:23:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/5:23:2)


**בִּשְׁנֵי חֲרִטִים.** מִינֵי בְּגָדִים וְסוּדָרִין, כְּמוֹ: הַמִּטְפָּחוֹת וְהָחֲרִיטִים, כָּךְ חִבְּרוֹ מְנַחֵם. אֲבָל יוֹנָתָן תִּרְגֵּם זֶה 'פַּלְדָּסִין', וְאֶת שֶׁבְּסֵפֶר יְשַׁעְיָהוּ תִּרְגֵּם 'מְחַכַּיָּא'. וְהַפּוֹתְרִין אוֹמְרִין: בִּשְׁנֵי חֲרִיטֵי, בִּשְׁנֵי כִּיסִין אֲרֻכִּים, פירדיי"ש בְּלַעַ"ז.


**In two bags.** Types of garments and kerchiefs, as in, “and the kerchiefs and the pouches [=חֲרִיטִים].”¹⁷ Menachem classified it thus. But [Targum] Yonoson rendered this as “פַּלְדָּסִין [=sheets],” and the one [חֲרִיטִים] in the Book of Yeshayahu, [he rendered] as “מְחַכַּיָּא [=a plate covering the genitals].” The exegetes say that “בִּשְׁנֵי חֲרִיטֵי,” means “in two long pockets,” *brides*, in O.F.

footnotes:
¹⁷ Yeshayahu 3:22.


###### Rashi on II Kings 5:23:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/5:23:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/5:23:3)


**אֶל שְׁנֵי נְעָרָיו.** שֶׁל נַעֲמָן.


**His two servants.** Na’amon’s.


###### Rashi on II Kings 5:23:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/5:23:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/5:23:4)


**וַיִּשְׂאוּ לְפָנָיו.** לִפְנֵי גֵּחֲזִי.


**And they carried them before him.** Before Geichazi.



### Verse 24

###### Rashi on II Kings 5:24:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/5:24:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/5:24:1)


**וַיָּבֹא אֶל הָעֹפֶל.** תִּרְגֵּם יוֹנָתָן: לַאֲתַר כָּסֵי.


**And he came to a concealed place.** [Targum] Yonoson rendered, “to a hidden place.”¹⁸

footnotes:
¹⁸ Alternatively, עֹפֶל means “a stronghold,” a secure place.—Radak


###### Rashi on II Kings 5:24:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/5:24:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/5:24:2)


**וַיִּקַּח מִיָּדָם.** מִיַּד שְׁנֵי נַעֲרֵי נַעֲמָן.


**And took from their hands.** From the hands of Na’amon’s two servants.


###### Rashi on II Kings 5:24:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/5:24:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/5:24:3)


**וַיְשַׁלַּח אֶת הָאֲנָשִׁים.** שֶׁלֹּא יִרְאֶה אוֹתָם אֱלִישָׁע.


**And he dismissed the men** so that Elisha would not see them.



### Verse 26

###### Rashi on II Kings 5:26:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/5:26:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/5:26:1)


**הַעֵת לָקַחַת אֶת הַכֶּסֶף.** לְהִתְעַשֵּׁר, וְלָקַחַת מִמֶּנּוּ בְּגָדִים וְזֵיתִים וְגוֹ'.


**Was that the time to take silver.** To become wealthy, and to buy with it clothing and olive [trees], etc.



## Chapter 6


### Verse 1

###### Rashi on II Kings 6:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/6:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/6:1:1)


**וַיֹּאמְרוּ בְנֵי הַנְּבִיאִים.** נִסִּים שֶׁנַּעֲשׂוּ לוֹ מְסַדֵּר וְהוֹלֵךְ. רַבּוֹתֵינוּ אָמְרוּ: מִכָּאן שֶׁהָיָה גֵּיחֲזִי דּוֹחֶה הַתַּלְמִידִים מִלְּפָנָיו, וּכְשֶׁנִּטְרַד בָּאוּ תַּלְמִידִים רַבִּים, וְנִדְחַק הַמָּקוֹם מֵהֶם.


**The disciples of the prophets said.** The miracles that were performed through him, he continues to list. Our Rabbis said, that from here [we deduce] that Geichazi would reject the students [who came] before him,¹ and when he was banished, many students came, and the place became cramped on account of them.²

footnotes:
¹ I.e., many of the students who came previously, found Geichazi’s behavior and bad character repulsive and did not stay while he was around.
² See Maseches Sotah 47a.



### Verse 3

###### Rashi on II Kings 6:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/6:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/6:3:1)


**הוֹאֶל.** הִתְרַצֶּה.


**Agree.** Be willing.



### Verse 5

###### Rashi on II Kings 6:5:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/6:5:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/6:5:1)


**וְאֶת הַבַּרְזֶל.** הַנְּסֹרֶת.


**The iron [ax blade].** The ax head.


###### Rashi on II Kings 6:5:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/6:5:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/6:5:2)


**וְהוּא שָׁאוּל.** שְׁאִלְתִּיו, וְאֵין לִי מִמַּה לְשַׁלֵּם.


**It is borrowed.** I borrowed it, and I have no funds to pay [for it].



### Verse 6

###### Rashi on II Kings 6:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/6:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/6:6:1)


**וַיָּצֶף.** צָף עַל הַמַּיִם.


**Floated up.** Floated upon the water.³

footnotes:
³ Elisha cut a new handle to fit the head of the ax and threw it into the water. The new handle found the hole in the blade, went into it, and floated back up with the blade attached.—Radak



### Verse 8

###### Rashi on II Kings 6:8:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/6:8:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/6:8:1)


**פְּלֹנִי אַלְמֹנִי.** כָּסֵי וְטָמִיר, פְּלוֹנִי, לְשׁוֹן: כִּי יִפָּלֵא 'יִתְכַּסֵּי'.


**Concealed, secret.** [Targum Yonoson rendered,] “covered and hidden.” “פלוני” is an expression of, “If it be concealed [=יפלא],”⁴ it will be covered.⁵

footnotes:
⁴ Devarim 17:8.
⁵ See Megillas Rus 4:1 and Rashi there.


###### Rashi on II Kings 6:8:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/6:8:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/6:8:2)


**אַלְמוֹנִי.** מִבְּלִי שֵׁם, שֶׁלֹּא הָיָה רוֹצֶה לְהִתְגַּלּוֹת.


**Secret.** Without a name, for he did not want it to be revealed.


###### Rashi on II Kings 6:8:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/6:8:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/6:8:3)


**תַּחֲנֹתִי.** שָׁם אֶחֱנֶה, וְאֶאֱרֹב אֶת מֶלֶךְ יִשְׂרָאֵל אוֹ אֶת גְּדוּדָיו הָעוֹבְרִים לִשְׁלֹל בְּאַרְצִי דֶּרֶךְ אוֹתוֹ הַמָּקוֹם.


**I will make my camp.** There I will encamp and ambush the king of Yisroel or his bands who pass to plunder in my land through that place.



### Verse 9

###### Rashi on II Kings 6:9:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/6:9:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/6:9:1)


**מֵעֲבֹר הַמָּקוֹם הַזֶּה.** מִלֶּכֶת שָׁם.


**Not to pass by this place.** From going there.


###### Rashi on II Kings 6:9:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/6:9:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/6:9:2)


**נְחִתִּים.** חוֹנִים.


**Are encamped.** Are encamped.



### Verse 10

###### Rashi on II Kings 6:10:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/6:10:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/6:10:1)


**וַיִּשְׁלַח מֶלֶךְ יִשְׂרָאֵל.** הָיָה שׁוֹלֵחַ וְרוֹאֶה אִם אֱמֶת הוּא.


**The king of Yisroel dispatched [scouts].** He would send and see whether it was true.


###### Rashi on II Kings 6:10:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/6:10:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/6:10:2)


**וְהִזְהִירֹה.** שֶׁהִזְהִיר אִישׁ הָאֱלֹהִים מֵעֲבֹר שָׁם.


**And warned him.** The man of God had warned him passing there.


###### Rashi on II Kings 6:10:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/6:10:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/6:10:3)


**לֹא אַחַת וְלֹא שְׁתָּיִם.** עָשָׂה לוֹ כָּךְ, כִּי אִם פְּעָמִים רַבּוֹת.


**Not once and not twice.** Did he do so for him, but many times.



### Verse 11

###### Rashi on II Kings 6:11:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/6:11:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/6:11:1)


**עַל הַדָּבָר הַזֶּה.** מִי הוּא מְגַלֶּה סוֹדוֹ.


**Over this situation.** Who was revealing his secret.



### Verse 13

###### Rashi on II Kings 6:13:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/6:13:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/6:13:1)


**אֵיכֹה.** הֵיכָן הוּא.


**Where he is.** Where he is.⁶⁷

footnotes:
⁶ See Shir Hashirim 1:7 for a similar expression.
⁷ According to Malbim, this was not a hostile act. The king sent his men to invite Elisha to his court in the hope of winning him over.



### Verse 16

###### Rashi on II Kings 6:16:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/6:16:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/6:16:1)


**אֲשֶׁר אִתָּנוּ.** בְּעֶזְרָתֵנוּ.


**Those who are with us.** To help us.



### Verse 18

###### Rashi on II Kings 6:18:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/6:18:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/6:18:1)


**וַיֹּאמַר הַךְ נָא וְגוֹ'.** עַל הַקְּלָלָה לֹא הִזְכִּיר אֶת הַשֵּׁם, אֲבָל עַל הַפְּקִיחָה הִזְכִּיר אֶת הַשֵּׁם עַל שְׁתֵּיהֶן, עַל שֶל נַעַר וְעַל שֶל גְּדוּד.


**And said, “Please strike, etc.”** On the curse he did not mention the Name [of God],⁸ but for opening [the eyes] he mentioned the Name [of God] on both of them, concerning opening the attendant’s [eyes], and concerning opening the troop’s eyes.

footnotes:
⁸ Because it is not proper that G’s Name be associated with evil and curses. See Bereishis Rabbah 3:6.


###### Rashi on II Kings 6:18:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/6:18:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/6:18:2)


**סַנְוֵרִים.** חֹלִי שֶׁל שִׁמָּמוֹן, רוֹאֶה וְאֵינוֹ יוֹדֵעַ מַה הוּא רוֹאֶה.


**With blindness.** A sickness of confusion; one sees, yet does not know what he sees.⁹

footnotes:
⁹ See Bereishis 19:11.



### Verse 19

###### Rashi on II Kings 6:19:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/6:19:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/6:19:1)


**וְלֹא זֹה הָעִיר.** שֶׁהַנָּבִיא בְּתוֹכָהּ, וֶאֱמֶת אָמַר לָהֶם, שֶׁכְּבָר יָצָא מִמֶּנָּה.


**Nor is this the city.** Wherein the prophet is found. He told them the truth because he had already left it.



### Verse 22

###### Rashi on II Kings 6:22:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/6:22:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/6:22:1)


**הַאֲשֶׁר שָׁבִיתָ וְגוֹ'.** וְכִי דַּרְכְּךָ לַהֲרֹג אוֹתָם שֶׁאַתָּה מֵבִיא שִׁבְיָה.


**[Would you slay] those you captured, etc.** Is it your custom to slay those whom you bring into captivity (and destroy them)?



### Verse 23

###### Rashi on II Kings 6:23:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/6:23:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/6:23:1)


**וַיִּכְרֶה.** לְשׁוֹן תִּקּוּן סְעוּדָה.


**He prepared.** An expression of preparing a meal.



### Verse 25

###### Rashi on II Kings 6:25:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/6:25:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/6:25:1)


**דִּבְיוֹנִים.** זֶבֶל הַזָּב מִן הַיּוֹנִים.


**Pigeon dung.** Dung that flows from the doves.¹⁰

footnotes:
¹⁰ When they no longer had wood to cook with, they used dung for fuel.



### Verse 26

###### Rashi on II Kings 6:26:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/6:26:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/6:26:1)


**הוֹשִׁיעָה אֲדֹנִי הַמֶּלֶךְ.** כְּסָבוּר שֶׁשּׁוֹאֶלֶת מְזוֹנוֹת.


**“Save me, my master, the king!”** He thought that she was asking for food.



### Verse 29

###### Rashi on II Kings 6:29:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/6:29:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/6:29:1)


**וַתַּחְבִּא אֶת בְּנָהּ.** מֵת, וְרוֹצָה לְאָכְלוֹ לְבַדָּהּ.


**But she has hidden her son.** [Who was] dead, and she wants to eat him by herself.¹¹

footnotes:
¹¹ Alternatively, the other woman’s child was alive and she was hiding him to save his life.—Radak



### Verse 30

###### Rashi on II Kings 6:30:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/6:30:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/6:30:1)


**וַיַּרְא הָעָם.** דֶּרֶךְ הַקֶּרַע, אֶת הַשַּׂק תַּחַת בְּגָדָיו.


**The people saw.** Through the tear [they saw] the sackcloth under his garments.



### Verse 31

###### Rashi on II Kings 6:31:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/6:31:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/6:31:1)


**אִם יַעֲמֹד רֹאשׁ אֱלִישָׁע.** שֶׁהַיְכֹלֶת בְּיָדוֹ לְבַקֵּשׁ רַחֲמִים.


**If the head of Elisha son of Shophot stands.** For he is able to beg for mercy.¹²

footnotes:
¹² He could have ended the hunger through prayer as Eliyahu had done. See I Melochim 18:41-45. But he failed to do so.



### Verse 32

###### Rashi on II Kings 6:32:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/6:32:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/6:32:1)


**וַיִּשְׁלַח אִישׁ מִלְּפָנָיו.** הַמֶּלֶךְ שָׁלַח לְהָרְגוֹ, וּבְטֶרֶם יָבֹא אוֹתוֹ הַמַּלְאָךְ אֶל אֱלִישָׁע, וְהוּא נִגְלָה לוֹ בְּרוּחַ הַקֹּדֶשׁ, וְאָמַר אֶל הַזְּקֵנִים: וְהִנֵּה הַמַּלְאָךְ יוֹרֵד אֵלָיו וְהַמֶּלֶךְ אַחֲרָיו.


**And he sent a man from his presence.** The king sent [a man] to kill him, and before the messenger had come to Elisha, it was revealed to him through the Divine Spirit, and he said to the elders, etc., [after which Scripture states,] “and behold the messenger was coming down to him,” and the king was after him.



### Verse 33
