###### Rashi on II Kings 6:33:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/6:33:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/6:33:1)


**וַיֹּאמֶר.** הַמֶּלֶךְ.


**He said.** The king.


###### Rashi on II Kings 6:33:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/6:33:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/6:33:2)


**הִנֵּה זֹאת הָרָעָה מֵאֵת ה'.** זֹאת אַחַת מִן הַקְּלָלוֹת שֶׁקִּלֵּל עַל יְדֵי משֶׁה: וְאָכַלְתָּ פְּרִי בִטְנְךָ בְּמָצוֹר וּבְמָצוֹק וְגוֹ'.


**“Behold, this tragedy is from Adonoy.”** This is one of the curses that He cursed through Moshe, “And you will eat the flesh of your womb, etc., in the siege and in the distress, etc.”¹³

footnotes:
¹³ Devarim 28:53.


###### Rashi on II Kings 6:33:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/6:33:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/6:33:3)


**מָה אוֹחִיל לַה' עוֹד.** לְהוֹשִׁיעַ, הֲלֹא לֹא יוֹעִיל.


**What more can I hope for from Adonoy?** To save, it will surely not avail.¹⁴

footnotes:
¹⁴ Alternatively, מָה אוֹחִיל means “what more can I pray [=אוֹחִיל] before God?”—Targum



## Chapter 7


### Verse 3

###### Rashi on II Kings 7:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/7:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/7:3:1)


**וְאַרְבָּעָה אֲנָשִׁים.** גֵּיחֲזִי וּבָנָיו.


**There were four men.** Geichazi and his sons.¹ ². Elisha had healed all other Bnei Yisroel suffering from *tzora’as*.—Malbim

footnotes:
¹ See Maseches Sanhedrin 107b.
² These four were the only people suffering from tzora’as


###### Rashi on II Kings 7:3:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/7:3:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/7:3:2)


**פֶּתַח הַשָּׁעַר.** כְּמוֹ שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר: בָּדָד יֵשֵׁב מִחוּץ לַמַּחֲנֵה מוֹשָׁבוֹ.


**At the entrance of the gate.** As it is stated, “He shall live alone; outside the camp shall be his habitation.”³.

footnotes:
³ Vayikra 13:46. This is proscribed by the Torah for people who suffer from tzora’as



### Verse 6

###### Rashi on II Kings 7:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/7:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/7:6:1)


**הִשְׁמִיעַ אֶת מַחֲנֵה אֲרָם.** נִדְמָה לָהֶם כְּאִלּוּ שׁוֹמְעִין.


**Had caused the camp of Arom to hear.** It seemed to them as though they were hearing.



### Verse 9

###### Rashi on II Kings 7:9:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/7:9:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/7:9:1)


**וּמְצָאָנוּ עָווֹן.** מִתְחַיְּבִין אָנוּ לַמַּלְכוּת.


**We will be considered guilty.** We will be held guilty by the throne.



### Verse 10

###### Rashi on II Kings 7:10:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/7:10:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/7:10:1)


**כַּאֲשֶׁר הֵמָּה.** כְּמוֹ שֶׁהָיוּ מְלֵאִים מִתְּחִלָּתָם, לֹא הוֹצִיאוּ מַה שֶּׁבְּתוֹכָם.


**As they were.** As they were full at the beginning; they did not take out what was in them.



### Verse 12

###### Rashi on II Kings 7:12:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/7:12:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/7:12:1)


**כִּי רְעֵבִים אֲנַחְנוּ.** וּתְאֵבִים לָצֵאת אֶל הַשָּׁלָל וְאֶל הַמָּזוֹן.


**We are starving.** And long to go out to the loot and to the food.⁴

footnotes:
⁴ Their enemy figured that the Bnei Yisroel are desperately starving and will therefore leave the city without first investigating whether the enemy is waiting in ambush.—Metzudas Dovid



### Verse 13

###### Rashi on II Kings 7:13:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/7:13:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/7:13:1)


**אֲשֶׁר נִשְׁאֲרוּ בָהּ.** בְּתוֹךְ הָעִיר, שֶׁלֹּא מֵתוּ בָּרָעָב.


**That are left in it [the city].** Within the city, that did not die of hunger.


###### Rashi on II Kings 7:13:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/7:13:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/7:13:2)


**הִנָּם כְּכָל הֲמוֹן יִשְׂרָאֵל אֲשֶׁר נִשְׁאֲרוּ בָהּ.** אִם יֹאמְרוּ מְסֻכָּנִין הֵם שֶׁלֹּא יַהַרְגֵם אַרָם, הֲרֵי הִנָּם בָּעִיר הַזֹּאת בְּסַכָּנַת הָרָעָב כִּשְׁאָר כָּל הֲמוֹן יִשְׂרָאֵל אֲשֶׁר נִשְׁאֲרוּ בָהּ, וְאִם יָמוּתוּ הִנָּם כְּכָל הֲמוֹן יִשְׂרָאֵל, אֲשֶׁר תָּמּוּ בָּרָעָב.


**Behold, they are like all the multitude of Yisroel that are left in [the city].** If they will say that they are in danger of being killed by the Aromeans, behold, [if they remain] in this city they are in danger like the rest of all the multitude of Yisroel that are left there, and if they die, they are like all the multitude of Yisroel that have perished from hunger.

###### Rashi on II Kings 7:17:1

**אֲשֶׁר נִשְׁעָן עַל יָדוֹ.** אֶתְמוֹל כְּשֶׁבָּא לְבֵית אֱלִישָׁע.


**Upon whose hand he leaned.** On the day before, when he came to Elisha’s house.⁵

footnotes:
⁵ See above v. 2. Initially he scoffed at Elisha’s prophecy. The king assigned him to stand guard at the gate to prevent looting of the camp. This would have kept the price of grain higher than predicted by the prophet. However, he was trampled by the people at the gate and he died, to fulfill the prophecy of Elisha.—Malbim

**וַיָּבֹא אֱלִישָׁע דַּמֶּשֶׂק.** לְהַחֲזִיר אֶת גֵּיחֲזִי בִּתְשׁוּבָה.


**Elisha came to Damesek.** To cause Geichazi to repent.⁴⁵

⁴ Maseches Sotah 47a. See also Rashi in Maseches Sanhedrin 107b.

### Verse 10

###### Rashi on II Kings 8:10:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/8:10:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/8:10:1)


**חָיֹה תִחְיֶה.** כְּלַפֵּי חֲזָאֵל אָמַר, שֶׁיִּחְיֶה תַּחַת בֶּן הֲדָד לַמַּלְכוּת.


**‘You will surely live.’** Pertaining to Chazoeil he said that he would live instead of Ben-Hadad [and occupy] the throne.⁶

footnotes:
⁶ It is read [=קְרִי] as ‘לוֹ’, meaning, “say to him, ‘you will surely recover,’” but it is written [=כְּתִיב] as ‘Gא’, and the meaning is “say, ‘you will surely not recover.’” According to Rashi the קְרִי pertains to Chazoeil, and the כְּתִיב pertains to Ben-Hadad; or Elisha meant that Ben-Hadad would not die of his illness, but he would die by assassination. Alternatively, the כְּתִיב reflects what was on Elisha’s mind and the קְרִי is what he said.—Radak



### Verse 11

###### Rashi on II Kings 8:11:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/8:11:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/8:11:1)


**וַיַּעֲמֵד אֶת פָּנָיו.** פָּנִים שֶל צַעַר שֶׁלּוֹ שֶׁהָיָה מְבַקֵּשׁ לִבְכּוֹת, הֶעֱמִיד, שֶׁלֹּא יִבְכֶּה בִּפְנֵי חֲזָאֵל, וַיָּשֶׂם לִבּוֹ לַעֲמֹד וּלְהִתְאַפֵּק.


**He [Elisha] kept his face emotionless.** [He held in] his expression of grief, for he wanted to cry; he held it in, so that he would not cry in front of Chazoeil. He made a concentrated effort to stand and control himself.


###### Rashi on II Kings 8:11:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/8:11:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/8:11:2)


**עַד בּשׁ.** כְּמוֹ בּוֹשֵׁשׁ, וְלֹא יָכֹל לְהִתְאַפֵּק וַיֵּבְךְּ.


**For a long time.** Like “בשש [=delay],”⁷ and he could not control himself [any longer] and he wept.

⁷ See commentary on 2:17 above.



### Verse 12

###### Rashi on II Kings 8:12:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/8:12:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/8:12:1)


**תְּרַטֵּשׁ.** לְשׁוֹן בִּקּוּעַ הַמֵּעַיִם, וְאָב לְכֻלָּן: וּקְשָׁתוֹת נְעָרִים תְּרַטַּשְׁנָה.


**You will dash.** An expression of splitting the intestines.⁸ The original source⁹ [for the meaning of תרטש is], “and bows will split [תְּרַטַּשְׁנָה] young men.”¹⁰

footnotes:
⁸ Alternatively, תְּרַטֵּשׁ means “dashing against a wall or the ground.”—Radak Or, “you will cause their infants to be abandoned” by killing their parents.—Ralbag
⁹ I.e., the conclusive evidence for this definition.
¹⁰ Yeshayahu 13:18.


###### Rashi on II Kings 8:12:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/8:12:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/8:12:2)


**וְהָרֹתֵיהֶם.** מְעֻבָּרוֹת שֶׁלָּהֶם.


**And their pregnant women.** Their pregnant women.



### Verse 15

###### Rashi on II Kings 8:15:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/8:15:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/8:15:1)


**הַמַּכְבֵּר.** תַּרְגּוּם יוֹנָתָן: גּוּנְכָא, וְכֵן וַתְּכַסֵּהוּ בַּשְּׂמִיכָה, תַּרְגּוּם: בְּגוּנְכָא, וְהוּא בֶּגֶד.


**A blanket.** [Targum] Yonoson renders גּוּנְכָא [=blanket],¹¹ and similarly, “and she covered him with a blanket [=[בַּשְּׂמִיכָה,”¹² he renders, בְּגוּנְכָא [=a blanket], i.e., a garment.

footnotes:
¹¹ Alternatively, “a pillow.”—Radak Or, a thick garment.—Metzudas Tzion
¹² Shoftim 4:18.


###### Rashi on II Kings 8:15:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/8:15:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/8:15:2)


**וַיִּפְרֹשׂ עַל פָּנָיו.** לְהִצְטַנֵּן.


**He spread it over his [Ben-Hadad’s] face.** To cool off.¹³

footnotes:
¹³ He told Ben-Hadad that he was doing this to give him some relief of his illness, but actually, Chazoeil intended to kill him.—Radak



### Verse 16

###### Rashi on II Kings 8:16:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/8:16:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/8:16:1)


**וּבִשְׁנַת חָמֵשׁ וְגוֹ'.** יְהוֹשָׁפָט הִמְלִיךְ אֶת יוֹרָם בְּחַיָּיו שְׁתֵּי שָׁנִים כְּשֶׁחָזְרוּ מִן הַמִּלְחָמָה שֶׁנִּלְחֲמוּ בְּמֶלֶךְ מוֹאָב, כָּךְ שְׁנוּיָה בְּסֵדֶר עוֹלָם. בִּשְׁנַת חָמֵשׁ לִיהוֹרָם וִיהוֹשָׁפָט בִּשְׁנַת חֲמִשִּׁים, שֶׁנִּגְזְרָה גְּזֵרָה עַל יְהוֹשָׁפָט לֵהָרֵג בְּרָמוֹת גִּלְעָד, אֶלָּא שֶׁתָּלָה לוֹ בִּשְׁבִיל שֶׁזָּעַק, שֶׁבַע שָׁנִים.


**In the fifth year, etc.** Yehoshophot crowned Yorom [who reigned] two years during his lifetime, when they returned from the war they had waged with the king of Mo’av;¹⁴ so it was taught in Seder Olam. In the fifth year of Yehorom[’s reign], which is the fiftieth¹⁵ [חָמֵשׁ] year” as it appears in Rashi’s caption. Rashi explains the verse that Yehorom son of Yehoshophot, began his reign in the fifth year since it was decreed on Yehoshophot to be killed in Ramos-Gilod. The decree was reprieved for seven years, so Yorom thus ruled for two years during Yehoshophot’s lifetime. Alternatively, “the *fiftieth* year” [as our text reads] may refer to the year in which Yehoshophot finished his dual kingship with his son Yehorom. It was in the year 30*50*. year of Yehoshophot, it was decreed for Yehoshophot to be killed in Ramos-Gilad, but [the decree] was reprieved for seven years because he cried out.¹⁶

footnotes:
¹⁴ See Radak’s commentary pertaining to Rashi’s calculation.
¹⁵ Perhaps the text should read “the fifth
¹⁶ See above 1:17 and Rashi there.



### Verse 19

###### Rashi on II Kings 8:19:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/8:19:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/8:19:1)


**נִיר.** מֶמְשָׁלָה.


**A kingdom.** Kingdom.¹⁷

footnotes:
¹⁷ Adonoy king shines like a light [=נֵר].—Metzudas Tzion See Targum in Bamidbar 21:30 who renders ‘וַנִּירָם’ as ‘וּמַלְכוּ’[=its kingdom].



### Verse 20

###### Rashi on II Kings 8:20:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/8:20:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/8:20:1)


**וַיַּמְלִכוּ עֲלֵיהֶם מֶלֶךְ.** וּמִימֵי דָּוִד לֹא הָיָה לָהֶם מֶלֶךְ, כְּמוֹ שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר: וּמֶלֶךְ אֵין בֶּאֱדוֹם; וַיָּשֶׂם בֶּאֱדוֹם נְצִיבִים, שְׁמוֹנָה מְלָכִים מָלְכוּ בֶּאֱדוֹם לִפְנֵי מְלֹךְ מֶלֶךְ לְיִשְׂרָאֵל, הַמְנוּיִים בְּסֵפֶר בְּרֵאשִׁית, כְּנֶגְדּוֹ מָלְכוּ בְּיִשְׂרָאֵל שְׁמוֹנָה מְלָכִים, אִישׁ בּשֶׁת, וְדָוִד, וּשְׁלֹמֹה, וּרְחַבְעָם, וַאֲבִיָּם, וְאָסָא, וִיהוֹשָׁפָט, וִיהוֹרָם, וְלֹא הָיָה מֶלֶךְ לֶאֱדוֹם, וּמַה שֶּׁכָּתוּב לְמַעְלָה שֶׁהָלַךְ מֶלֶךְ אֱדוֹם עִם יְהוֹרָם בֶּן אַחְאָב וְעִם יְהוֹשָׁפָט לְמִלְחָמָה עַל מֶלֶךְ מוֹאָב, לֹא מֶלֶךְ גָּמוּר הוּא אֶלָּא נָצִיב, שֶׁאַף הוּא הָיָה קָרוּי מֶלֶךְ וּכְמוֹ שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר: וּמֶלֶךְ אֵין בֶּאֱדוֹם נִצָּב מֶלֶךְ, הַנְּצִיב הָיָה לָהֶם בִּמְקוֹם מֶלֶךְ.


**And appointed a king over themselves.** From the time of Dovid they did not have a king, as it is stated, “There was no king in Edom,.”¹⁸ [and] “And he appointed governors in Edom.”¹⁹ Eight kings reigned in Edom before a king reigned in Yisroel, they are listed in the Book of Bereishis.²⁰ Corresponding to them, eight kings reigned in Yisroel: Ish-Boshes, Dovid, Shlomo, Rechovom, Aviyom, Asa, Yehoshophot, and Yehorom, while Edom had no king.²¹ What is written above that the king of Edom²² went with Yehorom son of Achov, and with Yehoshophot, to war against the king of Mo’av [can be explained that] he was not a real king, but a governor, who is also referred to as a king, as it is stated, “There was no king in Edom, the governor was king.”²³ The governor served instead of a king.

footnotes:
¹⁸ I Melochim 22:48.
¹⁹ II Shmuel 8:14.
²⁰ See Bereishis 36:31-43, and Rashi there. The eight kings are listed in Bereishis Rabboh 83:2. However, Bereishis Rabboh lists Shaul as one of the eight and omits Yehorom because during Yehorom’s reign, Edom was ruled by a king.
²¹ See above 3:9.
²² I Melochim 22:48.
²³ I.e., Yorom attacked and struck the Edomites who were near the border.



### Verse 21

###### Rashi on II Kings 8:21:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/8:21:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/8:21:1)


**הַסֹּבֵיב אֵלָיו.** הַסְּמוּכִים אֶל הַסְּפָר.


**That were surrounding him.** Those near the border.²⁴

footnotes:
²⁴ Alternatively, “הַסֹּבֵיב אֵלָיו” refers to those Edomites who had not rebelled against Yehudah and “וַיַּכֶּה” refers to the King of Edom, i.e., the King of Edom struck and killed the Edomites who had not joined in the rebellion.—Radak



### Verse 22

###### Rashi on II Kings 8:22:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/8:22:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/8:22:1)


**תִּפְשַׁע לִבְנָה.** מָרְדוּ יוֹשְׁבֵי לִבְנָה וּמָרְדוּ בַמֶּלֶךְ, וְאֵינִי יוֹדֵעַ מֵאֵיזֶה אֻמָּה הָיוּ, שֶׁהֲרֵי לִבְנָה מֵאֶרֶץ יִשְׂרָאֵל הִיא, וְאוֹמֵר אֲנִי, שֶׁהָיוּ מִשֵּׁבֶט יְהוּדָה, וּמָרְדוּ בַמֶּלֶךְ.


**Livnoh rebelled.** The inhabitants of Livnoh rebelled, and they rebelled against the king. I do not know from what nation they were, because Livnoh is part of Eretz Yisroel.²⁵ But I say that they were of the tribe of Yehudah and they rebelled against the king.²⁶

footnotes:
²⁵ Livnoh is listed as one of Yehudah’s cities that were located at the southern extremity of Yehudah’s territory at Edom’s border. See Yehoshua 15:21, 42.
²⁶ Alternatively, Livnoh was an Edomite town close to the border, but at first did not join in the rebellion with the rest of Edom. However, after the war, Livnoh joined the Edomite rebellion.—Radak

###### Rashi on II Kings 8:24:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/8:24:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/8:24:1)


**וַיִּקָּבֵר עִם אֲבֹתָיו בְּעִיר דָּוִד.** וּבְדִבְרֵי הַיָּמִים אוֹמֵר: בְּעִיר דָּוִד וְלֹא בְּקִבְרוֹת הַמְּלָכִים.


**And was buried with his forefathers in the City of Dovid.** In Divrei Hayomim it states, “[They buried him] in the City of Dovid, but not in the graves of the kings.”²⁷

## Chapter 9


### Verse 1

###### Rashi on II Kings 9:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/9:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/9:1:1)


**וֶאֱלִישָׁע הַנָּבִיא קָרָא וְגוֹ'.** בְּאוֹתוֹ הַפֶּרֶק שֶׁהָיָה יְהוֹרָם חוֹלֶה בְּיִזְרְעֶאל.


**Elisha the prophet called, etc.** At the same time that Yehorom was ill in Yizre’el.


###### Rashi on II Kings 9:1:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/9:1:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/9:1:2)


**לְאַחַד מִבְּנֵי הַנְּבִיאִים.** יוֹנָה בֶּן אֲמִתַּי הָיָה.


**One of the prophets’ disciples.** It was Yonah son of Amitai.


###### Rashi on II Kings 9:1:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/9:1:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/9:1:3)


**חֲגֹר מָתְנֶיךָ.** כָּל חֲגוֹרַת מָתְנַיִם לְשׁוֹן זֵרוּז גְּבוּרָה הוּא, לְפִי שֶׁהוּא שָׁלוּחַ לִדְבַר הַסַּכָּנָה [[צָרִיךְ זֵרוּז].


**“Gird you loins.”** Every “girding of the loins” is an expression of hurry¹ [and] might; because he was sent on a perilous mission (he needed to hurry).²

footnotes:
¹ See above 4:29, and Shemos 12:11.
² Even though one who performs a mitzvah is ordinarily immune from any harm befalling him, this does not apply to exceptionally dangerous activities such as this. See Maseches Chulin 142a.



### Verse 4

###### Rashi on II Kings 9:4:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/9:4:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/9:4:1)


**הַנַּעַר הַנָּבִיא.** נַעֲרוֹ שֶל אֱלִישָׁע הַנָּבִיא, כָּךְ תִּרְגֵּם יוֹנָתָן: וַאֲזַל עוּלֵימָא תַּלְמִידָא דִּנְבִיָּא.


**The lad, the prophet’s disciple.** The disciple of Elisha the prophet,³ so rendered [Targum] Yonoson, “and the lad, the disciple of the prophet, went.”⁴

footnotes:
³ Rashi is explaining the reason for the repetition of הַנַּעַר in this verse.
⁴ Alternatively, “and the lad, the lad who was a prophet [in his own right], went.”—Malbim



### Verse 11

###### Rashi on II Kings 9:11:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/9:11:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/9:11:1)


**אַתֶּם יְדַעְתֶּם אֶת הָאִישׁ.** אַתֶּם מַכִּירִים אֶת הָאִישׁ, שֶׁהוּא מְשֻׁגָּע.


**“You know the man.”** You recognize the man, that he is insane.⁵

⁵ Occasionally, prophets under the influence of prophecy lost control of themselves and acted strangely.—Metzudas Dovid. See I Shmuel 19:24. Also, followers of the Baal were making these derogatory remarks about the prophet.—Radak



### Verse 12

###### Rashi on II Kings 9:12:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/9:12:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/9:12:1)


**וַיֹּאמְרוּ שֶׁקֶר.** אַתָּה אוֹמֵר, מַעְלִים אַתָּה מַה שֶּׁאָמַר לְךָ.


**They said, “That is a lie.”** [What] you are saying; you are hiding what he said to you.⁶

⁶ Yeihu wanted to conceal the prophecy and wait for a more opportune time to reveal it.



### Verse 13

###### Rashi on II Kings 9:13:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/9:13:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/9:13:1)


**אֶל גֶּרֶם הַמַּעֲלוֹת.** עַל דְּרַג שָׁעַיָּא, כְּמִין מַעֲלוֹת עֲשׂוּיוֹת לְמוּל שְׁעוֹת הַיּוֹם, לָדַעַת עַל כָּל שָׁעָה וְשָׁעָה, כְּשֶׁהַצֵּל יוֹרֵד שָׁעָה אַחַת. גֶּרֶם, אורלי"ו בְּלַעַ"ז, לְשׁוֹן פְּגָם, וְ'הַגְרָמָה' שֶׁבְּהִלְכוֹת שְׁחִיטָה, מִן הַלָּשׁוֹן הַזֶּה הוּא, שֶׁשּׁוֹחֵט וְהוֹלֵךְ וְיוֹרֵד, וְנִרְאֶה כְּמִין גַּבְשׁוּשִׁית לְמַעְלָה.


**On the top step.** (Targum) [Yonoson rendered,] עַל דְּרַג שָׁעַיָּא⁷ [=on the step of the hours], a type of steps made corresponding to the hours of the day,⁸ to know each hour, when the shade descends one hour. גֶּרֶם, *ourle,* in O.F., an expression of a notch. “הַגְרָמָה,” pertaining to the laws of שְׁחִיטָה [=ritual slaughtering] is from this derivation, that one slaughters in a straight line, and then [cuts] downwards, making an appearance of a mound above.

footnotes:
⁷ Our text reads לִדְּרַג שָׁrַיָּא
⁸ I.e., the steps were a type of a sundial, each step representing one hour of the day.

### Verse 27

###### Rashi on II Kings 9:27:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/9:27:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/9:27:1)


**הַכֻּהוּ אֶל הַמֶּרְכָּבָה וְגוֹ'.** וּבְדִבְרֵי הַיָּמִים הוּא אוֹמֵר: וַיְבַקֵּשׁ אֶת אֲחַזְיָה וַיִּלְכְּדוּהוּ וְהוּא מִתְחַבֵּא בְּשׁוֹמְרוֹן וַיְבִיאוּהוּ אֶל יֵהוּא, יֵשׁ לְיַשֵּׁב הַמִּקְרָאוֹת, וַיָּנָס דֶּרֶךְ הַגָּן, מִיִּזְרְעֶאל לְשׁוֹמְרוֹן, וַיִּרְדֹּף אַחֲרָיו יֵהוּא. אַךְ יֵשׁ גִּמְגּוּם בַּדָּבָר, שֶׁהֲרֵי לֹא הָיְתָה מֶמְשֶׁלֶת יֵהוּא בְּשׁוֹמְרוֹן עַד יוֹם הַמָּחֳרָת, כְּמוֹ שֶׁמְּפֹרָשׁ בָּעִנְיָן: וַיִּכְתֹּב יֵהוּא סְפָרִים וַיִּשְׁלַח שׁוֹמְרוֹן וְגוֹ'. רַבּוֹתֵינוּ פֵּרְשׁוּ בְּאַגָּדַת חֵלֶק: וְהוּא מִתְחַבֵּא בְּשׁוֹמְרוֹן, שֶׁהָיָה קוֹדֵר אַזְכָּרוֹת וְכוֹתֵב עֲבוֹדַת כּוֹכָבִים תַּחְתֵּיהֶם. וּלְפִי דִבְרֵיהֶם יֵשׁ לוֹמַר, שֶׁאֵין הַחְבָּאָה זוֹ מִפְּנֵי יֵהוּא, אֶלָּא כָּךְ פֵּרוּשׁוֹ, וַיְבַקֵּשׁ אֶת אֲחַזְיָהוּ וַיִּלְכְּדוּהוּ כְּשֶׁהָיָה נָס דֶּרֶךְ הַגָּן, וְהוּא הָיָה מַרְשִׁיעַ לַעֲשׂוֹת מִתְּחִלָּה, שֶׁהָיָה בָּא לְשׁוֹמְרוֹן וְהִתְחַבֵּא מִפְּנֵי שָׂרֵי יְהוּדָה, לַעֲשׂוֹת הַתּוֹעֵבָה הַזֹּאת, לְפִי שֶׁיּוֹשְׁבֵי שׁוֹמְרוֹן עוֹבְדֵי עֲבוֹדַת כּוֹכָבִים וּמַזָּלוֹת הָיוּ, וַיְבִיאוּהוּ אֶל יֵהוּא, אַנְשֵׁי יֵהוּא שֶׁרָדְפוּ אַחֲרָיו, וַיֹּאמֶר הַכֻּהוּ אֶל הַמֶּרְכָּבָה.


**“Strike him down in the chariot, etc.”** And in Divrei Hayomim it states, “And he sought Achazyohu, and they captured him when he was hiding in Shomron, and they brought him to Yeihu. ”¹¹ The verses can be reconciled as follows: “And [he] fled by way of the Beis-Hagan,” from Yizre’el to Shomron, and Yeihu pursued him. There is, however, a discrepancy in the matter, because Yeihu did not reign in Shomron until the following day, as it is explained in this account, “Yeihu wrote letters and sent them to Shomron, etc.”¹² Our Rabbis explained in the Aggadah [entitled] “Cheilek,”¹³ “When he was hiding in Shomron,”¹⁴ that he would cut out the Divine Names and write [names of] pagan deities in their place. According to their opinion, one can say that his hiding was not because of Yeihu, rather the following is its explanation: “And he sought Achazyohu, and they captured him when he was escaping by way of the garden.” He had been acting wickedly from the beginning, for he would come to Shomron and hide from the officers of Yehudah, to commit this abomination, because the inhabitants of Shomron were idolaters. “And they brought him to Yeihu,”¹⁵ [i.e.,] Yeihu’s men, who had pursued him, [after which it states,] “And he said, ‘Strike him in the chariot.’”¹⁶

footnotes:
¹¹ II Divrei Hayomim 22:9.
¹² Below 10:1.
¹³ Maseches Sanhedrin 102b.
¹⁴ II Divrei Hayomim 22:9.
¹⁵ Ibid.
¹⁶ Alternatively, Achazyohu was wounded on the way up to Gur, and he fled to Megido where his condition worsened. The verse therefore uses the expression וַיָמָת [literally=“and he died”] to express his condition although he was still alive. See I Shmuel 25:37 where “וַיָמָת” is used to express the condition of a live person. He then went to hide in Shomron where he was discovered by Yeihu’s men. They brought Achazyohu to Yeihu in Yizre’el and he was executed there.—Radak

**בְּמַעֲלֵה גוּר.** שֶׁשָּׁם הֱבִיאוּהוּ אֵלָיו.


**On the way up to Gur.** For they brought him [Achazyoh] there to him [Yeihu].

**וַיָּנָס מְגִדּוֹ.** מֻכֶּה בְנִכְלִיּוּת.


**And he fled to Meggido.** He was struck as a result of a conspiracy [=בְנִכְלִיּוּת].¹⁷

### Verse 29

###### Rashi on II Kings 9:29:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/9:29:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/9:29:1)


**וּבִשְׁנַת אַחַת עֶשְׂרֵה שָׁנָה לְיוֹרָם בֶּן אַחְאָב מָלַךְ אֲחַזְיָה.** לְמַעְלָה הוּא אוֹמֵר: בִּשְׁנַת שְׁתֵּים עֶשְׂרֵה, וְעַל כָּרְחֲךָ יְהוֹרָם אָבִיו לֹא מֵת אֶלָּא בִּשְׁנַת שְׁתֵּים עֶשְׂרֵה לְיוֹרָם, שֶׁהֲרֵי בִּשְׁנַת חָמֵשׁ לְיוֹרָם מָלַךְ, וּשְׁמוֹנֶה שָׁנִים מָלַךְ, וּמַהוּ שֶׁאָמַר כָּאן בִּשְׁנַת אַחַת עֶשְׂרֵה, אוֹמֵר אֲנִי, שֶׁמָּלַךְ בְּחַיֵּי אָבִיו שָׁנָה, לְפִי שֶׁהָיָה נָגוּף בְּתַחֲלוּאִים רָעִים, כְּמוֹ שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר בְּדִבְרֵי הַיָּמִים: וְאַחֲרֵי כָּל זֹאת נְגָפוֹ ה' לָחֳלִי לְאֵין מַרְפֵּא, וַיְהִי לְיָמִים מִיָּמִים יָצְאוּ מֵעָיו עִם חָלְיוֹ וְגוֹ', וַיַּמְלִיכוּ יוֹשְׁבֵי יְרוּשָׁלַיִם אֶת אֲחַזְיָהוּ. כָּאן הוּא אוֹמֵר בֶּן עֶשְׂרִים וּשְׁתַּיִם שָׁנָה אֲחַזְיָהוּ בְּמָלְכוֹ, וּבְדִבְרֵי הַיָּמִים: בֶּן אַרְבָּעִים וּשְׁתַּיִם שָׁנָה הָיָה בְּמָלְכוֹ, וְכָל יְמֵי יְהוֹרָם אָבִיו אַרְבָּעִים שָׁנָה הָיוּ וְלֹא יוֹתֵר, שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר בּוֹ שְׁלשִׁים וּשְׁתַּיִם שָׁנָה בְּמָלְכוֹ, וּשְׁמוֹנֶה שָׁנָה מָלַךְ, וְהֵיאַךְ אֶפְשָׁר לַבֵּן לִהְיוֹת גָּדוֹל מֵאָבִיו שְׁתֵּי שָׁנִים, אֶלָּא עֶשְׂרִים שָׁנָה לִפְנֵי שֶׁנּוֹלַד, שֶׁהֵם שְׁתֵּי שָׁנִים קֹדֶם שֶׁנּוֹלַד אָבִיו, נִגְזְרָה גְּזֵרָה זוֹ, מִיּוֹם שֶׁנָּשָׂא אָסָא אֶת בַּת עָמְרִי לִיהוֹשָׁפָט בְּנוֹ נִגְזְרָה גְּזֵרָה עַל בֵּית דָּוִד, שֶׁתִּכְלֶה עִם בֵּית אַחְאָב, וְכֵן הוּא אוֹמֵר: וּמֵאֱלֹהִים הָיְתָה תְּבוּסַת אֲחַזְיָהוּ לָבֹא אֶל יוֹרָם וְגוֹ', כָּךְ שְׁנוּיָה בְּסֵדֶר עוֹלָם, וּבְתוֹסֶפְתָּא דְּסוֹטָה. אַךְ בְּכָל הַמִּקְרָא לֹא מָצִינוּ שֶׁנָּשָׂא יְהוֹשָׁפָט אֶת בַּת עָמְרִי, אַךְ מָצָאתִי בְּדִבְרֵי הַיָּמִים: וַיִּתְחַתֵּן לְאַחְאָב, וְשֶׁמָּא זוֹ הִיא אֲחוֹתוֹ שֶׁנָּשָׂא, וּבִשְׁנַת שְׁלשִׁים וְאַחַת לְאָסָא נְשָׂאָהּ, שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר: בִּשְׁנַת שְׁלשִׁים וְאַחַת לְאָסָא מָלַךְ עָמְרִי. וְשָׁנִינוּ בְּסֵדֶר עוֹלָם, שֶׁמָּלַךְ מַלְכוּת שְׁלֵמָה, שֶׁהֲרֵי שֵׁשׁ שָׁנִים קֹדֶם לָכֵן מָלַךְ עַל חֲצִי הָעָם, וּכְשֶׁנָּשָׂא אָסָא אֶת בִּתּוֹ לִיהוֹשָׁפָט, נִתְעַלָּה עָמְרִי וְהָרְגוּ אֶת תִּבְנִי, צֵא וַחֲשֹׁב מִשְּׁנַת שְׁלשִׁים וְאַחַת לְאָסָא עַד מוֹת אֲחַזְיָהוּ, וְתִמְצָאֵם אַרְבָּעִים וּשְׁתַּיִם.


**In the eleventh year of Yorom, son of Achov, Achazyoh became king.** Above it states, “In the twelfth year.”¹⁸ And you are forced [to say] that Yehorom his father died in the twelfth year of Yorom, because he became king in the fifth year of Yorom and he reigned for eight years. Why then does it say here, “In the eleventh year”? I say that he reigned one year during his father’s lifetime because he was afflicted with severe maladies, as it is stated, “And after all this, Adonoy afflicted him [in his intestines] with incurable illness, and it was at the end of a year … his intestines came out because of his illness, etc.,”¹⁹ “and the inhabitants of Yerusholayim made king—Achazyohu.”²⁰ Here it states, “Achazyohu was twenty-two years old when he became king,”²¹ but in Divrei Hayomim [it states], “He was forty-two years old when he reigned.”²² Yehorom, his father, lived for forty years, no more, as it is stated, concerning him, “He was thirty-two years old when he became king, and he reigned eight years [in Yerusholayim].”²³ How then is it possible for a son to be two years older than his father? Rather, twenty years before he was born, which is two years before his father was born, this decree was issued. From the day that Asa took Omri’s daughter in marriage for Yehoshophot his son, a decree was issued upon the House of Dovid that it be destroyed with the House of Achov. And similarly it states, “And from God [it was decreed] for the ruination of Achazyohu, that he come to Yorom, etc.”²⁴ Thus it is taught in Seder Olam and in the Tosefta of [Maseches] Sotah.²⁵ However, in all of Scripture, we do not find that Yehoshophot married Omri’s daughter. However, I did find in Divrei Hayomim, “And he intermarried with Achov.”²⁶ Perhaps this [refers to] his sister whom he married. He married her in the thirty-first year of Asa, as it is stated, “In the thirty-first year of the reign of Asa, [King of Yehudah], Omri reigned [over Yisroel].”²⁷ And we learned in Seder Olam that he reigned over the entire kingdom, for six years before then he reigned over half the people, and when Asa took his [Imri’s] daughter in marriage to [his son] Yehoshophot, Imri gained prestige, and they assassinated Tivni. Proceed and calculate from the thirty-first year of Asa until Achazyohu’s death and you will find them to be forty-two [years].

footnotes:
¹⁸ 8:25.
¹⁹ II Divrei Hayomim 21:18-19.
²⁰ Ibid. 22:1.
²¹ Above 8:26.
²² II Divrei Hayomim 22:2.
²³ Above 8:17.
²⁴ II Divrei Hayomim 22:7.
²⁵ 12:3.
²⁶ II Divrei Hayomim 18:1.
²⁷ I Melochim 16:23.



### Verse 30

###### Rashi on II Kings 9:30:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/9:30:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/9:30:1)


**וַתֵּיטֶב אֶת רֹאשָׁהּ.** וּתְקָנַת יַת רֵישָׁהּ, כְּדֵי שֶׁתְּהֵא לְחֵן בְּעֵינֵי יֵהוּא, וְיִשָּׂאֶנָּה.


**And made up her hair.** [Targum Yonoson] (renders) וּתְקָנַת יַת רֵישָׁהּ [=she adorned her head], in order to have charm in Yeihu’s eyes and he would marry her.²⁸

footnotes:
²⁸ Alternatively, she made herself look beautiful hoping that Yeihu would spare her life.—Metzudas Dovid



### Verse 31

###### Rashi on II Kings 9:31:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/9:31:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/9:31:1)


**וַתֹּאמֶר הֲשָׁלוֹם.** תִּרְצֶה לִהְיוֹת עִמּוֹ בְּשָׁלוֹם.


**And she said, “Is everything alright?”** Do you wish to be with me in peace?


###### Rashi on II Kings 9:31:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/9:31:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/9:31:2)


**זִמְרִי הֹרֵג אֲדֹנָיו.** אִם הָרַגְתָּ אֶת אֲדוֹנְךָ אֵין זֶה דָּבָר חָדָשׁ, שֶׁהֲרֵי זִמְרִי גַּם הוּא הָרַג אֶת אֵלָה בֶן בַּעְשָׁא.


**Zimri, murderer of his master.** It is nothing new if you assassinated your master,²⁹ for Zimri too assassinated Eilah, son of Ba’asha.³⁰

footnotes:
²⁹ She was attempting to minimize this murderous act by citing that what he had done had precedent and was therefore acceptable.—Metzudas Dovid
³⁰ See I Melochim 16:9-10.

###### Rashi on II Kings 9:33:1

### Verse 35

###### Rashi on II Kings 9:35:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/9:35:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/9:35:1)


**הַגֻּלְגֹּלֶת וְהָרַגְלַיִם.** אָמְרוּ רַבּוֹתֵינוּ: שֶׁהָיְתָה מְרַקֶּדֶת לִפְנֵי חֲתָנִים בְּיָדֶיהָ וּבְרַגְלֶיהָ וּמְכַשְׁכֶּשֶׁת בְּרֹאשָׁהּ.


**The skull [and] the feet.** Our Rabbis said, that she would dance before bridegrooms with her hands and feet, and shake her head.³²³³

footnotes:
³² Because these limbs participated in performing acts of kindness, the merit that she had thereby earned was not forgotten. Therefore these limbs were spared from being devoured by dogs. See Pirkei Derabi Eliezer Chapter 17. Also, she would join funeral processions and clap her hands and lament for the dead.
³³ The prophecy in v. 10 above that “no one will bury her,” referring to Ezevel was fulfilled, because most of her body had been devoured, and was not buried.—Radak

### Verse 1

**And with you are the chariot[s] and the horses.** And they have strength to wage war.

### Verse 6

###### Rashi on II Kings 10:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/10:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/10:6:1)


**וּבְנֵי הַמֶּלֶךְ שִׁבְעִים אִישׁ אֶת גְּדֹלֵי הָעִיר וְגוֹ'.** כָּל זֶה כָּתַב בַּסֵּפֶר, וַאֲנִי יוֹדֵעַ חֶשְׁבּוֹנָם, שִׁבְעִים אִישׁ וְהֵם עִם גְּדוֹלֵי הָעִיר הַמְּגַדְּלִים אוֹתָם.


**The sons of the king [number] seventy men, and the great men of the city, etc.** All this was written in the letter, “And I know their count, seventy men, and they are with the elders of the city who are raising them.²

### Verse 9

###### Rashi on II Kings 10:9:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/10:9:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/10:9:1)


**צַדִּקִים אַתֶּם.** כֻּלְּכֶם בְּעֵינֵיכֶם צַדִּיקִים, וּמַחֲזִיקִים אוֹתִי בְּחֶזְקַת רָשָׁע, עַל שֶׁהָרַגְתִּי אֶת הַמֶּלֶךְ, וְעַתָּה רְאוּ, הִנֵּה אֲנִי קָשַׁרְתִּי עַל אֲדוֹנִי וָאֶהְרְגֵהוּ, אֶת כָּל אֵלֶּה מִי הִכָּה, אֲנִי לֹא הֲרַגְתִּים, אַךְ מֵאֵלֶּה תֵדְעוּ אֵפוֹא, כִּי גְּזֵרַת הַמֶּלֶךְ הִיא עֲלֵיהֶם, וְהַהוֹרְגָם צַדִּיק הוּא, וּמְקַיֵּם אֶת מִצְוַת הַמָּקוֹם.


**“You are righteous.”** In your eyes you are all righteous, and you consider me as being wicked because I killed the king. But look, now I revolted against my master and killed him. But who killed all these? I did not kill them. But from these you will know then that it is a decree of the King upon them, and whoever kills them is righteous and fulfills the commandment of the Omnipresent.

###### Rashi on II Kings 10:20:1

### Verse 22

###### Rashi on II Kings 10:22:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/10:22:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/10:22:1)


**עַל הַמֶּלְתָּחָה.** תִּרְגֵּם יוֹנָתָן: לְדִמְמָנָא עַל קוֹמַטְרַיָּא, אַרְגָּזִים וְקֻפְסָאוֹת, שֶׁכּוֹרְכִין בְּתוֹכָן וְגוֹנְזִין תַּכְשִׁיטֵי עוֹבְדֵי הַבַּעַל.


**Of the wardrobe.** [Targum] Yonoson rendered, “לְדִמְמָנָא עַל קוֹמַטְרַיָּא [=to the one appointed over the wardrobe],” chests and boxes in which they wrap and store the ceremonial garments of the Baal worshipers.



### Verse 24

###### Rashi on II Kings 10:24:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/10:24:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/10:24:1)


**נַפְשׁוֹ תַּחַת נַפְשׁוֹ.** נֶפֶשׁ הַשּׁוֹמֵר תַּחַת הַנֶּפֶשׁ הַנִּמְלָט.


**You will forfeit your life for his life.** The life of the guard shall be [forfeit] for the life of the one who escapes.¹⁰

footnotes:
¹⁰ Because of his negligence.—Metzudas Dovid



### Verse 25

###### Rashi on II Kings 10:25:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/10:25:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/10:25:1)


**וַיַּשְׁלִכוּ.** וְרָמוּ רָהֲטַיָּא וְגַבְרַיָּא קְטִילִין, הִשְׁלִיכוּ חֲלָלִים אֶת עוֹבְדֵי הַבַּעַל.


**Threw them out.** [Targum Yonoson] (renders), “וְרָמוּ רָהֲטַיָּא וְגַבְרַיָּא קְטִילִין [=and the couriers and the mighty warriors cast cadavers],” they cast down the cadavers of the Baal worshipers.



### Verse 27

###### Rashi on II Kings 10:27:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/10:27:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/10:27:1)


**לְמָחֳרָאוֹת.** לְשׁוֹן בֵּית הַכִּסֵּא, וְכֵן: לֶאֱכֹל אֶת חוֹרָאֵיהֶם.


**Into an outhouse.** An expression meaning a privy, and similarly [it states], “to eat their excrement [=חוראיהם].”¹¹

footnotes:
¹¹ Yeshayahu 36:12.



### Verse 29

###### Rashi on II Kings 10:29:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/10:29:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/10:29:1)


**רַק חֲטָאֵי יָרָבְעָם וְגוֹ'.** מִדְּאָגָה שֶׁלֹּא תָשׁוּב הַמַּמְלָכָה לְבֵית דָּוִד, כְּמוֹ שֶׁנִּתְיָרֵא יָרָבְעָם.


**However, from the sins of Yerovom, etc.** Out of fear, that the kingdom return to the House of Dovid, as Yerovom had feared.



### Verse 30

###### Rashi on II Kings 10:30:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/10:30:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/10:30:1)


**וַיֹּאמֶר ה' אֶל יֵהוּא.** בְּיַד יוֹנָה.


**Adonoy said to Yeihu.** Through Yonah.


###### Rashi on II Kings 10:30:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/10:30:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/10:30:2)


**בְּנֵי רְבִעִים.** אַרְבַּע דּוֹרוֹת, תַּחַת שֶׁהִשְׁמַדְתָּ אֶת בֵּית אַחְאָב שֶׁמָּלְכוּ אַרְבַּע דּוֹרוֹת, עָמְרִי, וְאַחְאָב, וַאֲחַזְיָה, וִיהוֹרָם.


**Four generations of your sons.** Four generations¹² [as reward] for your having destroyed the house of Achov, which reigned for four generations: Omri, Achov, Achazyoh, and Yehorom.

footnotes:
¹² The four generations of Yeihu’s descendants are: Yehoachaz, Yehoash, Yorovom and Zecharyoh.



### Verse 32

###### Rashi on II Kings 10:32:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/10:32:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/10:32:1)


**לְקַצּוֹת.** כְּמוֹ: קַצְתִּי בְּחַיָּי.


**To show His disgust.** As in, “קַצְתִּי בְּחַיָּי [=I am disgusted with my life].”¹³¹⁴

footnotes:
¹³ Bereishis 27:46.
¹⁴ Alternatively, “began to cut [=לְקַצוֹת] into Yisroel” as in Devorim 25:12, “you shall cut off [=וְקַצוֹתָ].”—Metzudas Tzion Or, “began to strike at those living at the border [=קָצֶה],” i.e., those living in the eastern part of Eiver Hayardein.



## Chapter 11


### Verse 2

###### Rashi on II Kings 11:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/11:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/11:2:1)


**בַּחֲדַר הַמִּטּוֹת.** בַּעֲלִיַּת בֵּית קָדְשֵׁי הַקֳּדָשִׁים, כְּמָה שֶׁהוּא אוֹמֵר: וַיְהִי אִתָּהּ בֵּית ה' מִתְחַבֵּא וְגוֹ', עָלָיו אָמַר דָּוִד: כִּי יִצְפְּנֵנִי בְּסֻכֹּה יַסְתִּירֵנִי בְּסֵתֶר אָהֳלוֹ. וְקוֹרֵא אוֹתָהּ חֲדַר הַמִּטּוֹת, עַל שֵׁם: בֵּין שָׁדַי יָלִין. וְזֶהוּ שֶׁיִּסֵּד רַבִּי אֱלִיעֶזֶר: נִפֵּץ חֲדַר הַמִּטּוֹת, חָרְבַּן בֵּיתְךָ יְכַפֵּר עֲלֵיהֶם.


**Into the bed-chamber‘room.** In the attic of the Holy of Holies,¹, and she therefore had access to the chambers in the Beis Hamikdosh. as it states, “And he remained with her in the Beis [Hamikdosh] of Adonoy, hiding, etc.”² Concerning him Dovid said, “For He will hide me in His Tabernacle … He will conceal me in the shelter of His Tent.”³ He calls it the “bed-chamber” in accordance with, “Between my breasts He lodges.”⁴⁵ This is what Rabbi Eliezer⁶ instituted, [May He remember for atonement] “the destruction of the bed-chamber,” [i.e.,] “May the destruction of Your Beis [Hamikdosh] atone for them.”

footnotes:
¹ In II Divrei Hayomim 22:11 it indicates that Yehosheva was the wife of Yehoyada the chief kohein
² Below v. 3.
³ Tehillim 27:5.
⁴ Shir Hashirim 1:13.
⁵ The Holy Presence rested between the two staves of the Ark within the Holy of Holies. The staves protruded into the curtain like a woman’s breasts. See Rashi I Melochim 8:8 and Maseches Yoma 54a.
⁶ Rashi is referring to Rabbi Elazar Hakalir, the author of this poetic liturgy.



### Verse 4

###### Rashi on II Kings 11:4:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/11:4:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/11:4:1)


**לַכָּרִי.** שָׂרֵי הַמֵּאוֹת לַגִּבּוֹרִים, וְשָׂרֵי מֵאוֹת לָרָצִים. וּלְשׁוֹן כָּרִי שֶׁלֹּא אָמַר לַכָּרִים, שֵׁם דָּבָר שֶל קְבוּצַת הַגִּבּוֹרִים, ברני"ר בְּלַעַ"ז.


**The mighty warriors.** The officers of the hundreds of the mighty warriors and the officers of the hundreds of the runners. The expression כָּרִי is used, and not כָּרִים,⁷ because [כָּרִי] is a noun, [meaning] a company of mighty warriors, *barned*, in O.F.

footnotes:
⁷ Which is the plural of כָּרִי.


###### Rashi on II Kings 11:4:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/11:4:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/11:4:2)


**וַיִּכְרֹת לָהֶם בְּרִית.** שֶׁיִּהְיוּ בְּעֶזְרָתוֹ, לְהַמְלִיךְ אֶת בֶּן הַמֶּלֶךְ.


**He made a covenant with them.** That they will help him in crowning the king’s son.



### Verse 5

###### Rashi on II Kings 11:5:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/11:5:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/11:5:1)


**הַשְּׁלִשִׁית מִכֶּם בָּאֵי הַשַּׁבָּת.** הַמִּשְׁמָרוֹת הָיוּ מִתְחַדְּשׁוֹת וּבָאוֹת בְּשַׁבָּת, מִשְׁמְרוֹת כְּהֻנָּה וּלְוִיָּה, אַחַת נִכְנֶסֶת וְאַחַת יוֹצֵאת, וְצִוָּה אֶת הַמִּשְׁמָר הַנִּכְנָס לְהִתְחַלֵּק לִשְׁלשָׁה חֲלָקִים, הַשְּׁלִישִׁית בְּבֵית הַמֶּלֶךְ, לִשְׁמֹר שֶׁלֹּא יַעֲמֹד אִישׁ עַל הַמֶּלֶךְ לְהָרְגוֹ.


**One third of you of those who come on Shabbos.** The watches would be renewed on Shabbos,⁸ and *Leviyim* that served in rotation in the Beis Hamikdosh. They changed places on Shabbos. the watches of the *kohanim* and *Leviyim*. one would enter and one would leave. He ordered the watch that was entering, to divide into three parts, one third in the king’s palace, to guard that no one rise up against the king to assassinate him.⁹

footnotes:
⁸ See Mishnayos Maseches Taanis 2:6,7. There were twenty-four families of kohanim
⁹ I.e., Yoash.



### Verse 6

###### Rashi on II Kings 11:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/11:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/11:6:1)


**וְהַשְּׁלִשִׁית בְּשַׁעַר סוּר. הוּא שַׁעַר הַמִּזְרָחִי שֶׁבָּעֲזָרָה, שַׁעַר הַסִּירָה. וְהַשְּׁלִשִׁית בְּשַׁעַר הָרָצִים.** אַף הוּא מִשַּׁעֲרֵי הָעֲזָרָה. וְיוֹנָתָן תִּרְגֵּם: בְּתַרְעָא דְּבַתְרוֹהִי תַּרְעָא רָהֲטַיָּא. וְשַׁעַר הָרָצִים, דֶּרֶךְ שָׁם בָּאִין מִבֵּית הַמֶּלֶךְ לְבֵית הַמִּקְדָּשׁ, כְּמָה שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר בְּסוֹף הָעִנְיָן, וְאוֹתוֹ שַׁעַר הָעֲזָרָה שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר אַחֲרָיו שַׁעַר הָרָצִים, קְרָאוֹ בְּדִבְרֵי הַיָּמִים: שַׁעַר הַסִּפִּים.


**one third in the Sur Gate, the eastern gate of the Temple court, the Hasirah Gate. [6.] And one third [will be] at the gate behind the runners.** That too was one of the gates of the Temple court. [Targum] Yonoson rendered, “בְּתַרְעָא דְּבַתְרוֹהִי תַּרְעָא רָהֲטַיָּא, [=the gate which was behind the Gate of the Runners].” Through the Gate of the Runners one would go from the royal palace to the Beis Hamikdosh, as it is stated at the end of this matter. And that gate of the Temple court, about which it is stated at the gate behind the runners,¹⁰ was called in Divrei Hayomim, “the Gate of the Sippim.”¹¹

footnotes:
¹⁰ See below, v. 6.
¹¹ II Divrei Hayomim 23:4.


###### Rashi on II Kings 11:6:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/11:6:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/11:6:2)


**וּשְׁמַרְתֶּם אֶת מִשְׁמֶרֶת הַבַּיִת מַסָּח.** שְׁמִירָה זוֹ צִוָּם עַל הַחֵלֶק הָרִאשׁוֹן שֶׁאָמַר עָלָיו וְשָׁמְרוּ אֶת מִשְׁמֶרֶת בֵּית הַמֶּלֶךְ.


**And you shall keep the watch of the palace without taking your mind off it.** This watch he commanded the first group, concerning whom he said, “will keep watch of the king’s palace.”¹²

footnotes:
¹² See above, v. 5.


###### Rashi on II Kings 11:6:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/11:6:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/11:6:3)


**מַסָּח.** שִׁמְרוּהוּ מְאֹד, שֶׁלֹּא תַסִּיחוּ דַעְתְּכֶם, לְשׁוֹן נוֹאָשׁ, שֶׁלֹּא יְהֵא יֵאוּשׁ בַּדָּבָר, וּמֵ"ם שֶׁל מַסָּח מִן הַיְסוֹד.


**Without taking your mind off it.** Guard it very diligently, that you do not take your mind off it, an expression of “despair [=ננוֹאָשׁ],”¹³ that there should be no despair in this matter. The ‘מ’ of מַסָּח is part of the root.¹⁴

footnotes:
¹³ I Shmuel 27:1.
¹⁴ I.e., The ‘מ’ is not a prefix meaning “from.”



### Verse 7

###### Rashi on II Kings 11:7:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/11:7:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/11:7:1)


**וּשְׁתֵּי הַיָּדוֹת בָּכֶם.** שֶׁאָמַרְתִּי שֶׁיְּהֵא בְּשַׁעַר סוּר וּבְשַׁעַר הָרָצִים, וְעִמָּהֶם כָּל יוֹצְאֵי הַשַּׁבָּת, כָּל אַנְשֵׁי מִשְׁמָר הַיּוֹצֵא.


**The two groups of you.** Who I said should be in the Sur Gate and in the Gate of the Runners, and with them all those who leave on Shabbos, [i.e.,] all the men of the departing watch.


###### Rashi on II Kings 11:7:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/11:7:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/11:7:2)


**וְשָׁמְרוּ אֶת מִשְׁמֶרֶת בֵּית ה' אֶל הַמֶּלֶךְ.** שֶׁלֹּא יְבוֹאֵהוּ נֶזֶק מִתּוֹךְ בֵּית ה', וְלֹא הֻצְרַךְ לְכָל זֹאת אֶלָּא שַׁבָּת רִאשׁוֹנָה, עַד שֶׁהוּמְתָה עֲתַלְיָה, וּבָרְחוּ עוֹזְרֶיהָ מִן הָעִיר.


**Will guard Beis [Hamikdosh] of Adonoy for the king.** That no harm befall him from within the Beis [Hamikdosh] of Adonoy. He did not require all this except for the first Shabbos, until Atalyoh was killed and her allies fled from the city.



### Verse 8

###### Rashi on II Kings 11:8:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/11:8:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/11:8:1)


**וְהַבָּא אֶל הַשְּׂדֵרוֹת.** אִישׁ נָכְרִי שֶׁיָּבֹא לְתוֹךְ הַמָּחוֹז הַקָּרוּי שְׂדֵרוֹת, וְהוּא מְקוֹם מְבוֹא הַמֶּלֶךְ מִבֵּיתוֹ לְבֵית ה'. וְלֹא יָדַעְתִּי מַהוּ לְשׁוֹן שְׂדֵרוֹת. וְיוֹנָתָן תִּרְגֵּם: סוּדְרִיָא.


**And whoever breaks the ranks.** A stranger who comes within the boundary called “שדרות,”¹⁵ which is the place of the king’s entry from his palace to the Beis [Hamikdosh] of Adonoy. I do not know the origin of the word “שְׂדֵרוֹת,” but [Targum] Yonoson rendered it “סוּדְרִיָא.”¹⁶

footnotes:
¹⁵ Any stranger entering this area was suspected of being Atalyoh’s ally who was seeking to assassinate the king.—Metzudas Dovid
¹⁶ Targum substitutes a ‘ש’ for the ‘ס’. It was sort of a passage from the palace to the Beis Hamikdosh.



### Verse 12

###### Rashi on II Kings 11:12:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/11:12:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/11:12:1)


**אֶת הַנֵּזֶר וְאֶת הָעֵדוּת.** סֵפֶר הַתּוֹרָה, כְּמוֹ שֶׁכָּתוּב: וְהָיְתָה עִמּוֹ וְקָרָא בוֹ כָּל יְמֵי חַיָּיו. וְרַבּוֹתֵינוּ אָמְרוּ: הַנֵּזֶר הוּא הָעֵדוּת, שֶׁכָּל הָרָאוּי לַמַּלְכוּת הוֹלַמְתּוֹ, וְשֶׁאֵינוֹ רָאוּי לַמַּלְכוּת אֵינָהּ הוֹלַמְתּוֹ.


**[Placed] the crown on him and [gave him] the Testimony.** The Torah Scroll, as it is written, “And it shall be with him, and he shall read in it all the days of his life.”¹⁷¹⁸ And our Rabbis stated that the crown itself was the “testimony,” for it would fit anyone who is fit for the throne, but it would not fit anyone unfit for the throne.¹⁹

footnotes:
¹⁷ Devorim 17:19. Adonoy king must always have a Torah Scroll with him at all times. Therefore, the Torah Scroll acts as a testimony of royalty. Alternatively, the Torah Scroll is referred to as “testimony.” See Shemos 25:16 and Rashi there.—Metzudas Dovid
¹⁸ Alternatively, עֵדוּת refers to the royal robes.—Radak
¹⁹ See Maseches Avodah Zarah 44a, and Rashi there.



### Verse 14

###### Rashi on II Kings 11:14:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/11:14:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/11:14:1)


**הָעַמּוּד.** הוּא מַעֲמַד הַמֶּלֶךְ בְּבֵית ה'.


**On the pedestal.** That is the king’s platform in the Beis [Hamikdosh] of Adonoy.²⁰

footnotes:
²⁰ Alternatively, עַמּוּד merely refers to a location in that area that was set aside for the king.


###### Rashi on II Kings 11:14:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/11:14:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/11:14:2)


**קֶשֶׁר קָשֶׁר.** מֶרֶד מֶרֶד יֵשׁ כָּאן.


**“A revolt! A revolt!”** Revolt. There is a revolt here.



### Verse 15

###### Rashi on II Kings 11:15:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/11:15:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/11:15:1)


**הוֹצִיאוּ אֹתָהּ.** מִבֵּית הַמִּקְדָּשׁ לִשְׂדֵרוֹת, וְהֵבִיאוּ אוֹתָהּ דֶּרֶךְ בֵּית הַשְּׂדֵרוֹת לְבֵית הַמֶּלֶךְ, וְאַל תַּנִּיחוּהָ לָצֵאת דֶּרֶךְ מוֹצָא הָעֲזָרָה אֶל הָעִיר, פֶּן תִּמְצָא עוֹזְרִים וּמִתְחַבְּרִים אֵלֶיהָ.


**“Remove her.”** Out of the Beis Hamikdosh to the ranks [=שְׂדֵרוֹת] [i.e.,] and bring her by way of the שְׂדֵרוֹת to the king’s palace, but do not allow her to leave through the exit of the Temple court through the city, where she might find allies who will join her.


###### Rashi on II Kings 11:15:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/11:15:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/11:15:2)


**וְהַבָּא אַחֲרֶיהָ.** בְּעֶזְרָתָהּ.


**And anyone who follows her.** To help her.


###### Rashi on II Kings 11:15:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/11:15:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/11:15:3)


**הָמֵת בֶּחָרֶב.** לְהָמִית בְּחֶרֶב.


**Kill with the sword.** To slay with a sword.



### Verse 16

###### Rashi on II Kings 11:16:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/11:16:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/11:16:1)


**וַיָשִׂמוּ לָהּ יָדַיִם.** מָקוֹם, אשטנצ"א בְּלַעַ"ז.


**They made a place for her.** A place,²¹ *aisances*, in O.F.

footnotes:
²¹ See Devorim 23:13 where the word “יַד” means “a place.”



### Verse 17

###### Rashi on II Kings 11:17:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/11:17:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/11:17:1)


**בֵּין ה' וּבֵין הַמֶּלֶךְ וּבֵין הָעָם.** שֶׁיְּהוּ הַמֶּלֶךְ וְהָעָם אַחֲרֵי ה', וְאַחַר כָּךְ בֵּין הַמֶּלֶךְ וּבֵין הָעָם, שֶׁיְּהוּ עֲבָדִים לַמֶּלֶךְ.


**Between Adonoy and between the king and the people.** That the king and the people will follow Adonoy,²² and afterwards [he enacted a covenant] between the king and the people that they should be subservient to the king.²³

footnotes:
²² And that they uproot all the idolatry that had become pervasive at the time.
²³ And in return the king will wage wars for the people.—Metzudas Dovid



### Verse 18

###### Rashi on II Kings 11:18:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/11:18:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/11:18:1)


**פְּקֻדֹּת.** מְמֻנִּים לִשְׁמֹר בִּמְקוֹם מִשְׁמַרְתָּם כְּהִלְכָתָן, כִּי עֲתַלְיָהוּ בִּטְּלָה אֶת סֵדֶר מִשְׁמְרוֹת הַבַּיִת.


**Assignments.** Appointed to watch at the location of their watch according to their charge, because Atalyohu had abolished the order of the watches in the Beis [Hamikdosh].²⁴

footnotes:
²⁴ See II Divrei Hayomim 24:7.



### Verse 19

###### Rashi on II Kings 11:19:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/11:19:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/11:19:1)


**וַיָּבוֹאוּ דֶּרֶךְ שַׁעַר הָרָצִים.** אֶל בֵּית הַמֶּלֶךְ.


**They came by way of the Gate of the Runners.** To the king’s palace.²⁵ the king’s palace.” However, Rashi in v. 5 above explained that the Gate of the Runners was one of the gates of the Temple’s court. Therefore Rashi renders the verse as, “by way of the Gate of the Runners *to* the king’s palace.” Alternatively, the Gate of the Runners was one of the gates of the king’s palace.—Targum

footnotes:
²⁵ Literally the verse reads “by way of the Gate of the Runners of



## Chapter 12


### Verse 3

###### Rashi on II Kings 12:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/12:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/12:3:1)


**כָּל יָמָיו. אֲשֶׁר הוֹרָהוּ יְהוֹיָדָע.** אֲבָל מִשֶּׁמֵּת יְהוֹיָדָע, אָז בָּאוּ שָׂרֵי יְהוּדָה לְהִשְׁתַּחֲוֹת לַמֶּלֶךְ, וַעֲשָׂאוּהוּ אֱלוֹהַּ, אָמְרוּ לוֹ: הַנִּכְנָס לְבֵית קֹדֶשׁ הַקֳּדָשִׁים שָׁעָה אַחַת מְסֻכָּן לָמוּת, וְאַתָּה נֶחְבֵּאתָ בּוֹ שֵׁשׁ שָׁנִים, כְּדַאי אַתָּה לִהְיוֹת אֱלוֹהַּ, אָז שָׁמַע הַמֶּלֶךְ אֲלֵיהֶם.


**All his days, as Yehoyoda the *kohein* instructed him.** But after Yehoyoda died, the officers of Yehudah came to prostrate themselves before the king, and they deified him. They said to him, “One who enters the Holy of Holies for only a moment is in danger of dying, yet were hidden there for six years, so you are fit to be a deity.” Then the king listened to them.¹² instructed him,” i.e., as long as Yehoyada was alive.—Radak Or, “all his days” refers to Yehoyada’s days. After Yehoyada died, Yoash began to worship *asheirohs* and other idols. When Zecharyoh prophesied against him, Yoash had him killed, and the following year Arom attacked Yerusholayim.

footnotes:
¹ Shemos Rabboh 8:2.
² Accordingly, the meaning of the verse is “all his days as Yehoyada the kohein



### Verse 5

###### Rashi on II Kings 12:5:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/12:5:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/12:5:1)


**כֶּסֶף עוֹבֵר אִישׁ כֶּסֶף נַפְשׁוֹת עֶרְכּוֹ.** שְׁנֵי כְסָפִים יֵשׁ כָּאן, כָּל הָעוֹבֵר עַל הַפְּקֻדִים, כֶּסֶף עוֹבֵר, הוּא כֶּסֶף הַשְּׁקָלִים שֶׁבְּכָל שָׁנָה, הַנֶּאֱמַר בּוֹ: וְאִישׁ שֶׁיִּתְנַדֵּב עֶרְכַּת נַפְשׁוֹ, הָאוֹמֵר עֶרְכִּי עָלַי.


**The half-*shekel* [given by every male over twenty,] and donations representing the value of one’s age.** Two types of money are mentioned here. “Everyone who passes the numbering,”³ [The first type is] the money of anyone who passes the numbering, i.e., the money of the annual *shekalim*, about which it is stated, and [the second type is] anyone who volunteers his own value, i.e., one who says, “My value shall be incumbent upon me [to donate].”⁴

footnotes:
³ Shemos 30:13-14.
⁴ See Vayikra 27:1-8.


###### Rashi on II Kings 12:5:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/12:5:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/12:5:2)


**כָּל כֶּסֶף אֲשֶׁר יַעֲלֶה וְגוֹ'.** כְּגוֹן הָאוֹמֵר הֲרֵי עָלַי מָנָה לְבֶדֶק הַבַּיִת.


**All monies that [a person’s heart] inspires him, etc.** E.g., one who says, “I obligate myself to donate a *maneh* for making repairs to the Beis [Hamikdosh],



### Verse 6

###### Rashi on II Kings 12:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/12:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/12:6:1)


**יִקְחוּ לָהֶם הַכֹּהֲנִים.** יִהְיֶה שֶׁלָּהֶם, וְהֵם יְקַבְּלוּ עֲלֵיהֶם לְחַזֵּק אֶת בֶּדֶק הַבַּיִת מִשֶּׁלָּהֶם.


**Let take [the money] for themselves—the *kohanim*.** [I.e.,] it will be theirs, and [in return] they will obligate themselves to make the [necessary] repairs to the Beis [Hamikdosh] from their own [funds] .


###### Rashi on II Kings 12:6:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/12:6:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/12:6:2)


**מֵאֵת מַכָּרוֹ.** מֵאֵת מַכִּירוֹ הָרָגִיל אֶצְלוֹ.


**From his friend.** From his acquaintance who frequently visits him.


###### Rashi on II Kings 12:6:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/12:6:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/12:6:3)


**בֶּדֶק.** בְּקִיעָה וְסֶדֶק הַחוֹמָה.


**Restoration.** Split[s] and crack[s] in the wall.⁵

footnotes:
⁵ בֶּדֶק means “examination.” One must examine splits and cracks to determine their method of repair.—Radak



### Verse 8

###### Rashi on II Kings 12:8:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/12:8:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/12:8:1)


**וְעַתָּה אַל תִּקְחוּ כֶסֶף.** עוֹד, וְאַל תְּחַזְּקוּ הַבַּיִת מִשֶּׁלָּכֶם.


**And now do not take the money.** Anymore, and do not repair the Beis [Hamikdosh] with your money.⁶

footnotes:
⁶ Although the Beis Hamikdosh was only 155 years old at the time, it was in need of extensive repair because Atalyoh and her sons made breaches in its wall. See II Divrei Hayomim 24:7.—Radak


###### Rashi on II Kings 12:8:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/12:8:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/12:8:2)


**כִּי לְבֶדֶק הַבַּיִת תִּתְּנֻהוּ.** אִם יִפְחוֹת יִפְחוֹת, וְאִם יוֹתִיר יוֹתִיר.


**But for the restoration of the Beis [Hamikdosh]—you should give it over** Whether it will be less [than needed], let it be less, and if it will be more [than needed], let it be left over.



### Verse 10

###### Rashi on II Kings 12:10:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/12:10:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/12:10:1)


**אֵצֶל הַמִּזְבֵּחַ.** בָּעֲזָרָה.


**Near the altar.** In the [Temple] Court.


###### Rashi on II Kings 12:10:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/12:10:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/12:10:2)


**מִיָּמִין בְּבוֹא אִישׁ.** מִימִין בִּיאַת הָאֲנָשִׁים.


**To the right, as a person enters.** On the right [side] where the people come in.


###### Rashi on II Kings 12:10:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/12:10:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/12:10:3)


**שֹׁמְרֵי הַסַּף.** סִפֵּי הָעֲזָרָה, הַמְמֻנִּים עַל מַפְתְּחוֹת הָעֲזָרָה, שֶׁהֲרֵי תִּרְגְּמוֹ יוֹנָתָן: אֲמַרְכְּלַיָּא. וְהָאֲמַרְכָּלִים שִׁבְעָה הָיוּ, לְשִׁבְעָה שַׁעֲרֵי הָעֲזָרָה, בְּתוֹסֶפְתָּא דִּשְׁקָלִים.


**The guards of the threshold.** The thresholds of the [Temple] Court, [i.e.,] those in charge of the keys of the [Temple] Court,⁷ for [Targum] Yonoson rendered it as, “אֲמַרְכְּלַיָּא [=custodian of the keys],” and there were seven אֲמַרְכָּלִים, for the seven gates of the [Temple] Court. [This is stated in] Tosefta of [Maseches] Shekalim.⁸

footnotes:
⁷ Alternatively, these were the custodians of the Temple’s vessels.—Ralbag
⁸ 2:15.



### Verse 11

###### Rashi on II Kings 12:11:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/12:11:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/12:11:1)


**וַיְהִי.** מִיּוֹם אֶל יוֹם. **כִּרְאוֹתָם כִּי רַב הַכֶּסֶף בָּאָרוֹן וַיַּעַל סֹפֵר הַמֶּלֶךְ וְגוֹ'.**


**Whenever.** A daily occurrence (constantly).⁹ **They saw that there was much money in the box, and they would go up—the king’s scribe [and the *Kohein Gadol*] etc.**

footnotes:
⁹ I.e., when they saw that the boxes were full they would “bag and count the money.”—Radak



### Verse 12

###### Rashi on II Kings 12:12:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/12:12:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/12:12:1)


**וְנָתְנוּ אֶת הַכֶּסֶף הַמְתֻכָּן.** הָיוּ נוֹתְנִין אוֹתוֹ הַכֶּסֶף הַמָּנוּי, לְאַחַר שֶׁהוֹצִיאוּ מִן הָאָרוֹן וּשְׁקָלוּהוּ וּמְנָאוּהוּ, הָיוּ נוֹתְנִין אוֹתוֹ עַל יְדֵי הַגִּזְבָּרִין הַמְמֻנִּים עַל עוֹשֵׂי הַמְּלָאכָה.


**They gave the counted money.** They would give the counted money, i.e., after it was taken out of the chest and weighed¹⁰ and counted, they would give it into the hands of the treasurers who were in charge of the workers.

footnotes:
¹⁰ Alternatively, the money was “minted and counted,” i.e., the coins without a stamp were minted so that they could be spent.—Metzudas Dovid


###### Rashi on II Kings 12:12:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/12:12:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/12:12:2)


**הַמְתֻכָּן.** לְשׁוֹן מִנְיָן, כְּמוֹ: וְתוֹכֵן לְבֵנִים תִּתֵּנוּ.


**The counted.** [מְתֻכָּן is] an expression of a number, as in, “and the number [=וְתוֹכֵן] of bricks you shall give.”¹¹

footnotes:
¹¹ Shemos 5:18.


###### Rashi on II Kings 12:12:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/12:12:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/12:12:3)


**הַמֻּפְקָדִים.** הַמְמֻנִּים.


**Who were [thus] appointed.** Who were appointed.


###### Rashi on II Kings 12:12:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/12:12:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/12:12:4)


**וַיּוֹצִיאֻהוּ.** לְשׁוֹן הוֹצָאָה, לִשְׂכֹּר חָרָשֵׁי הָעֵץ.


**They paid.** [וַיּוֹצִיאֻהוּ is] an expression of spending, for the purpose of hiring carpenters.


###### Rashi on II Kings 12:12:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/12:12:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/12:12:5)


**וְלַבֹּנִים.** הֵם הָאַרְדְּכָלִין חָרָשֵׁי הָאֶבֶן, שֶׁחַבְרֵיהֶם עוֹשִׂים עַל פִּיהֶם רָאשֵׁי הָאֻמָּנוּת.


**And the builders.** They are the architects, the stone smiths, whose colleagues worked under their orders, [i.e., בּוֹנִים were] the supervisors of the craftsmen.

**And the masons.** The builders of the wall, the architects’ apprentices.

**Silver pitchers, musical instruments.** They are musical instruments.¹²¹³

### Verse 16

###### Rashi on II Kings 12:16:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/12:16:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/12:16:1)


**וְלֹא יְחַשְּׁבוּ אֶת הָאֲנָשִׁים.** לֹא הָיוּ הָאַמְרְכָּלִין בָּאִין לְחֶשְׁבּוֹן עִם הָאֲנָשִׁים הַגִּזְבָּרִין, אֲשֶׁר יִתְּנוּ הָאַמְרְכָּלִין אֶת הַכֶּסֶף תָּמִיד עַל יָדָם לְתִתּוֹ לְעוֹשֵׂי הַמְּלָאכָה, לְפִי שֶׁלֹּא הָיוּ חֲשׁוּדִים בְּעֵינֵיהֶם, כִּי בֶאֱמוּנָה הֵם עוֹשִׂים.


**They did not demand and accounting from the men.** The supervisors would not make an accounting with the treasurers that the supervisors would give the money—continually—into whose hand to give it to the workers, because they were not suspect in their eyes, for they worked with integrity.

###### Rashi on II Kings 12:17:1

**כֶּסֶף אָשָׁם וְכֶסֶף חַטָּאוֹת.** הַמַּפְרִישׁ מָעוֹת לְחַטָּאתוֹ וְלַאֲשָׁמוֹ, וְלָקַח אֶת הַבְּהֵמָה וְנוֹתַר מִן הַמָּעוֹת.


**Money for guilt-offerings and money for sin-offerings.** If one sets aside money for his sin-offering or for his guilt-offering, and purchased the animal, and there was money left over.


###### Rashi on II Kings 12:17:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/12:17:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/12:17:2)


**לֹא יוּבָא בֵּית ה'** לְבֶדֶק הַבַּיִת.


**Was not brought to the Beis [Hamikdosh] of Adonoy.** For repairing the damage of the Beis [Hamikdosh].


###### Rashi on II Kings 12:17:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/12:17:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/12:17:3)


**לַכֹּהֲנִים יִהְיוּ.** וְהֵם לוֹקְחִים מֵהֶם עוֹלוֹת קַיִץ לַמִּזְבֵּחַ, הַבָּשָׂר לְעוֹלָה, וְהָעוֹרוֹת שֶׁלָּהֶם, כָּךְ שָׁנִינוּ בִּתְמוּרָה: זֶה מִדְרָשׁ דָּרַשׁ יְהוֹיָדָע הַכֹּהֵן, כָּל הַבָּא מִמּוֹתַר חַטָּאת וּמִמּוֹתַר אָשָׁם, יִלָּקַח בָּהֶן עוֹלוֹת.


**It was for the*kohanim*.** And they would [use the funds to] buy burnt-offerings, “fruit” for the altar; the flesh was for a burnt-offering and the hides were theirs [the *kohanim’s*]. Thus we learned in [Maseches] Temurah.¹⁴ Yehoyoda the *kohein* expounded on this as follows: Everything that comes from the remainder of a sin-offering and of a guilt-offering should be used to purchase burnt-offerings.¹⁵

### Verse 22

###### Rashi on II Kings 12:22:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/12:22:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/12:22:1)


**וְיוֹזָכָר בֶּן שִׁמְעָת וִיהוֹזָבָד בֶּן שֹׁמֵר.** כָּתוּב בְּדִבְרֵי הַיָּמִים: שִׁמְעָת הָעַמּוֹנִית וִיהוֹזָבָד בֶּן שִׁמְרִית הַמּוֹאָבִית, וְלָמָּה פֵּרְשׁוֹ הַכָּתוּב, לְלַמֵּד שֶׁנִּפְרַע מִמֶּנּוּ הַקָּבָּ"ה עַל יְדֵי אֲנָשִׁים כַּיּוֹצֵא בוֹ, יָבוֹאוּ עַמּוֹנִים וּמוֹאָבִים כְּפוּיֵי טוֹבָה, שֶׁכָּפוּ בְּטוֹבָתוֹ שֶל אַבְרָהָם אָבִינוּ שֶׁעָשָׂה לְלוֹט שֶׁנִּלְחַם עִם הַמְּלָכִים לְהַצִּילוֹ, וְהֵם שָׂכְרוּ אֶת בִּלְעָם לְקַלֵּל אֶת בָּנָיו, וְיִפָּרַע מִיּוֹאָשׁ, שֶׁכָּפָה בְּטוֹבָתוֹ שֶל יְהוֹיָדָע, וְהָרַג אֶת זְכַרְיָהוּ בְּנוֹ, כְּמוֹ שֶׁמְּפָרֵשׁ בְּדִבְרֵי הַיָּמִים. וּמִדְרָשׁ זֶה בְּסִפְרִי.


**Yozochor the son of Shimos, and Yehozovod the son of Shomeir.** In Divrei Hayomim it is written, “[Zovod, son of] Shimos the Amonite and Yehozovod, son of Shimris the Moavite.”¹⁷ Why did Scripture specify? To teach us that the Holy One, Blessed Is He, requited him through people with traits similar to his. Let the ungrateful Amonites and Moavites, who ignored our father Avrohom’s favor, which he did for Lot, (that he fought with the kings to rescue him, yet they hired Bilaam to curse his descendants), and he will requite Yoash who ignored the favor of Yehoyoda, and killed Zecharyohu his son, as it is explained [in Scripture].¹⁸ This Midrash is in Sifrei.

footnotes:
¹⁷ II Divrei Hayomim 24:26. These men are listed as the ones who rebelled against Yehoash.
¹⁸ Ibid. v. 21-22.



## Chapter 13


### Verse 5

###### Rashi on II Kings 13:5:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/13:5:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/13:5:1)


**וַיִּתֵּן ה' לְיִשְׂרָאֵל מוֹשִׁיעַ.** אֶת יוֹאָשׁ בֶּן יְהוֹאָחָז, שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר בּוֹ: שָׁלשׁ פְּעָמִים תַּכֶּה אֶת אֲרָם.


**And Adonoy provided Yisroel with a savior.** Yoash son of Yehoachaz, concerning whom it is stated, “You will strike [and defeat] Arom [only] three times.”¹

footnotes:
¹ Below, v. 19.



### Verse 10

###### Rashi on II Kings 13:10:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/13:10:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/13:10:1)


**בִּשְׁנַת שְׁלשִׁים וָשֶׁבַע וְגוֹ'.** מִקְרָא זֶה מֻכְחַשׁ מִשְּׁנֵי צְדָדִין, וְלֹא מָצָאתִי לוֹ מְתַקֵּן, שֶׁהָיָה לוֹ לוֹמַר שְׁלשִׁים וָתֵשַׁע, שֶׁהֲרֵי עָמַד אָבִיו בִּשְׁנַת עֶשְׂרִים וְשָׁלשׁ לְיוֹאָשׁ, וּמָלַךְ שְׁבַע עֶשְׂרֵה שָׁנָה, נִמְצָא שֶׁמֵּת בִּשְׁנַת שְׁלשִׁים וָתֵשַׁע לְיוֹאָשׁ. וְעוֹד הָא אָמוּר לְמַטָּה בָּעִנְיָן: בִּשְׁנַת שְׁתַּיִם לְיוֹאָשׁ בֶּן יְהוֹאָחָז מֶלֶךְ יִשְׂרָאֵל מָלַךְ אֲמַצְיָה בֶּן יוֹאָשׁ מֶלֶךְ יְהוּדָה, נִמְצָא שֶׁעָמַד יוֹאָשׁ בֶּן יְהוֹאָחָז בִּשְׁנַת אַרְבָּעִים שֶל יוֹאָשׁ שֶׁהָיְתָה שָׁנָה אַחֲרוֹנָה לוֹ, וּבְשָׁנָה שְׁנִיָּה לְיוֹאָשׁ בֶּן יְהוֹאָחָז מָלַךְ אֲמַצְיָה, וְאַף כִּי תֹאמַר שֶׁמָּלַךְ אֲמַצְיָה בִּשְׁנַת מוֹת אָבִיו, וְקָרָא הַכָּתוּב שְׁנַת שְׁתַּיִם לְיוֹאָשׁ, לָמַדְתָּ שֶׁלֹּא מָלַךְ יוֹאָשׁ מֶלֶךְ יִשְׂרָאֵל עַד שְׁנַת שְׁלשִׁים וָתֵשַׁע שֶל יוֹאָשׁ מֶלֶךְ יְהוּדָה.


**In the thirty-seventh year, etc.** This verse is contradicted from two sides, and I have not found a reconciliation, for it should have said, “thirty-ninth [year],” because his father ascended [to the throne] in the twenty-third year of Yoash[’s reign], and reigned seventeen years, consequently, he died in the thirty-ninth year of Yoash[’s reign]. Also, it is stated below in this section, “In the second year of Yoash son of Y[eh]oachaz, king of Yisroel, Amatzyoh, son of Yoash became king.”²—the king of Yehudah—Consequently, Yoash son of Yehoachaz, ascended [to the throne] in the fortieth year of Yoash, which was his last year, and in the second year of Yoash son of Yehoachaz, Amatzyoh became king. And even if you say that Amatzyoh became king in the year of his father’s death, and Scripture calls this the second year of Yoash[’s reign], [nonetheless,] you can deduce that Yoash the king of Yisroel did not become king until the thirty-ninth year of Yoash the king of Yehudah.

footnotes:
² Below 14:1.



### Verse 13

###### Rashi on II Kings 13:13:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/13:13:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/13:13:1)


**וְיָרָבְעָם יָשַׁב עַל כִּסְאוֹ.** בְּנוֹ הָיָה. וְיֵשׁ לִתְמֹהַּ, לָמָּה נִכְתְּבוּ כָּאן שְׁנֵי מִקְרָאוֹת הַלָּלוּ, שֶׁהֲרֵי עוֹד לְפָנֵינוּ הוּא מְסַפֵּר וְהוֹלֵךְ בְּדִבְרֵי יוֹאָשׁ, וְחוֹזֵר וְכוֹתֵב וְיֶתֶר דִּבְרֵי יוֹאָשׁ וְגוֹ', וַיִּשְׁכַּב יוֹאָשׁ עִם אֲבוֹתָיו וְגוֹ'. וְאוֹמֵר אֲנִי, שֶׁלֹּא נִכְתְּבוּ אֶלָּא לְהַפְסִיק, שֶׁלֹּא לִסְמֹךְ מִיתַת אֱלִישָׁע לְמִקְרָא שֶל עֲבוֹדָה זָרָה, לוֹמַר וַיַּעַשׂ הָרָע וְגוֹ' וֶאֱלִישָׁע חָלָה וְגוֹ'.


**And Yorovom sat on his throne.** He was his [Yoash’s] son. There is reason to wonder why these two verses were written here, for [Scripture] continues and tells about events of Yoash, and then repeats, “The rest of Yoash’s deeds, etc.,³ and Yehoash slept with his forefathers, etc.,”⁴ I therefore say that they [i.e., these two verses] were written [here] for the sole purpose of causing a break, so as not to juxtapose Elisha’s death with a verse [dealing] with idolatry,⁵ by saying, “He did what was evil, etc.,⁶ and Elisha became sick.”⁷

footnotes:
³ Below 14:15.
⁴ Ibid. v. 16.
⁵ Alternatively, the verses are inserted here to indicate that Yorovom was placed on the throne by his father during his lifetime, and he ruled for two years.—Radak
⁶ v. 11.
⁷ v. 14.

**“Open the window towards the east.”** Opposite the land of Arom which is located in the east, as it is stated, “Arom from the east.”⁸

### Verse 19

###### Rashi on II Kings 13:19:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/13:19:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/13:19:1)


**וַיֹּאמֶר לְהַכּוֹת חָמֵשׁ אוֹ שֵׁשׁ פְּעָמִים.** הָיָה לְךָ.


**And [he] said, “[If only] you had struck [the arrows at the ground] five or six times.** [I.e.,] you should have.


###### Rashi on II Kings 13:19:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/13:19:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/13:19:2)


**אָז.** אִם כָּךְ עָשִׂיתָ, הִכִּיתָ אֶת אֲרָם חָמֵשׁ אוֹ שֵׁשׁ פְּעָמִים, שֶׁהֲרֵי לְסִימַן תְּשׁוּעָה הִרְכַּבְתִּי יָדֶיךָ עַל הַקֶּשֶׁת.


**Then.** Had you done so, you would have struck Arom five or six times, for as a symbol of victory, I placed your hands on the bow.



### Verse 20

###### Rashi on II Kings 13:20:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/13:20:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/13:20:1)


**יָבֹאוּ בָאָרֶץ.** הָיוּ רְגִילִין לָבֹא וְלִשְׁלֹל בָּאָרֶץ.


**Would invade the land.** It was common for them to come and plunder the land.¹⁰

footnotes:
¹⁰ After Elisha’s death.—Radak For as long as he was alive no Aramean dared to invade as indicated in II Melochim 6:23.


###### Rashi on II Kings 13:20:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/13:20:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/13:20:2)


**בָּא שָׁנָה.** בְּמֵיעָל שַׁתָּא, בִּתְשׁוּבַת הַשָּׁנָה, בְּעוֹד הָאָרֶץ מְלֵאָה דְּשָׁאִים וְיֵשׁ מַאֲכָל לִבְהֶמְתָּם, דֶּרֶךְ חֲיָלוֹת לָצֵאת.


**At the beginning of each year.** [Targum Yonoson renders,] “at the beginning of the year,”¹¹ [i.e.,] at the return of the year, when the ground is still full of vegetation, and there is food for their animals, it is customary for troops to set out [for war].

footnotes:
¹¹ I.e., בָּא שָׁנָה means “when the year [= שָׁנָה] enters [=בָּא].” However, שָׁנָה is feminine and בא is masculine. This difficulty can be resolved by substituting בָּא with בָּהּ, meaning “in that very year,” i.e., the year that Elisha died.—Radak. Or, בָּא שָׁנָה means, “when he [Elisha] had come [to his resting place] for a year.—Ralbag

### Verse 7

###### Rashi on II Kings 14:7:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/14:7:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/14:7:1)


**וַיִּקְרָא אֶת שְׁמָהּ יָקְתְאֵל.** שֶׁהָיְתָה לּוֹ לְקִהוּי שִׁנַּיִם, כְּמוֹ שֶׁכָּתוּב בְּדִבְרֵי הַיָּמִים: אַחֲרֵי בּוֹא אֲמַצְיָהוּ מֵהַכּוֹת אֶת אֱדוֹמִים וַיָּבֵא אֶת אֱלֹהֵי בְּנֵי שְׂעִיר וַיַּעֲמִידֵם לוֹ לֵאלֹהִים וְלִפְנֵיהֶם יִשְׁתַּחֲוֶה וְגוֹ'. וְהוֹכִיחוֹ הַנָּבִיא וְאָמַר לוֹ: יָדַעְתִּי כִּי יָעַץ אֱלֹהִים לְהַשְׁחִיתֶךָ, וּמַה הִיא הָעֵצָה, שֶׁהִשִּׁיאוֹ לְהִתְגָּרוֹת בְּמֶלֶךְ יִשְׂרָאֵל.


**And named it Yokseil.** For it set his teeth on edge, as it is written, “After Amatzyohu came from killing the Edomites, he brought the gods of the children of Seir, etc., for deities, he would bow down to them, etc.,”¹ and the prophet rebuked him and said to him, “I know that God has made a plan to destroy you.”² Now what was the advice? That He persuaded him to incite the king of Yisroel.

footnotes:
¹ II Divrei Hayomim 25:14.
² Ibid. v. 16.

**“The thistle that was in Levanon.** Shechem son of Chamor.


###### Rashi on II Kings 14:9:2

**Sent [a message] to the cedar tree.** Yaakov.


###### Rashi on II Kings 14:9:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/14:9:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/14:9:3)


**וַתַּעֲבֹר חַיַּת הַשָּׂדֶה.** בְּנֵי יַעֲקֹב בָּאוּ עַל הַחֲלָלִים, אַף כָּאן אַתָּה בָּא לְהִתְמַשֵּׁל אֵלַי. וּמְשַׁל בִּזָּיוֹן הוּא, שֶׁדִּמָּהוּ לְחוֹחַ, וְאֶת עַצְמוֹ לְאֶרֶז.


**And it passed by the wild beast [that was in Levanon].** “Yaakov’s sons came upon the slain.”⁴ Here too, you come to compare yourself to me. This comparison was to humiliate him, for he likened him [Amatzyohu] to a thistle, and himself to a cedar [tree].⁵

**And the wild beast that was in Levanon passed by and trampled, etc.** Because you are arrogant, the troops of my armies will trample you too.

**And the children [held] hostage.** The children of the princes, who were placed in the king’s palace as security that their fathers would not rebel against him. And so did [Targum] Yonoson render, “ the children of the princes.”

**And Amatzyohu … lived, etc.** The entire fifteen years, Uzziyohu his son reigned during his lifetime, for so it is written, “And since the time that Amatzyoh turned away from following the Adonoy.”⁶

**וַיָּנָס לָכִישָׁה.** שָׁם הָיָה כָּל חָמֵשׁ עֶשְׂרֵה שָׁנָה.

### Verse 22

###### Rashi on II Kings 14:22:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/14:22:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/14:22:1)


**אַחֲרַי שְׁכַב הַמֶּלֶךְ עִם אֲבֹתָיו.** אַחֲרֵי מוֹת אֲמַצְיָה אָבִיו, מִכָּאן אַתָּה לָמֵד שֶׁמָּלַךְ עֻזִּיָּה בִּימֵי אָבִיו, וּמִכָּל מָקוֹם לֹא נִתְּנָה אֵילָת בְּיָדוֹ עַד לְאַחַר מוֹת אָבִיו, שֶׁאִם לֹא מָלַךְ בְּחַיֵּי אָבִיו, לָמָּה לּוֹ לוֹמַר אַחֲרֵי שְׁכַב הַמֶּלֶךְ עִם אֲבֹתָיו, וּכְשֶׁאַתָּה מוֹנֶה יְמֵי הַבַּיִת עַל פִּי שְׁנוֹת הַמְּלָכִים, אִם אֵינְךָ מְחַסֵּר חָמֵשׁ עֶשְׂרֵה שָׁנִים הַלָּלוּ שֶׁנִּמְנֵית לַאֲמַצְיָה וּלְעֻזִּיָּהוּ, תִּמְצָא שֶׁעָמַד אַרְבַּע מֵאוֹת וְעֶשְׂרִים וְחָמֵשׁ.


**After the king lay with his forefathers.** After the death of his father Amatzyoh. From here you deduce that Uzziyoh reigned during his father’s lifetime. Nevertheless, Eilas was not delivered into his hand until after his father’s death, for if he did not reign during his father’s lifetime, why was it necessary to state, “after the king lay with his forefathers”? And when you count the days of the Beis [Hamikdosh] according to the years of the kings, if you do not subtract these fifteen years, which are counted for Amatzyoh and Uzziyohu, you will find that it existed four hundred twenty-five years.⁸

footnotes:
⁸ The Beis Hamikdosh stood 410 years according to the Gemara in Maseches Yoma 9a; this figure can be arrived at by subtracting these 15 years from 425.

###### Rashi on II Kings 14:25:1

**הוּא הֵשִׁיב.** מִיַּד מַלְכֵי אֲרָם.


**He restored.** From the hand of Arom.


###### Rashi on II Kings 14:25:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/14:25:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/14:25:2)


**יוֹנָה בֶן אֲמִתַּי.** הוּא שֶׁמָּשַׁח אֶת יֵהוּא, שֶׁאָמַר לוֹ בְּנֵי רִבֵּעִים יֵשְׁבוּ לָךְ. אֲבָל רַבּוֹתֵינוּ דִּקְדְּקוּ, וַיְהִי דְבַר ה' אֶל יוֹנָה שֵׁנִית, וְלֹא שְׁלִישִׁית, לְפִי שֶׁתָּבַע כְּבוֹד הַבֵּן, וְלֹא תָבַע כְּבוֹד הָאָב, וְהוּקְשָׁה לָהֶם זֶה הַמִּקְרָא, וְתֵרְצוּהוּ, כִּדְבַר ה' אֲשֶׁר דִּבֵּר בְּיַד יוֹנָה לְנִינְוֵה, וְנֶהְפְּכָה גְּזֵרָה רָעָה לְטוֹבָה, כָּךְ נֶהְפַּךְ לְיִשְׂרָאֵל בִּימֵי יָרָבְעָם בֶּן יוֹאָשׁ, מֵרָעָה שֶׁהָיוּ בָהּ, אֲשֶׁר אִבְּדָם מֶלֶךְ אֲרָם וַיְשִׂימֵם כֶּעָפָר לָדוּשׁ, וְעַכְשָׁיו נֶהְפַּךְ לָהֶם לְטוֹבָה.


**Yonah son of Amitai.** He was the one who anointed Yeihu, who said to him, “[Your] descendants of the fourth generation will occupy [the throne of Yisroel].”⁹ Our Rabbis, however, deduced, “And the word of Adonoy came to Yonah a second time,”¹⁰ but not a third time, because he demanded the honor of the son, but he did not demand the honor of the Father. This verse was therefore difficult to them, and they reconciled it [as follows]: Just like the word of Adonoy, Who had spoken through Yonah concerning Nineveh, and the evil decree was overturned for good, so was it changed for Yisroel in the days of Yerovom son of Yoash, from the distress in which they were found, that “the Arom’s king had destroyed them and trampled them like dirt,”¹¹ and now it was changed for the good.¹²

footnotes:
⁹ Yonah 3:1.
¹⁰ Alternatively, God spoke to Yonah only twice concerning Nineveh, but He spoke to him many times concerning other matters.—Radak
¹¹ Above, 13:7.
¹² See Maseches Yevamos 98a.



### Verse 26

###### Rashi on II Kings 14:26:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/14:26:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/14:26:1)


**מֹרֶה מְאֹד.** מֵצֵר מְאֹד, קונטריאי"ש בְּלַעַ"ז, כְּמוֹ: אֲשֶׁר יַמְרֶה אֶת פִּיךָ: מַמְרִים הֱיִיתֶם: וַתִּהְיֶי מֹרַת רוּחַ. וְיֵשׁ פּוֹתְרִים: מוֹרֶה מְאֹד, יָרוּד מְאֹד, לְשׁוֹן: יָרָה בַיָּם.


**Intensifying increasingly.** Very vexing, *contrarios*, in O.F., as in, “who will rebel [יַמְרֶה] against your command,”¹³ [and as in,] “You have been contrary [מַמְרִים],”¹⁴ [and as in,] “and they were a source of spiritual bitterness [מֹרַת רוּח].”¹⁵ Others interpret מוֹרֶה מְאֹד as “very low,” an expression of, “He hurled [יָרָה] into the sea.”¹⁶
