¹³ Yehoshua 1:18.
¹⁴ Devorim 9:24.
¹⁵ Bereishis 26:38.
¹⁶ Shemos 15:4.



## Chapter 15


### Verse 1

###### Rashi on II Kings 15:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/15:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/15:1:1)


**בִּשְׁנַת עֶשְׂרִים וָשֶׁבַע שָׁנִים לְיָרָבְעָם וְגוֹ'.** אֶפְשָׁר לוֹמַר כֵּן, וַהֲלֹא עֻזִּיָּה וְיָרָבְעָם מָלְכוּ כְּאַחַת, כְּמוֹ שֶׁפֵּרֵשׁ בְּסָמוּךְ, אֶלָּא מַה תַּלְמוּד לוֹמַר בִּשְׁנַת עֶשְׂרִים וָשֶׁבַע לְיָרָבְעָם מָלַךְ עֲזַרְיָה, שֶׁמָּלַךְ מַלְכוּת מְנֻגַּעַת, לָמַדְנוּ שֶׁנִּתְנַגַּע בִּשְׁנַת עֶשְׂרִים וָשֶׁבַע לְמַלְכוּתוֹ.


**In the twenty-seventh year of Yorovom, etc.** Is it possible to say so? Did not Uzziyoh¹ and Yorovom reign simultaneously, as was explained just before? What, then, is the meaning of the verse, ”In the twenty-seventh year of Yorovom … Azaryoh … became king”? That [from that year] he reigned a plagued kingship, [i.e.,] that he was stricken with *tzora'as* in the twenty-seventh year of his reign.

footnotes:
¹ Azaryoh and Uzziyohu refer to the same person. See below verses 13 and 32.



### Verse 5

###### Rashi on II Kings 15:5:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/15:5:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/15:5:1)


**וַיְנַגַּע ה' אֶת הַמֶּלֶךְ.** מְפֹרָשׁ בְּדִבְרֵי הַיָּמִים שֶׁנִּכְנַס לָהֵיכָל לְהַקְטִיר עַל הַמִּזְבֵּחַ הַקְּטֹרֶת.


**Adonoy brought a plague upon the king.** It is explained in Divrei Hayomim² that he entered the Temple to burn incense on the Incense Altar.³ for attempting to assume the role of the *kohein gadol* and burn incense on the altar. See II Divrei Hayomim 26-16:21 and Rashi in Bamidbar 17:5.

footnotes:
² II Divrei Hayomim 26:16-21.
³ He was punished with tzora'as


###### Rashi on II Kings 15:5:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/15:5:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/15:5:2)


**בְּבֵית הַחָפְשִׁית.** עָשָׂה לוֹ בַּיִת בְּבֵית הַקְּבָרוֹת, כְּמָה דְּאַתְּ אֲמַר בַּמֵּתִים חָפְשִׁי.


**In a retirement house.** He made himself a house in the cemetery,⁴ is forced to live in isolation. He was therefore unable to attend to his royal duties and was thus “freed” from his responsibilities. ⁵ is accounted as dead. And like the dead who is free from the many toils of life, as it states in Tehilim 88:6, “I am [considered] among the dead who are free,” so is a *metzora*. as it is stated, ”I am [considered] among the dead who are free.”⁶ In [Talmud] Yerushalmi.

footnotes:
⁴ The word חפשי means freedom. The Gemara in Maseches Horiyos 10a explains that Uzziyohu became “free” of the duties of being king. Targum indicates that he lived outside the walls of Yerusholayim. The reason is because a metzora
⁵ The house was in the cemetery because our Rabbis taught in Maseches Nedarim 12b that a metzora
⁶ Tehilim 88:6.



### Verse 8

###### Rashi on II Kings 15:8:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/15:8:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/15:8:1)


**בִּשְׁנַת שְׁלשִׁים וּשְׁמֹנֶה שָׁנָה לַעֲזַרְיָהוּ מֶלֶךְ יְהוּדָה מָלַךְ זְכַרְיָהוּ.** אַף מִכָּאן יֵשׁ לִלְמֹד שֶׁמָּלַךְ עֲזַרְיָה מִשֶּׁמֵּת יוֹאָשׁ מֶלֶךְ יִשְׂרָאֵל חָמֵשׁ עֶשְׂרֵה שָׁנָה בְּחַיֵּי אֲמַצְיָה אָבִיו, שֶׁאִם לֹא עָמַד עַד שֶׁמֵּת אָבִיו, נִמְצָא שֶׁלֹּא עָמַד עַד בִּשְׁנַת חָמֵשׁ עֶשְׂרֵה לְיָרָבְעָם בֶּן יוֹאָשׁ, וְיָרָבְעָם מָלַךְ אַרְבָּעִים וְאַחַת, נִמְצָא שֶׁמֵּת יָרָבְעָם בִּשְׁנַת עֶשְׂרִים וָשֶׁבַע לְעֻזִּיָּה, וְהֵיאַךְ הוּא אוֹמֵר בִּשְׁנַת שְׁלשִׁים וּשְׁמוֹנֶה. אֶלָּא עַל כָּרְחֲךָ, עֻזִּיָּה וְיָרָבְעָם מָלְכוּ כְּאַחַת, אֶלָּא שֶׁיָּרָבְעָם מָלַךְ בְּחַיֵּי אָבִיו יְהוֹאָשׁ שָׁלשׁ שָׁנִים, לְכָךְ הוּא אוֹמֵר וְיָרָבְעָם יָשַׁב עַל כִּסְאוֹ, שֶׁיָּשַׁב כְּבָר, וְעֻזִּיָּה עָמַד מִשֶּׁמֵּת יוֹאָשׁ, וּמָלַךְ יָרָבְעָם מַלְכוּת שְׁלֵמָה. וּבְסֵדֶר עוֹלָם רָאִיתִי, שֶׁיָּרָבְעָם מָלַךְ בְּחַיֵּי אָבִיו שָׁנָה, וְאֵינִי יוֹדֵעַ אִם שִׁבּוּשׁ הַסּוֹפְרִים הוּא, שֶׁאֵינוֹ יָכוֹל לְיַשֵּׁב בִּשְׁנַת שְׁלשִׁים וּשְׁמוֹנֶה לַעֲזַרְיָה מָלַךְ זְכַרְיָה, אֶלָּא בְּעִנְיָן זֶה.


**In the thirty-eighth year of Azaryohu king of Yehudah, Zecharyoh … became king.** From here too, it is possible to deduce that Azaryoh[u] reigned from the time that Yoash king of Yisroel died, fifteen years during the lifetime of Amatzyoh his father, for if he did not become [king] until his father died, it follows that he did not become [king] until the fifteenth year of Yorovom son of Yoash. Now, since Yorovom reigned forty-one years, it follows that Yorovom died in the twenty-seventh year of Uzziyoh. So, how does [Scripture] state, “In the thirty-eighth year?” Rather, we are forced [to deduce] that Uzziyoh and Yorovom reigned simultaneously, except that Yorovom reigned during the lifetime of his father Yehoash—three years. Therefore [Scripture] states, ”and Yorovom had sat on his throne,”⁷ [i.e., on the throne] that he had already sat. And Uzziyoh became [king] when Yoash died and Yorovom reigned over a complete monarchy. I saw in Seder Olam that Yorovom reigned one year during his father's lifetime, and I do not know whether it is the writers' error, for I cannot reconcile, ”In the thirty-eighth year of Azaryohu … Zecharyohu … reigned,” except in this manner.

footnotes:
⁷ Above, 13:13.

**בִּשְׁנַת עֶשְׂרִים לְיוֹתָם.** הָיָה לוֹ לוֹמַר בִּשְׁנַת אַרְבַּע לְאָחָז, שֶׁהֲרֵי כָּל יְמֵי מַלְכוּת יוֹתָם שֵׁשׁ עֶשְׂרֵה שָׁנָה, אֶלָּא לְפִי שֶׁהָיָה אָחָז רָשָׁע, רָצָה הַכָּתוּב לִמְנוֹתָם לְיוֹתָם בַּקֶּבֶר, וְלֹא לְאָחָז בַּחַיִּים, כָּךְ הוּא בְּסֵדֶר עוֹלָם.


**In the twentieth year of Yosam.** He should have said, “in the fourth year of Achaz,” because Yosam's entire reign lasted [only] sixteen years, but since Achaz was wicked, Scripture prefers to count [the years with reference] to Yosam who was in the grave, rather than to Achaz, who was alive.¹³ Thus it is [stated] in Seder Olam.

### Verse 14

###### Rashi on II Kings 16:14:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/16:14:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/16:14:1)


**וְאֵת הַמִּזְבַּח הַנְּחשֶׁת וַיַּקְרֵב וְגוֹ'.** אִי אֶפְשָׁר לוֹמַר מִזְבַּח הַנְּחשֶׁת שֶׁעָשָׂה משֶׁה, שֶׁהֲרֵי נִגְנַז, וְאִי אֶפְשָׁר לוֹמַר מִזְבַּח אֲבָנִים שֶׁעָשָׂה שְׁלֹמֹה וּקְרָאוֹ מִזְבַּח הַנְּחשֶׁת, שֶׁהֲרֵי אִי אֶפְשָׁר לְקָרְבוֹ מִזָּוִית לְזָוִית אֶלָּא אִם הוֹרְסוֹ, וַהֲרֵי שָׁנִינוּ: אֵשׁ שֶׁיָּרְדָה בִּימֵי שְׁלֹמֹה לֹא נִסְתַּלְּקָה מֵעַל הַמִּזְבֵּחַ, עַד שֶׁבָּא מְנַשֶּׁה וְסִלְּקָהּ, שֶׁהוּא הָרַס אֶת הַמִּזְבֵּחַ, כְּמוֹ שֶׁמָּצִינוּ בְּאַגָּדַת חֵלֶק. אֵין לִי לְפָרֵשׁ מִזְבֵּחַ זֶה, אֶלָּא בְּכִיּוֹרוֹת וּמְכוֹנוֹת שֶׁל נְחשֶׁת, שֶׁהָיוּ צָרְכֵי מִזְבֵּחַ, וְהָיוּ אֵצֶל הַמִּזְבֵּחַ הַקֹּדֶשׁ, וּכְשֶׁעָשָׂה אָחָז אֶת הַמִּזְבֵּחַ לַעֲבוֹדָה זָרָה, סִלֵּק אֶת הַכִּיּוֹר וְהִקְרִיבוֹ לְצַד אַחֵר מֵאֵת פְּנֵי הַבַּיִת, שֶׁלֹּא לְהַפְסִיק בֵּין הַמִּזְבֵּחַ שֶׁעָשָׂה וּבֵין בֵּית ה', וַיִּתֵּן אוֹתוֹ אֵצֶל מִזְבַּח הַקֹּדֶשׁ צָפוֹנָה. וּמָצִינוּ שֶׁפֵּרְשׁוּ רַבּוֹתֵינוּ דֻּגְמָתוֹ: וַיָּבֹאוּ וַיַּעַמְדוּ אֵצֶל מִזְבַּח הַנְּחשֶׁת, מִזְבַּח הַנְּחשֶׁת מִי הֲוָה, אָמַר לָהֶם הִתְחִילוּ מִמָּקוֹם שֶׁאוֹמְרִין שִׁירָה לְפָנַי. הֲרֵי כָּאן לִכְלֵי שִׁיר שֶׁל נְחשֶׁת, קְרָאוֹ מִזְבֵּחַ.


**And the Copper Altar … he brought, etc.** It is impossible to say that this was the Copper Altar that Moshe made, because it was hidden. It is also impossible to say that his was the stone altar that Shlomo made, and he called it the “Copper Altar,”² because it was impossible to move it from one corner to another without taking it apart,³ but we learned that the fire that descended in Shlomo's time, did not leave the altar until Menashe came and removed it,⁴ for he demolished the altar, as we find in the Aggadah of [the chapter entitled] “Cheilek.”⁵ I have no way to explain this “[copper] altar” except as [referring to] the basin and the bases of copper, which were accessories of the altar and were near the holy altar. Now, when Achaz made the altar for the idols, he removed the basin and moved it to another side, [farther] away from the Temple, so as not to intervene between the altar he made and the Temple of Adonoy, and he placed it next to the holy altar on the north. We find that our Rabbis⁶ explained a passage in a similar manner, ”And they came and stood next to the Copper Altar.”⁷ Did the Copper Altar exist? He said to them, ”Begin from the place where they recite songs before Me.” Thus we see here that copper musical instruments are referred to as a “[copper] altar.”⁸

footnotes:
² II Divrei Hayomim 7:7.
³ Alternatively, “he placed the new altar near the Copper Altar, i.e., near Shlomo's altar which is referred to as the Copper Altar. He placed it closer to the Sanctuary than Shlomo's altar without moving that altar to show that it was important to him.—Radak
⁴ Maseches Zevachim 61b.
⁵ See Maseches Sanhedrin 103b.
⁶ Maseches Shabbos 55a.
⁷ Yechezkeil 9:2.
⁸ It follows then, that the copper basin which is an accessory to the altar is referred to as “the Copper Altar.”



### Verse 15

###### Rashi on II Kings 16:15:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/16:15:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/16:15:1)


**לְבַקֵּר.** לִפְרָקִים, לִכְשֶׁיַּעֲלֶה בְּלִבִּי לְבַקְּרוֹ.


**To visit.** At intervals, when it enters my mind to visit it.⁹

footnotes:
⁹ The Copper Altar will be for occasional use, thereby, establishing the new altar as the primary one.



### Verse 17

###### Rashi on II Kings 16:17:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/16:17:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/16:17:1)


**אֶת הַמִּסְגְּרוֹת הַמְּכֹנוֹת.** אִיצְטַבְּלָאוֹת שֶׁהַמְּכוֹנוֹת עֲשׂוּיוֹת עֲלֵיהֶם, כְּמוֹ שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר: וְזֶה מַעֲשֵׂה הַמְּכוֹנוֹת מִסְגְּרוֹת לָהֶם.


**The frames of the bases.** The rows upon which the bases were made,¹⁰ as it is stated, ”The bases were made in the following manner: They had frames … ”¹¹ ¹²

footnotes:
¹⁰ He removed the bases from the ten basins, and out of contempt, he set the basins on a stone floor.—Radak
¹¹ I Melochim 7:28.
¹² See I Melochim 7:23-27.



### Verse 18

###### Rashi on II Kings 16:18:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/16:18:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/16:18:1)


**מוּסַךְ הַשַּׁבָּת.** גַּג אֹהֶל עָשׂוּי לְצֵל, לָשֶׁבֶת תַּחְתָּיו בָּעֲזָרָה בְּיוֹם הַשַּׁבָּת.


**The canopy for Shabbos.** The roof of a tent made for shade,¹³ that had served during the week, would stay until after Shabbos. That too he removed.—Radak to sit under it in the [Temple] courtyard on Shabbos.

footnotes:
¹³ Alternatively, a building in which the mishmar, i.e., the family of kohanim


###### Rashi on II Kings 16:18:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/16:18:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/16:18:2)


**וְאֶת מְבוֹא הַמֶּלֶךְ.** שֶׁהָיָה בָּא בּוֹ מִבֵּיתוֹ לְבֵית ה' דֶּרֶךְ הַחוּץ, הֵסֵב אוֹתוֹ בִּמְקוֹם הֶצְנֵעַ, מִפְּנֵי מֶלֶךְ אַשּׁוּר שֶׁלֹּא יַחֲמֹד אוֹתָן הַכֵּלִים, וְגַם אֶת הַמָּבוֹא הֵסֵב, שֶׁמָּא יִצְטָרֵךְ לְהֵחָבֵא, וְיִבְרַח בְּהֶצְנֵעַ.


**And the king's … entrance.** [The entrance] through which he would come, from his house to the Beis [Hamikdosh] of Adonoy through the outside,¹⁴ he changed to a concealed place because of the king of Ashur, so that he would not covet those vessels, and he also changed the entrance, in case he would have to hide and secretly escape.

footnotes:
¹⁴ He sealed up that entrance so that access to the palace could be gained only through the Beis Hamikdosh. This would make an attack on the palace more difficult because now the only access to the palace would be through the Beis Hamikdosh.—Ralbag



## Chapter 17


### Verse 1

###### Rashi on II Kings 17:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/17:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/17:1:1)


**בִּשְׁנַת שְׁתֵּים עֶשְׂרֵה לְאָחָז וְגוֹ'.** אִי אֶפְשָׁר לוֹמַר שֶׁמָּלַךְ הוֹשֵעַ בִּשְׁנַת שְׁתֵּים עֶשְׂרֵה לְאָחָז, שֶׁהֲרֵי בִּשְׁנַת אַרְבַּע לְאָחָז הָרַג אֶת פֶּקַח וּמָלַךְ תַּחְתָּיו, וּכְשֶׁתִּבְדֹּק בִּשְׁנוֹת פֶּקַח תִּמְצָא כֵּן, וְגַם אִי אֶפְשָׁר לוֹמַר שֶׁלֹּא מָלַךְ אֶלָּא תֵּשַׁע שָׁנִים, שֶׁהֲרֵי מָלַךְ מִשְּׁנַת אַרְבַּע לְאָחָז עַד שְׁנַת שֵׁשׁ לְחִזְקִיָּהוּ שֶׁנִּלְכְּדָה שׁוֹמְרוֹן, הֲרֵי יוֹתֵר מִשֵּׁשׁ עֶשְׂרֵה שָׁנָה, וּמַה תַּלְמוּד לוֹמַר תֵּשַׁע שָׁנִים, מִשֶּׁמָּרַד בְּמֶלֶךְ אַשּׁוּר, כָּךְ שְׁנוּיָה בְּסֵדֶר עוֹלָם. וְאִי אֶפְשָׁר לוֹמַר שֶׁבִּשְׁנַת שְׁתֵּים עֶשְׂרֵה לְאָחָז הִתְחִיל לִמְרֹד, שֶׁאִם כֵּן תִּמְצָא אֶת שְׁנֵי מִרְדּוֹ חָמֵשׁ שָׁנִים בִּימֵי אָחָז, וְשֵׁשׁ שָׁנִים בִּימֵי חִזְקִיָּהוּ הֲרֵי אַחַת עֶשְׂרֵה, וְכָאן הוּא אוֹמֵר תֵּשַׁע, לֹא הֻזְכַּר שְׁנַת שְׁתֵּים עֶשְׂרֵה לְאָחָז אֶלָּא לְעִנְיַן מִקְרָא שֶׁל אַחֲרָיו: עָלָיו עָלָה שַׁלְמַנֶאסֶר וְגוֹ', שֶׁבִּשְׁנַת שְׁתֵּים עֶשְׂרֵה לְאָחָז עָלָה סַנְחֵרִיב עַל הוֹשֵעַ, וְהִגְלָה לְאוֹתָן שֶׁבְּעֵבֶר הַיַּרְדֵּן, וְכָךְ שָׁנִינוּ בְּסֵדֶר עוֹלָם, וּמִקְרָא כָּתוּב בְּדִבְרֵי הַיָּמִים: וַיָּעָר אֱלֹהֵי יִשְׂרָאֵל אֶת רוּחַ פּוּל מֶלֶךְ אַשּׁוּר וַיַּגְלֵם לָראוּבֵנִי וְלַגָּדִי, וְלֹא פֵּרֵשׁ בְּאֵיזֶה זְמַן, וְכָאן פֵּרֵשׁ שֶׁבִּשְׁנַת שְׁתֵּים עֶשְׂרֵה לְאָחָז הָיָה. שָׁלשׁ גָּלֻיּוֹת גָּלוּ עֲשֶׂרֶת הַשְּׁבָטִים, בִּשְׁנַת עֶשְׂרִים לְפֶקַח, כְּמוֹ שֶׁכָּתוּב לְמַעְלָה: בָּא תִּגְלַת פַּלֶאסֶר מֶלֶךְ אַשּׁוּר וַיִּקַּח אֶת עִיּוֹן וְאֶת (דָּן) [אָבֵל] וְגוֹ' כָּל אֶרֶץ נַפְתָּלִי וַיַּגְלֵם אַשּׁוּרָה, וְזֶה הָיָה שְׁנַת הָאַרְבַּע לְאָחָז, שָׁהָה שְׁמֹנֶה שָׁנִים וּבָא עֲלֵיהֶם, שְׁנַת שְׁתֵּים עֶשְׂרֵה לְאָחָז, וְהִגְלָם לָראוּבֵנִי וְלַגָּדִי, וּכְשֶׁרָאָה הוֹשֵׁעַ בֶּן אֵלָה מָרַד בּוֹ וְשָׁלַח מַלְאָכִים אֶל סוֹא מֶלֶךְ מִצְרַיִם, שָׁהָה שְׁמוֹנֶה שָׁנִים, וּבָא וְצָר עַל שׁוֹמְרוֹן, וְנִלְכְּדָה לְקֵץ שָׁלשׁ שָׁנִים, וְגָלוּ כֻּלָּן, וְזֶהוּ שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר: כָּעֵת הָרִאשׁוֹן הֵקַל אַרְצָה זְבֻלוּן וְאַרְצָה נַפְתָּלִי, הֵקַל אַף בַּשְּׁנִיָּה, שֶׁלֹּא הִגְלָה אֶלָּא שְׁנֵי הַשְּׁבָטִים, אֲבָל הָאַחֲרוֹן הִכְבִּיד, טִיאֵט אֶת הַכֹּל, כָּזֶה שֶׁמְּכַבֵּד אֶת הַבַּיִת. וּמִכָּל מָקוֹם לְפִי הַחֶשְׁבּוֹן שֶׁכָּתַב כָּאן אֶת מִנְיַן שְׁנֵי מִרְדּוֹ תֵּשַׁע שָׁנִים, לֹא מָרַד בּוֹ עַד שְׁנַת אַרְבַּע עֶשְׂרֵה לְאָחָז, שֶׁהֲרֵי אָחָז מָלַךְ שֵׁשׁ עֶשְׂרֵה, שָׁלשׁ שָׁנִים שֶׁל אָחָז הָיָה הַמֶּרֶד, וְשֵׁשׁ שָׁנִים בִּימֵי חִזְקִיָּהוּ.


**In the twelfth year of Achaz, etc.** It is impossible to say that Hoshei'a became king in the twelfth year of Achaz, because in the fourth year of Achaz, he assassinated Pekach and reigned in his place,¹ and when you examine Pekach's years you will find it so. Likewise, it is impossible to say that he reigned for only nine years, for he reigned from the fourth year of Achaz until the sixth year of Chizkiyohu, when Shomron was conquered, and this amounts to more than sixteen years. What then is the meaning of “nine years”? [It was nine years] since he rebelled against the king of Ashur. This is what is learned in Seder Olam. And it is impossible to say that in the twelfth year of Achaz he started to rebel, for if so, you will find the years of his revolt five years during the days of Achaz and six years during the days of Chizkiyohu, making [a total of] eleven years, yet here he states [that he reigned] nine [years]. ”The twelfth year of Achaz” was mentioned only in connection with the verse following, ”Shalmaneser [king Ashur] went up against him, etc.”² [i.e.,] that in the twelfth year of Achaz, Sancheiriv went up against Hoshei'a and exiled those who were on the eastern bank of the Yardein. Thus we learned in Seder Olam. And a verse is written in Divrei Hayomim, ”Adonoy of Yisroel aroused Pul king of Ashur … and he exiled the people of Reuvein and the people of Gad,”³ and it did not explain during which time frame. But here it explains that it occurred in the twelfth year of Achaz['s reign]. The ten tribes were exiled at three different times: [The first exile was] in the twentieth year of Pekach, as it is written above, ”Tiglas Pileser the king of Ashur came and took Iyon and Dan, etc., and the entire land of Naphtoli and exiled them to Ashur.”⁴ Now this was the fourth year of Achaz. [The second exile,] he waited eight years and came upon them in the twelfth year of Achaz, and exiled the members of Reuvein and the Gad. But, when Hoshei'a son of Eilah saw this, he revolted against him and sent messengers to So the king of Egypt.⁵ [And the third exile] he waited eight years and came and laid siege on Shomron. After three years it was conquered, and everyone was exiled. This is [the meaning of] what is written, ”Like the first time, he dealt mildly, the land of Zevulun and the land of Naphtoli,”⁶ so did he deal mildly⁷ also in the second [exile], for he exiled but two tribes. But, “in the last one, he swept,” [i.e.,] he swept everything out like one who sweeps a house. In any case, according to the amount he wrote here as the number of years of his revolt, [i.e.,] nine years, he did not revolt against him until the fourteenth year of Achaz, for Achaz reigned sixteen years; the revolt was during three years of Achaz['s reign] and six years during the days of Chizkiyohu['s reign].

footnotes:
¹ See above, 15:30.
² Below, v. 3.
³ According to Rashi, the nine years mentioned in this verse refer to the total length of Hoshei'a's revolt, which began in the fourteenth year of Achaz.
⁴ I Divrei Hayomim 5:26.
⁵ See above, 15:29. In our editions, however, Dan is not mentioned in the verse.
⁶ Yeshayohu 8:23.
⁷ By exiling only two tribes.



### Verse 2

###### Rashi on II Kings 17:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/17:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/17:2:1)


**רַק לֹא כְּמַלְכֵי יִשְׂרָאֵל.** שֶׁבִּטֵּל פַּרְדֵיסָאוֹת שֶׁיָּשְׁבוּ מִימֵי יָרָבְעָם עַל הַדְּרָכִים, לִשְׁמֹר שֶׁלֹּא יַעֲלוּ יִשְׂרָאֵל לָרֶגֶל, וְזֶה בִּטְּלָן, לְפִי שֶׁגָּלוּ עֶגְלֵי הַזָּהָב כְּבָר בִּשְׁתֵּי גָּלֻיּוֹת, בְּגָלוּת רִאשׁוֹנָה, וַיִּקַּח אֶת עִיּוֹן וְאֶת דָּן, הִגְלָה עֵגֶל שֶׁבְּדָן, וּבַשְּׁנִיָּה כְּשֶׁגָּלוּ רְאוּבֵן וְגָד, נָטַל עֵגֶל שֶׁבְּבֵית אֵל, לְקַיֵּם מַה שֶּׁנֶּאֱמַר: גַּם אוֹתוֹ לְאַשּׁוּר יוּבָל. וּלְפִי שֶׁבִּטְּלוּ פַּרְדֵּיסָאוֹת וְהֵם נִמְנְעוּ מִלַּעֲלוֹת לָרֶגֶל, לְפִיכָךְ נֶחְתַּם גְּזַר דִּינָם לִגְלוֹת בְּיָמָיו, שֶׁעַד עַכְשָׁיו תָּלוּ הַקַּלְקָלָה בְּמַלְכֵיהֶם, וְעַתָּה לֹא הָיָה בְּמִי לִתְלוֹת, וְזֶהוּ שֶׁאָמַר הוֹשֵׁעַ בֶּן בְּאֵרִי: כִּי עַתָּה הִזְנֵיתָ אֶפְרַיִם נִטְמָא יִשְׂרָאֵל, עַתָּה נִגְלֵית רָעַתְכֶם.


**But not like the kings of Yisroel.** For he abolished the sentries who were stationed on the roads from the days of Yorovom, to guard against the [Bnei] Yisroel from going up and performing the pilgrimage for the festivals, and this one abolished them,⁸ because the golden calves had already been exiled in the two [previous] exiles. In the first exile, [it states,] “he took Iyon and Dan,”⁹ [i.e.,] he exiled the calf that was in Dan. And in the second [exile], when Reuvein and Gad were exiled, he took the calf that was in Beis Eil, to fulfill what was stated, ”That too, will be transported to Ashur.”¹⁰ And because the sentries were abolished, and they [nonetheless] refrained from going up and performing the pilgrimage for the festivals, therefore, their verdict to be exiled in his days was sealed, for until now, they had blamed the corruption on their kings, but now they had no one to blame. This is what Hoshei'a the son of Be'eiri said, ”For now you have gone astray, Ephrayim, Yisroel has become defiled.”¹¹ [i.e.,] now your evil has been revealed.

footnotes:
⁸ He abolished the sentries on the fifteenth day of Menachem Av, and therefore that day was proclaimed a minor holiday. See Maseches Taanis 30b.—Radak Hoshei'a 10:6.
⁹ See above 15:29.
¹⁰ Hoshei'a 10:6.
¹¹ Ibid 5:3. See Rashi in Maseches Gittin 88a.



### Verse 6

###### Rashi on II Kings 17:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/17:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/17:6:1)


**בִּשְׁנַת הַתְּשִׁעִית.** לְמִרְדּוֹ שֶל הוֹשֵׁעַ.


**In the ninth year.** Of Hoshei'a's revolt.


###### Rashi on II Kings 17:6:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/17:6:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/17:6:2)


**וַיֶּגֶל אֶת יִשְׂרָאֵל.** וַיַּגְלֶה אֶת יִשְׂרָאֵל, וְזֶה שִׁעוּר כָּל תֵּבָה שֶׁפֹּעַל שֶׁלָּהּ בְּהֵ"א, כְּגוֹן: 'פָּנָה' 'בָּנָה' 'גָּלָה' 'זָנָה' 'רָבָה', כְּשֶׁבָּא לָתֵת בְּרֹאשׁ הַתֵּבָה וָא"ו יוֹ"ד, אִם הַמִּלָּה מוּסֶבֶת עַל הַפֹּעַל, תְּהֵא הַיּוֹ"ד נְקוּדָה בְּחִירִ"ק, כְּגוֹן: וַיִּבֶן שָׁם מִזְבֵּחַ; וַיִּפֶן וַיֵּרֶד משֶׁה: וַיִּגֶל יְהוּדָה מֵעַל אַדְמָתוֹ: וַתֵּלֶךְ וַתִּזֶן גַּם הִיא: וַיִּרֶב הָעָם וַיַּעַצְמוּ מְאֹד. וְאִם הוּא מְדַבֵּר בִּלְשׁוֹן מַפְעִיל, תְּהֵא הַיּוֹ"ד נְקוּדָה בְּסֶגוֹ"ל, כְּגוֹן: וַיֶּפֶן זָנָב אֶל זָנָב: וַיֶּגֶל אֶת יִשְׂרָאֵל אַשּׁוּרָה: וַיֶּזֶן אֶת יוֹשְׁבֵי יְרוּשָׁלַיִם: וַיֶּרֶב בְּבַת יְהוּדָה תַּאֲנִיָּה וַאֲנִיָּה.


**And exiled Yisroel.** [ויגל is the same as] ויגלה [=and he exiled] Yisroel. This is the rule for every word whose verb root [ends] with a `ה', e.g., פנה[=to face], בנה [=to build], גלה [=to go into exile], זנה [=to commit adultery], רבה [=to multiply]; when one comes to add a prefix to the word with `ו ' or a `י', if the word applies to the doer, the `י' will be voweled with a “chirik,” e.g., “And he built [=ויבן] an altar there;”¹² ”And Moshe turned [=ויפן] and descended;”¹³ ”And Yisroel was exiled [=ויגל] off its land;”¹⁴ ”And she too went and committed whoredom[=ותזן];”¹⁵ ”And the people increased [=וירב] and became very strong.”¹⁶ If however, one is talking in the causative conjugation, the `י' will be voweled with a “segol,” e.g., “And turned [=ויפן] tail to tail;”¹⁷ ”And exiled [=ויגל] the Israelites to Ashur;”¹⁸ ”And he caused the dwellers of Yerusholayim to go astray [=ויזן];”¹⁹ ”And He increased [=וירב] among the people of Yehudah grief and lamentation.”²⁰

footnotes:
¹² Bereishis 12:7.
¹³ Shemos 32:15.
¹⁴ Below 25:21.
¹⁵ Yirmiyahu 3:8.
¹⁶ Shemos 1:20.
¹⁷ Shoftim 15:4.
¹⁸ Below, 18:11.
¹⁹ II Divrei Hayomim 21:11.
²⁰ Eichah 2:5.



### Verse 9

###### Rashi on II Kings 17:9:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/17:9:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/17:9:1)


**וַיְחַפְּאוּ.** בָּדְאוּ, פִּתְרוֹנוֹ לְפִי עִנְיָנוֹ.


**[Bnei Yisroel] fabricated.** They concealed;²¹ this interpretation is according to its context.

footnotes:
²¹ Alternatively, “Bnei Yisroel said.”—Targum Yonoson Or, “Bnei Yisroel claimed that God did not see them.”—Radak


###### Rashi on II Kings 17:9:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/17:9:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/17:9:2)


**מִמִּגְדַּל נוֹצְרִים.** שֶׁאֵינוֹ עָשׂוּי אֶלָּא לְהַעֲמִיד צוֹפֶה לִנְצֹר, בְּכָל בַּיִת גָּבוֹהַּ הֶעֱמִידוּ עֲבוֹדַת גִּלּוּלִים.


**From watchtower.** Which is made only to station a lookout to watch; they now erected idols in every high house.²²

footnotes:
²² They denied God's Providence and adopted a new doctrine. They said that God did not see them because He delegated the heavenly bodies to supervise over the earth. Therefore they erected idols in high places honoring these heavenly bodies.—Ralbag


###### Rashi on II Kings 17:9:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/17:9:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/17:9:3)


**עַד עִיר מִבְצָר.** כְּמוֹ מִקָּטֹן וְעַד גָּדוֹל.


**To fortified city.** Like “from small to big.”



### Verse 13

###### Rashi on II Kings 17:13:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/17:13:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/17:13:1)


**וַיָּעַד ה'.** הִתְרָה בָּהֶם.


**And Adonoy warned.** He warned [=ויעד] them.²³

footnotes:
²³ As in “I warn [=העדותי] you today,” in Devarim 8:19.


###### Rashi on II Kings 17:13:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/17:13:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/17:13:2)


**נְבִיאֵי כָל חֹזֶה.** נְבִיאֵי כָּל חָזוֹן, יֵשׁ חוֹזֶה שֶׁהוּא שֵׁם דָּבָר, כְּמוֹ: וְעִם שְׁאוֹל עָשִׂינוּ חוֹזֶה.


**Prophets of all visions.** Prophets of all visions.²⁴ There is חוזה that is a noun, such as, ”And with the grave have we made a boundary [=חוזה].”²⁵

footnotes:
²⁴ Alternatively, נביא and חוזה are two different types of prophets. Adonoy נביא perceives prophecy through dreams; and a חוזה [=seer] is one who perceives prophecy in a vision, which is a higher form of prophecy.—Ralbag
²⁵ Yeshayohu 28:15.



### Verse 16

###### Rashi on II Kings 17:16:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/17:16:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/17:16:1)


**שְׁנֵי עֲגָלִים.** בְּבֵית אֵל וּבְדָן.


**Two molten calves.** In Beis Eil and in Dan.


###### Rashi on II Kings 17:16:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/17:16:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/17:16:2)


**אֶת הַבָּעַל.** עֲבוֹדָה זָרָה שֶׁשְּׁמָהּ בַּעַל.


**The Baal.** A pagan deity named Baal.



### Verse 17

###### Rashi on II Kings 17:17:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/17:17:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/17:17:1)


**וַיִּתְמַכְּרוּ.** מָכְרוּ עַצְמָן לְכָךְ.


**And they devoted themselves.** They sold themselves for that [purpose].



### Verse 18

###### Rashi on II Kings 17:18:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/17:18:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/17:18:1)


**וַיְסִרֵם מֵעַל פָּנָיו.** שֶׁגָּלוּ עִם הוֹשֵׁעַ בֶּן אֵלָה.


**And He banished them from His Presence.** For they were exiled with Hoshei'a son of Eiloh.²⁶

footnotes:
²⁶ I.e., He exiled them from His land.”—Metzudas Dovid



### Verse 23

###### Rashi on II Kings 17:23:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/17:23:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/17:23:1)


**וַיִּגֶל יִשְׂרָאֵל.** זֶה נָקוּד בְּחִירִ"ק, שֶׁהוּא לְשׁוֹן גָּלָה, וְאֵינוֹ לְשׁוֹן הִגְלָה.


**And Yisroel went into exile.** This is voweled with a “chirik,” which is an expression of “went in exile.” It is not an expression of “was exiled.”²⁷

footnotes:
²⁷ See Rashi above, v. 6.



### Verse 25

###### Rashi on II Kings 17:25:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/17:25:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/17:25:1)


**לֹא יָרְאוּ אֶת ה'.** אַף עַל פִּי שֶׁהָעַמִּים עוֹבְדֵי גִּלּוּלִים הֵם יְרֵאִים מִפָּנָיו, וְקָרוּ לֵהּ אֱלָהָא דֶּאֱלָהַיָּא, כְּעִנְיָן שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר: וּשְׁמִי נוֹרָא בַגּוֹיִם, וְאֵלּוּ לֹא יְרֵאוּנִי, כִּי אָמְרוּ אִלּוּ הָיְתָה בּוֹ יִרְאָה לֹא נָתַן עַמּוֹ לְגָלוּת.


**They did not fear Adonoy.** Even though the pagan nations fear Him and call him the God of the gods, in the manner as it is stated, ”And My Name is feared among the nations,”²⁸ but these did not fear Me, for they said, “If He were worthy of fear, He would not have allowed for His people to be exiled.”

footnotes:
²⁸ Malachi 1:14.



### Verse 29

###### Rashi on II Kings 17:29:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/17:29:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/17:29:1)


**אֲשֶׁר עָשׂוּ הַשֹּׁמְרֹנִים.** יִשְׂרָאֵל בְּעוֹדָם שָׁם.


**That the citizens of Shomron had made.** [I.e.,] the [Bnei] Yisroel while they were still there.


###### Rashi on II Kings 17:29:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/17:29:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/17:29:2)


**גּוֹי גּוֹי אֱלֹהָיו.** לְאֻמָּה וְאֻמָּה עֲבוֹדַת גִּלּוּלִים שֶׁהָיוּ עוֹבְדִים בְּאַרְצָם, אַף כָּאן עָשׂוּ כֵּן כָּל גּוֹיֵי הָעִיר אֲשֶׁר הוֹרִישָם סַנְחֵרִיב שָׁם בְּעָרֵי יִשְׂרָאֵל.


**Each nation … its own god.** [I.e.,] each nation, [placed] the deity they had worshiped in their land, here, too, each nation did so in the city which Sancheiriv had given them as a possession there in the cities of Yisroel.



### Verse 30

###### Rashi on II Kings 17:30:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/17:30:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/17:30:1)


**סֻכּוֹת בְּנוֹת.** דְּמוּת תַּרְנְגֹלֶת עִם אֶפְרוֹחֶיהָ.


**Succos Benos.** [This idol was in] the form of a hen and her chicks.²⁹

footnotes:
²⁹ סכות stems from שכוי, being that a ‘ש’ and a ‘ס’ are interchangeable. The Gemara in Maseches Rosh Hashanah 26a cites a region where ‘שכוי’ is ‘a rooster.’ It follows then that ‘שכות’ or ‘סכות’ is a hen. Thus, סכות בנות is “a hen and her chicks.”—See Radak


###### Rashi on II Kings 17:30:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/17:30:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/17:30:2)


**נֵרְגַל.** דְּמוּת תַּרְנְגוֹל.


**Neirgal.** [This idol was in] the form of a rooster.³⁰

footnotes:
³⁰ See Maseches Sanhedrin 63b.


###### Rashi on II Kings 17:30:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/17:30:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/17:30:3)


**אֲשִׁימָא.** דְּמוּת תַּיִשׁ. וְכֵן קְרוּיִין בִּלְשׁוֹן כָּל אֻמָּה וְאֻמָּה, וְכֵן פֵּרְשׁוּ רַבּוֹתֵינוּ בְּסַנְהֶדְרִין.


**Ashimo.** [This idol was in] the form of a goat.³¹ They are called [these names] in the language of each nation, and so did our Rabbis explain in [Maseches] Sanhedrin.³²

footnotes:
³¹ אשימא stems from שמם [=desolation], and a goat has no wool is considered “desolate.” Or, אשימא stems from אשם [=guilt offering], for which a sheep is used.—Radak
³² 63b.



### Verse 31

###### Rashi on II Kings 17:31:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/17:31:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/17:31:1)


**נִבְחַז.** דְּמוּת כֶּלֶב.


**Nivchaz.** [This idol was in] the form of a dog.³³

footnotes:
³³ נבחז stems from נבח [=bark], as in “they cannot bark [=לנבח],” in Yeshayohu 56:10; נבחז is a contraction of נבח and חז, i.e. when a dog barks [=נבח] it shows [=חז] its teeth.—Radak


###### Rashi on II Kings 17:31:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/17:31:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/17:31:2)


**תַּרְתָּק.** דְּמוּת חֲמוֹר.


**Tartok.** [This idol was in] the form of a donkey.


###### Rashi on II Kings 17:31:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/17:31:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/17:31:3)


**לְאַדְרַמֶּלֶךְ.** דְּמוּת פֶּרֶד.


**To Adramelech.** [This idol was in] the form of a mule.³⁴

³⁴ אדרמלת is a contraction of דאדר ליה למריה [= it honors its master] by carrying his burdens.—Radak


###### Rashi on II Kings 17:31:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/17:31:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/17:31:4)


**וַעֲנַמֶּלֶךְ.** דְּמוּת סוּס.


**Anamelech.** [This idol was in] the form of a horse.³⁵

footnotes:
³⁵ ענמלת is a contraction of דעני ליה למריה בקרבא [= it responds to its master in battle].—Radak



### Verse 33

###### Rashi on II Kings 17:33:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/17:33:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/17:33:1)


**כְּמִשְׁפַּט הַגּוֹיִם אֲשֶׁר הִגְלוּ אֹתָם מִשָּׁם.** אוֹתָם עֲבוֹדַת כּוֹכָבִים וּמַזָּלוֹת שֶׁעוֹבְדִים הַגּוֹיִם אֲשֶׁר הִגְלָן סַנְחֵרִיב וְאֻכְלוּסָיו (אֶת אֵלּוּ) מִשָּׁם, הָיוּ אֵלּוּ עוֹבְדִין כָּאן.


**As was the custom of the nations from which they had been exiled.** Those deities that are worshiped by the nations whom Sancheiriv and his armies had exiled (these) from there, they would worship here.³⁶

footnotes:
³⁶ Alternatively, כמשפט הגוים refers to some of the Bnei Yisroel who also worshiped idols while worshiping God.—Radak



### Verse 34

###### Rashi on II Kings 17:34:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/17:34:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/17:34:1)


**אֵינָם יְרֵאִים אֶת ה'.** יִרְאָה שְׁלֵמָה כְּמִשְׁפַּט יִשְׂרָאֵל, וְאַף עַל פִּי שֶׁנִּתְגַּיְּרוּ מִיִּרְאַת הָאֲרָיוֹת, אֵין יִרְאָתָם אֶת ה' יִרְאָה שְׁלֵמָה (כְּמִשְׁפַּט יִשְׂרָאֵל), כְּמוֹ שֶׁמְּפָרֵשׁ וְהוֹלֵךְ, שֶׁאֵינָם עוֹסְקִים בְּתוֹרָה וּבְמִצְוֹת אֲשֶׁר צִוָּה ה' אֶת בְּנֵי יַעֲקֹב, וְאֵינָם עוֹשִׂים כְּחֻקּוֹתָם וּכְמִשְׁפָּטָם שֶׁהָיָה עֲלֵיהֶם לַעֲשׂוֹת מִשֶּׁנִּתְגַּיְּרוּ, אֶלָּא כְּמוֹ שֶׁהוֹרָם הַכֹּהֵן, שֶׁהָיָה מִן הַשּׁוֹמְרוֹנִים, שֶׁהָיוּ עוֹבְדִים עֲבוֹדָה זָרָה.


**They do not fear Adonoy.** A complete fear as is the custom of the [Bnei] Yisroel. And although they converted out of fear of the lions, [nonetheless,] their fear of Adonoy was not a complete fear, (like [Bnei] Yisroel's custom) as it goes on to elaborate, that they were not engaged in Torah [study] and [in performing] the commandments which God commanded the Bnei Yaakov, and they do not practice according to their statutes and according to their law, which they are obligated to practice once they converted, but as the priest who was of the people of Shomron, instructed them.³⁷—who were idolatrous,

footnotes:
³⁷ Alternatively, the verse refers to the exiled Bnei Yisroel who “until this day” have not repented from the idolatrous practices, but continue to combine the worship of God with the worship of idols as they had practiced in Shomron.—Radak



### Verse 35

###### Rashi on II Kings 17:35:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/17:35:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/17:35:1)


**וַיִּכְרֹת ה' אִתָּם בְּרִית.** כְּשֶׁנָּתַן לָהֶם תּוֹרָה בְּהַר סִינַי.


**And Adonoy enacted a covenant with them.** When He gave them the Torah at Mount Sinai.



## Chapter 18


### Verse 1

###### Rashi on II Kings 18:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/18:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/18:1:1)


**בִּשְׁנַת שָׁלשׁ.** לְמִרְדּוֹ שֶל הוֹשֵׁעַ.


**In the third year.** Of Hoshei'a's rebellion.¹ ²

footnotes:
¹ See above, 17:1, and Rashi there.
² I.e., in the third year of Hoshei'a's rebellion against Ashur and declaring his independence, Chizkiyoh became king.—Metzudas Dovid



### Verse 4

###### Rashi on II Kings 18:4:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/18:4:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/18:4:1)


**וַיִּקְרָא לוֹ נְחֻשְׁתָּן.** לְשׁוֹן בִּזָּיוֹן, כְּלוֹמַר מַה צֹּרֶךְ בָּזֶה, אֵינוֹ אֶלָּא נְחַשׁ נְחשֶׁת.


**And he called it Nechushton.** A derogatory expression,³ as though to say, “Why is this necessary?⁴ It is nothing but a copper serpent.”⁵

footnotes:
³ The additional “nun” to the word נחשת serves to belittle and mock the subject. See II Shmuel 13:20.
⁴ The copper serpent was not destroyed before now because it was incorrectly believed that one may not destroy any object that was made as a result of a Divine command. The serpent was originally made to encourage the Bnei Yisroel to pray to God after they were bitten by poisonous snakes. See Bamidbar 21:4-10 and Tosfos in Maseches Chulin 7a. It is also possible that they kept the copper serpent as a remembrance of the great miracle that occurred, just as they kept the jar of manna, in Shemos 16:33-35.
⁵ Alternatively, the Bnei Yisroel who were worshiping it called it the Nechushton.—Targum



### Verse 7

###### Rashi on II Kings 18:7:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/18:7:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/18:7:1)


**יַשְׂכִּיל.** יַצְלִיחַ.


**He succeeded.** He succeeded.⁶ ⁷

footnotes:
⁶ ישכיל stems from שכל [=intelligence]. Adonoy successful person is conceived by others as one who acts with intelligence.—Metzudas Dovid
⁷ As in, “Dovid was successful [=משכיל] in all his ways,” in I Shmuel 18:14.



### Verse 16

###### Rashi on II Kings 18:16:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/18:16:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/18:16:1)


**קִצַּץ.** אֶת הַדְּלָתוֹת שֶׁהָיָה שֶל זָהָב.


**Removed.** The doors, which were gold [plated].


###### Rashi on II Kings 18:16:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/18:16:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/18:16:2)


**וְאֶת הָאֹמְנוֹת.** תִּרְגֵּם יוֹנָתָן: וְיַת סְקוֹפַיָּא, אִיסְקוּפוֹת.


**And the thresholds.** [Targum] Yonoson rendered the סקופיא, [i.e.,] the thresholds.⁸

footnotes:
⁸ Alternately, אמנות are pillars, or it is a term used to include both the threshold and the lintel.—Ralbag



### Verse 17

###### Rashi on II Kings 18:17:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/18:17:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/18:17:1)


**אֶת תַּרְתָּן וְאֶת רַב סָרִיס.** לָמַדְנוּ בְּסֵדֶר עוֹלָם, שֶׁלֹּא בָאוּ שְׁלָשְׁתָּם יַחַד, אֶלָּא רַבְשָׁקֵה לְבַדּוֹ, כְּמוֹ שֶׁכָּתַב בְּסֵפֶר יְשַׁעְיָהוּ וְתַרְתָּן וְרַב סָרִיס בָּאוּ בִּשְׁלִיחוּת שְׁנִיָּה, כְּשֶׁבָּאָה לוֹ שְׁמוּעָה עַל תִּרְהָקָה מֶלֶךְ כּוּשׁ, וַיִּשְׁמַע וַיִּשְׁלַח מַלְאָכִים אֶל חִזְקִיָּהוּ וְגוֹ'.


**Tarton [and] Rav Soris.** We learned in Seder Olam that the three of them did not come together,⁹ rather but Ravshokei [came] alone, as it is written in the Book of Yeshayohu,¹⁰ and Tarton and Rav Soris came on a second mission, when he received word concerning Tirhokoh king of Cush, ”and he heard and sent emissaries to Chizkiyohu … ”¹¹

footnotes:
⁹ The king of Ashur dealt falsely with Chizkiyoh because he sent his officers to attack Yerusholayim even though Chizkiyoh paid the tribute that was imposed upon him.—Metzudas Dovid Or, Sancheiriv took the tribute and left. However, in the ensuing years Chizkiyohu failed to meet his demands and he therefore attacked again.—Radak
¹⁰ 36:2.
¹¹ Yeshayohu 37:9.


###### Rashi on II Kings 18:17:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/18:17:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/18:17:2)


**בִּתְעָלַת.** פוסקיר"ה בְּלַעַ"ז, בְּרֵכָה שֶׁעוֹשִׂין לְבֵיבְרֵי דָגִים.


**Near the channel.** *Fosed*, in O.F., [i.e.,] a moat made for schools of fish.


###### Rashi on II Kings 18:17:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/18:17:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/18:17:3)


**שְׂדֵה כוֹבֵס.** שָׂדֶה שֶׁהַכּוֹבְסִין שׁוֹטְחִין בּוֹ בְּגָדִים, וְכֵן תִּרְגֵּם יוֹנָתָן: חֲקָל מַשְׁטַח קַצְרַיָּא.


**The washer's field.** A field in which the launderers spread out garments, And so did [Targum] Yonoson render, ”a field where the launderers spread out.”¹²

footnotes:
¹² I.e., where the launderers spread out the clothing.



### Verse 18

###### Rashi on II Kings 18:18:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/18:18:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/18:18:1)


**הַמַּזְכִּיר.** אֵיזֶה מִשְׁפָּט בָּא לִפְנֵי הַמֶּלֶךְ רִאשׁוֹן, שֶׁיִּפְסְקֶנּוּ רִאשׁוֹן.


**The recorder.** [Who determines] which judgment came first before the king, that it be adjudicated first.¹³

footnotes:
¹³ Rashi in Yeshayohu 36:3 defines מזכיר, as “the writer of the records in the annals.”



### Verse 20

###### Rashi on II Kings 18:20:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/18:20:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/18:20:1)


**אָמַרְתָּ אַךְ דְּבַר שְׂפָתַיִם וְגוֹ'.** אָמַרְתָּ עַד הֵנָּה לֹא אֶעֱבֹד לְמֶלֶךְ אַשּׁוּר, אַךְ דְּבַר שְׂפָתַיִם הָיָה, כָּל זְמַן שֶׁלֹּא יֵצֵא מִמְּקוֹמוֹ לָבֹא עָלֶיךָ, לֹא הֻצְרַכְתָּ לְעֵצָה וּגְבוּרָה, אֲבָל עַכְשָׁיו שֶׁיָּצָא וּבָא, עֵצָה וּגְבוּרָה אַתָּה צָרִיךְ לַמִּלְחָמָה הַזֹּאת, עַתָּה אֱמֹר עַל מִי בָטַחְתָּ.


**You have said but words of the lips, etc.** Until now you have said, ”I will (not) pay tribute to the king of Ashur.” It was but words of the lips, as long as he did not leave his place to march upon you, you did not require counsel and might. But now, that he has left [his place] and marched [upon you], you require counsel and might for this war. Now, tell [me] upon whom you have depended?



### Verse 21

###### Rashi on II Kings 18:21:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/18:21:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/18:21:1)


**וּבָא בְּכַפּוֹ.** כְּשֶׁאָדָם נִסְמָךְ עַל מִשְׁעֶנֶת קָנֶה רָצוּץ שֶׁנִּשְׁבַּר בְּיָדוֹ, בָּאִין רָאשֵׁי הַקְּרוּמִיּוֹת וְנוֹקְבִין אֶת כַּפּוֹ.


**It will go into his palm.** When a person supports himself on a support of a splintered reed, which has broken in his hand, the splintered edges come and puncture his palm.¹⁴

footnotes:
¹⁴ Similarly, his reliance on Paroh for support will turn out to be bad for him.



### Verse 22

###### Rashi on II Kings 18:22:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/18:22:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/18:22:1)


**הֲלוֹא הוּא אֲשֶׁר הֵסִיר חִזְקִיָּהוּ וְגוֹ'.** יֵשׁ לִלְמֹד מִכָּאן, דְּרַבְשָׁקֵה יִשְׂרָאֵל מוּמָר הָיָה, שֶׁהָיָה מוֹדֶה שֶׁהַקָּבָּ"ה אֱלוֹהַּ, אֶלָּא שֶׁרְצוֹנוֹ לַעֲבֹד עֲבוֹדָה זָרָה.


**Is He not the one Whose high places and altars Chizkiyohu has removed, etc.** It can be deduced from here that Ravshokei was an apostate Jew, for he admitted that the Holy One, Blessed Is He, is God, but his desire is to worship idols.¹⁵

footnotes:
¹⁵ See Maseches Sanhedrin 60a.



### Verse 23

###### Rashi on II Kings 18:23:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/18:23:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/18:23:1)


**הִתְעָרֶב נָא.** הִתְפָּאֵר עָלָיו, בּוֹא בְּעֵרָבוֹן, עַל מְנָת אִם תּוּכַל לִתֵּן לְךָ רוֹכְבִים עַל אַלְפַּיִם סוּסִים.


**Provide a security.** Glorify yourself over him; enter a wager on the condition that if you are able to supply riders for two thousand horses.¹⁶

footnotes:
¹⁶ Ravshokei implied that Chizkiyohu did not even have enough riders for the two thousand horses, so surely he is unable to mount a rebellion.—Ralbag


###### Rashi on II Kings 18:23:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/18:23:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/18:23:2)


**הִתְעָרֶב.** גיי"ר בְּלַעַ"ז.


**Wager.** *Gajer*, in O.F.



### Verse 24

###### Rashi on II Kings 18:24:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/18:24:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/18:24:1)


**וְאֵיךְ תָּשִׁיב אֵת פְּנֵי פַחַת אַחַד עַבְדֵי אֲדֹנִי.** שֶׁהֲרֵי כָּל הַקָּטָן שֶׁבָּהֶם, שַׂר עַל אַלְפַּיִם אִישׁ, וְאַתָּה אֵינְךָ מַשִּׂיג לְאַלְפַּיִם אִישׁ, לָמַדְנוּ מִכָּאן עַל רָאשֵׁי גְּיָסוֹת מֵאָה וּשְׁמוֹנִים וַחֲמִשָּׁה אֶלֶף שֶׁנָּפְלוּ עִם סַנְחֵרִיב, הַקָּטָן שֶׁבָּהֶם, שַׂר עַל אַלְפַּיִם אִישׁ שֶׁעִמּוֹ.


**How can you turn away one captain of my master's servants.** For the smallest of them is captain over two thousand men, and you cannot obtain two thousand men. We deduce from here concerning the heads of the armies, who totaled one hundred and eighty-five thousand and fell with Sancheiriv, that the smallest of them was captain over two thousand men who were with him.



### Verse 25

###### Rashi on II Kings 18:25:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/18:25:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/18:25:1)


**הֲמִבַּלְעֲדֵי ה'.** וְכִי שֶׁלֹּא בִּרְשׁוּת בָּאתִי, כְּבָר נִתְנַבֵּא יְשַׁעְיָה: בִּימֵי אָחָז אָבִיךָ יָבִיא ה' עָלֶיךָ וְעַל עַמְּךָ וְגוֹ' אֶת מֶלֶךְ אַשּׁוּר.


**Is it with other than Adonoy.** Did I come without permission? Yeshayohu already prophesied in the days of Achaz your father, ”Adonoy shall bring upon you and upon your people, etc., the king of Ashur.”¹⁷

footnotes:
¹⁷ Yeshayohu 7:17. Radak cites another verse from Yeshayohu's prophecy, “and therefore the LRD is bringing upon them the mighty and abundant waters of the river, the king of Ashur, etc.,” in Yeshayohu 8:7. Sancheiriv therefore believed that he was acting with Divine approval.


###### Rashi on II Kings 18:25:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/18:25:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/18:25:2)


**וְהַשְׁחִיתָהּ.** אֶת זוֹ הוֹסִיף מִשֶּׁלּוֹ.


**And to destroy it.** This he added of his own accord.



### Verse 26

###### Rashi on II Kings 18:26:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/18:26:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/18:26:1)


**כִּי שֹׁמְעִים אֲנָחְנוּ.** אָנוּ בְּנֵי פַּלָּטִין, וּמַכִּירִין בְּלָשׁוֹן אֲרַמִּית וּבִשְׁאָר לְשׁוֹנוֹת, וְאַל תְּדַבֵּר אֵלֵינוּ יְהוּדִית, שֶׁכָּל הָעָם מַכִּירִין בּוֹ, וְאֵין אָנוּ רוֹצִים שֶׁיִּשְׁמְעוּ.


**For we understand it.** We are residents of the palace, and understand Aramaic and other languages. But do not speak Hebrew to us, which all the people understand,¹⁸ and we do not want them to hear.¹⁹

footnotes:
¹⁸ They were concerned about the morale of the people and therefore wanted to keep the conversation private.
¹⁹ It is not proper to reveal matters of war to the general public.—Metzudas Dovid



### Verse 27

###### Rashi on II Kings 18:27:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/18:27:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/18:27:1)


**לֶאֱכֹל אֶת צוֹאָתָם.** בְּרָעָב הַמָּצוֹר.


**To eat their own excrement.** In the hunger [caused] by the siege.²⁰

footnotes:
²⁰ If they continue guarding the wall to prevent Ashur's army from entering, they will lay a prolonged siege to the city that will be so severe that the people will starve during the siege, causing them to “eat their own, etc.”—Ralbag


###### Rashi on II Kings 18:27:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/18:27:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/18:27:2)


**חוֹרֵיהֶם.** רְעִי הַיּוֹצֵא דֶּרֶךְ חוֹר פִּי הַטַּבַּעַת. וְרַבּוֹתֵינוּ פֵּרְשׁוּ: לְכַנּוֹתָם לָשׁוֹן נָאָה צוֹאָתָם.


**Their dung.** Dung that is excreted through the orifice of the anus. Our Rabbis explained to euphemize and read, “צואתם” [instead of חריהם].²¹

footnotes:
²¹ See Maseches Megillah 25b.


###### Rashi on II Kings 18:27:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/18:27:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/18:27:3)


**מֵימֵי שִׁינֵיהֵם.** צוֹאָה לֶחָה, שִׁנֵּי דְּכַרְכְּשָׁא דְּקַיְמָא אַתְּלַת שִנֵּי. אוֹ לְשׁוֹן גָּלָל, שֶׁבָּא עַל יְדֵי לְעִיסַת הַשִּׁנַּיִם.


**Their own urine.** Loose excrement, the glands of the rectum, which is held by three glands. Alternatively, an expression of excrement which comes about through the chewing of the teeth [=שניהם].



### Verse 31

###### Rashi on II Kings 18:31:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/18:31:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/18:31:1)


**עֲשׂוּ אִתִּי בְרָכָה.** שְׁאִילַת שָׁלוֹם, כְּמוֹ: וַיְבָרֶךְ יַעֲקֹב אֶת פַּרְעֹה.


**”Make peace with me.”** A greeting, as in, “And Yaakov greeted [=ויברך] Paroh.”²² ²³

footnotes:
²² Bereishis 47:7.
²³ See Rashi in Bereishis 33:10 and above, in II Melochim 5:15.



### Verse 32

###### Rashi on II Kings 18:32:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/18:32:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/18:32:1)


**אֶל אֶרֶץ כְּאַרְצְכֶם.** כֵּן דַּרְכִּי, לְהַעֲבִיר אֶת הָאֻמּוֹת מִמְּדִינָה לִמְדִינָה, הָיָה לוֹ לוֹמַר אֶל אֶרֶץ טוֹבָה מִשֶּׁלָּכֶם, שֶׁהֲרֵי לְפַתּוֹתָם בָּא, אֶלָּא שֶׁיָּדַע שֶׁיַּכִּירוּ שֶׁדְּבָרָיו שֶׁקֶר.


**To a land like you land.** This is my custom to move the nations from province to province. He should have said, “to a land better than yours,” because he came to persuade them, but he knew that they would recognize that his statement was false.²⁴ ²⁵

footnotes:
²⁴ This custom was practiced in order to prevent any future attempts to regain independence. He realized that as long as a conquered nation remained in its own land, the people would ultimately rise up and rebel against their conquerors.
²⁵ See Rashi, in Yeshayohu 36:17. The Gemara in Maseches Sanhedrin 94a, states that Sancheiriv did not say “to a land better than yours,” because he did not want to denigrate Eretz Yisroel. As a result he was rewarded with the title “the great and noble Osenapar [=Sancheiriv],” in Ezra 4:10.


###### Rashi on II Kings 18:32:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/18:32:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/18:32:2)


**אֶרֶץ דָּגָן וְתִירוֹשׁ.** זוֹ אַפְרִיקִי.


**A land of grain and wine.** This is Africa.



### Verse 34

###### Rashi on II Kings 18:34:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/18:34:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/18:34:1)


**הֵנַע וְעִוָּה.** אוֹתָם מֶלֶךְ אַשּׁוּר, הֶחֱרִיבָם וְהִגְלָם.


**He exiled them and twisted them.** The King of Ashur, destroyed them and exiled them.²⁶

²⁶ Alternatively, “Heina and Iva” were provinces or deities.—Radak


###### Rashi on II Kings 18:34:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/18:34:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/18:34:2)


**כִּי הִצִּילוּ אֶת שֹׁמְרוֹן.** וְהַשּׁוֹמְרוֹנִים הָיוּ עוֹבְדִים אֶת אֱלֹהֵי אֲרָם שֶׁהָיוּ שְׁכֵנֵיהֶם.


**Did they save Shomron.** And the inhabitants of Shomron worshiped the gods of the Aromeans, who were their neighbors.



### Verse 37

###### Rashi on II Kings 18:37:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/18:37:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/18:37:1)


**קְרוּעֵי בְגָדִים.** עַל שֶׁשָּׁמְעוּ גִּדּוּפֵי הַשֵּׁם.


**With torn garments.** Because they heard blasphemies of the Name [of God].²⁷

²⁷ See Maseches Sanhedrin 60a. One who hears another Jew blaspheme God must rend his clothing. Ravshokei's blasphemy was in comparing God to the pagan deities.—Radak Alternatively, they rend their clothing on account of the terrible news and threats that they just heard, an event similar to II Shmuel 13:31 and II Melochim 5:7.



## Chapter 19


### Verse 3

###### Rashi on II Kings 19:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/19:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/19:3:1)


**וְתוֹכֵחָה.** שֶׁהָרְשָׁעִים מִתְוַכְּחִין וּמַרְאִין פָּנִים לְדִבְרֵיהֶם, לֵאמֹר לָנוּ הַכֹּחַ וְהַגְּבוּרָה.


**[And] debate.** That the wicked are debating¹ and showing evidence to [support] their words, claiming, “We have the power and the strength.”²

footnotes:
¹ Alternatively, ותוכחה means “disgrace.—Targum Or “reproof.”—Radak
² Alternatively the phrase refers to God and means, “may He rebuke for the words, etc.”


###### Rashi on II Kings 19:3:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/19:3:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/19:3:2)


**בָנִים.** יִשְׂרָאֵל.


**Children.** Yisroel.


###### Rashi on II Kings 19:3:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/19:3:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/19:3:3)


**עַד מַשְׁבֵּר.** עַד צָרָה הַדּוֹמָה לְאִשָּׁה יוֹשֶׁבֶת עַל מַשְׁבֵּר וְאֵין בָּהּ כֹּחַ לֵילֵד.


**As far as the birthstool.** As far as a distress similar to a woman sitting on the birthstool, but not having strength to give birth.



### Verse 4

###### Rashi on II Kings 19:4:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/19:4:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/19:4:1)


**וְהוֹכִיחַ.** אישפרובי"ר בְּלַעַ"ז (לְהַבְהִיר), הֶרְאָה פָּנִים לִדְבָרָיו, שֶׁהִצְלִיחַ בְּכָל אֲשֶׁר הָלַךְ.


**And he brought proof with the words.** *Aprover*, in O.F., He showed evidence to [support] his statement,³ [namely,] that he prospered wherever he went.

footnotes:
³ Below, v. 9.



### Verse 7

###### Rashi on II Kings 19:7:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/19:7:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/19:7:1)


**וְשָׁמַע שְׁמוּעָה.** וְיַעֲלֶה מֵעֲלֵיכֶם וְשָׁב לְאַרְצוֹ, וְלֹא מֵחֲמַת הַשְּׁמוּעָה, אֶלָּא לְאַחַר זְמַן יָשׁוּב לְאַרְצוֹ בְּבשֶׁת פָּנִים, וְהִפַּלְתִּיו שָׁם בֶּחָרֶב, וּמַה הִיא הַשְּׁמוּעָה, וַיִּשְׁמַע אֶל תִּרְהָקָה, שָׁמַע שֶׁיָּצָא תִּרְהָקָה לְהִלָּחֵם עִמּוֹ, וְעָלָה מֵעַל יְרוּשָׁלַיִם וְהָלַךְ לְכוּשׁ, וְנִלְחַם עִם תִּרְהָקָה וּפוּט וּמִצְרַיִם שֶׁעִמּוֹ, וְנִצְּחָם וְנָטַל חֶמְדַּת אוֹצְרוֹתֵיהֶם, וּבָא לִירוּשָׁלַיִם וְנָפַל שָׁם, הוּא שֶׁאָמַר: יְגִיעַ מִצְרַיִם וּסְחַר כּוּשׁ וְגוֹ' עָלַיִךְ יַעֲבֹרוּ וְגוֹ'.


**And he will hear a rumor.** And he will withdraw from you and return to his land. He will not [return] because of the rumor, but after a while he will return to his land in disgrace, and I will cause him to fall there by the sword. Now what was the rumor? ”And he heard about Tirhaikoh.”⁴ He heard that Tirhaikoh had gone out to wage war against him, so, he left Yerusholayim and went to Cush, and waged war with Tirhaikoh, and Egypt, who were with him. He defeated him and took their most coveted treasures, and came to Yerusholayim, where he fell. In reference to this, Yeshayohu stated, ”The toil of Egypt and the merchandise of Cush, etc., will become yours, etc.”⁵

footnotes:
⁴ Yeshayohu 45:14.
⁵ See commentary above, 18:34.



### Verse 9

###### Rashi on II Kings 19:9:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/19:9:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/19:9:1)


**וַיָּשָׁב וַיִּשְׁלַח מַלְאָכִים.** לְהוֹדִיעַ שֶׁאֵינוֹ שָׁב לְאַרְצוֹ, אֶלָּא לְהִלָּחֵם עִם כּוּשׁ, וְלַחֲזֹר כָּאן.


**He again sent messengers.** To notify him that he was not returning to his land, rather he was waging war with Cush, and then to return here.

### Verse 23

###### Rashi on II Kings 19:23:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/19:23:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/19:23:1)


**מְרוֹם הָרִים.** הַר הַבַּיִת.


**The highest mountains.** [This refers to] the Temple Mount.⁷

###### Rashi on II Kings 19:23:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/19:23:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/19:23:2)

**To the end of the Levanon.** [This refers to] the Beis Hamikdosh which whitens [=מלבן] sins.⁸

**וְאֶכְרֹת קוֹמַת אֲרָזָיו.** לֹא אָשׁוּב עַד שֶׁאַחְרִיבֶנּוּ.


**And I will cut down its tallest cedar [trees].** I will not return until I have destroyed it.⁹
