[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/19:23:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/19:23:4)


**קִצֹּה.** סוֹפוֹ.


**Remotest.** Its end.¹⁰

footnotes:
¹⁰ I.e., his army is so vast that they drink up all the water available, he therefore had to dig and search for other water sources.—Radak


###### Rashi on II Kings 19:23:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/19:23:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/19:23:5)


**יַעַר כַּרְמִלּוֹ.** מְלִיאַת אַרְצוֹ וְיוֹפִי הֲדָרוֹ.


**Its fruitful forest.** The fullness of its land and the beauty of its splendor.



### Verse 24

###### Rashi on II Kings 19:24:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/19:24:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/19:24:1)


**אֲנִי קַרְתִּי וְשָׁתִיתִי מַיִם זָרִים.** כְּלוֹמַר הִתְחַלְתִּי בְּכָל מַעֲשֶׂה וְגָמַרְתִּי, כְּזֶה הַכּוֹרֶה בּוֹר וּמוֹצֵא מַיִם זָרִים וְשׁוֹתֶה. מַיִם נוֹבְעִים קוֹרֵא זָרִים, לְפִי שֶׁעַד הֵנָּה לֹא הִכִּירוּ בָּהֶם.


**I dug and drank strange water.** I.e., I started all my deeds and completed [them], as one who digs a hole and finds flowing water and drinks it. Flowing water he calls “זרים [=strange],” because until now no one knew of them.


###### Rashi on II Kings 19:24:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/19:24:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/19:24:2)


**וְאַחֲרִב בְּכַף פְּעָמַי.** אִם צַרְתִּי עַל עִיר הַבּוֹטַחַת בְּנַהֲרוֹתֶיהָ, הֵבֵאתִי עָלֶיהָ גְּיָסוֹת רַבּוֹת שֶׁהוֹבִישׁוּ יְאוֹרֶיהָ בִּשְׁתִיָּתָם וּשְׁתִיַּת בְּהֶמְתָּם וּמִרְמַס רַגְלֵיהֶם.


**And I dry up with the soles of my feet.** If I besiege a city that depends upon its rivers, I bring many troops upon it who dry up its rivers with their drinking and the drinking of their cattle¹¹ and by the treading of their feet.¹²

footnotes:
¹¹ His soldiers were so numerous that as they crossed a river they carried away so much water on their feet that the river bed was left dry by the time they all crossed it. See Maseches Sanhedrin 95b.
¹² Yeshayohu 10:5.


###### Rashi on II Kings 19:24:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/19:24:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/19:24:3)


**וְאַחֲרִב.** לְשׁוֹן הֹוֶה, כֵּן דַּרְכִּי תָמִיד.


**And I dry up.** This is in the present tense [because] this is always my way.



### Verse 25

###### Rashi on II Kings 19:25:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/19:25:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/19:25:1)


**הֲלֹא שָׁמַעְתָּ לְמֵרָחוֹק.** לָמָּה תִתְפָּאֵר וְתִתְגַּדֵּל בָּזֹאת, לֹא שֶׁלְּךָ הִיא, כִּי שָׁמַעְתָּ לְמֵרָחוֹק עַל יְדֵי הַנְּבִיאִים שֶׁגָּזַרְתִּי עַל הָעַמִּים לַהֲבִיאֲךָ עֲלֵיהֶם, כְּעִנְיַן שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר: הוֹי אַשּׁוּר שֵׁבֶט אַפִּי.


**Have you not heard from afar.** Why do you glorify and aggrandize yourself with this? This is not yours, for you have heard from afar through the prophets, that I decreed upon the nations to bring you upon them, as the matter that is stated, ”Ho, Ashur, the staff of My wrath.”¹³

footnotes:
¹³ Yeshayohu 30:33.


###### Rashi on II Kings 19:25:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/19:25:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/19:25:2)


**לְמִימֵי קֶדֶם.** מִשֶּׁנִּבְרָא הָעוֹלָם, עָלְתָה בְּמַחֲשָׁבָה, שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר: כִּי עָרוּךְ מֵאֶתְמוּל תָּפְתֶּה גַּם הִיא לַמֶּלֶךְ הוּכָן, זֶה סַנְחֵרִיב שֶׁנִּשְׂרַף בְּאֵשׁ גֵּיהִנֹּם, שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר: נְאֻם ה' אֲשֶׁר אוּר לוֹ בְּצִיּוֹן.


**In days of yore.** Since the world was created, it entered My thought, as it is stated, ”For [Gehinnom] is arranged from days of yore; that too was prepared for the king.”¹⁴ This refers to Sancheiriv, who was burned in the fire of Gehinnom, as it is stated, ”The word of Adonoy, Who has fire in Tzion.”¹⁵

footnotes:
¹⁴ Yeshayohu 31:9.
¹⁵ קצרי יד is an expression meaning “lack of power,” as in Bamidbar 11:23, “Is the power of AD limited [= תקצר’ ה היד].”


###### Rashi on II Kings 19:25:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/19:25:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/19:25:3)


**עַתָּה הֲבֵיאתִיהָ.** וְזֶה הַכֹּחַ שֶׁבְּיָדְךָ.


**Now I have brought it about.** And this is the power that is in your hand.


###### Rashi on II Kings 19:25:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/19:25:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/19:25:4)


**וּתְהִי.** בִּיאָתָהּ שֶל גְּזֵרָתִי, לְהִשָּׁאוֹת עָרִים בְּצוּרוֹת, לִהְיוֹת לְגַלִּים נִצִּים, לְגַלֵּי אֲדָמָה שֶׁצּוֹמְחִין בָּהֶן יְרָקוֹת, שֶׁאַתָּה מַחֲרִיבָן בִּגְזֵרָתִי.


**And it shall be.** The coming of My decree shall be to make desolate fortified cities into blossoming hills, into piles of earth upon which vegetables grow, for you destroy them because of My decree.


###### Rashi on II Kings 19:25:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/19:25:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/19:25:5)


**לַהְשׁוֹת.** לְשׁוֹן שְׁאִיָּה גַּלְמוּדָה.


**To make desolate.** An expression of desolation, forsaken.



### Verse 26

###### Rashi on II Kings 19:26:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/19:26:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/19:26:1)


**וְישְׁבֵיהֶן קִצְרֵי יָד.** הֵם, שֶׁאֲנִי הִתַּשְׁתִּי אֶת כֹּחָן לְפָנֶיךָ, וְאֵין הַגְּבוּרָה שֶׁלְּךָ, אֵין אַתָּה גִּבּוֹר, אֲבָל הֵם חַלָּשִׁים.


**Their inhabitants were short of strength.** Those whom I weakened before you,¹⁶ and the might is not yours.¹⁷ You are not mighty, rather they are weak.¹⁸

footnotes:
¹⁶ I.e., it is a Divine gift for which Sancheiriv could claim no credit.
¹⁷ The nations that Sancheiriv conquered had already been condemned to punishment and destruction.
¹⁸ In Tehilim 129:6 the verse states” … like the grass on rooftops which even before it is plucked, withers.”


###### Rashi on II Kings 19:26:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/19:26:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/19:26:2)


**חֲצִיר גַּגּוֹת.** הַמְמַהֵר לִיבַשׁ.


**Like grass of the rooftops.** Which dries quickly.¹⁹

footnotes:
¹⁹ Alternatively, grass is unable to move and defend itself from being cut.—Ralbag


###### Rashi on II Kings 19:26:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/19:26:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/19:26:3)


**וּשְׁדֵפָה לִפְנֵי קָמָה.** כַּשִּׁבֳּלִים הַשְּׁדוּפוֹת, בְּטֶרֶם יִתְקַשּׁוּ לָבֹא לִידֵי קָמָה.


**And wind blast before [becoming] standing grain.** Like blasted stalks of grain, before they hardened to become standing grain.



### Verse 27

###### Rashi on II Kings 19:27:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/19:27:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/19:27:1)


**וְשִׁבְתְּךָ וְצֵאתְךָ וּבֹאֲךָ.** תִּרְגֵּם יוֹנָתָן: וּמִיתְבָךְ בְּעֵצָה וּמִפְקָךְ לְאַגָּחָא קְרָבָא וּמֵיתָךְ לְאַרְעָא דְיִשְׂרָאֵל גָּלֵי קֳדָמַי, מֵאִתִּי נִהְיְתָה.


**Your sitting and your going out and your coming in.** [Targum] Yonoson rendered, ”And your sitting in counsel, and your going out to wage war, and your coming to the land of Yisroel, is revealed to Me;” it emanated from Me.


###### Rashi on II Kings 19:27:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/19:27:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/19:27:2)


**וְאֵת הִתְרַגֶּזְךָ אֵלָי.** וְאֶת אֲשֶׁר סוֹפְךָ לְהִתְגָּאוֹת וּלְהִתְעוֹרֵר אֵלַי בְּרֹגֶז וְרַעַשׁ, אשטרימי"ה בְּלַעַ"ז (הִתְרַגְזוּת, הִתְרַגְשׁוּת).


**And your raging against Me.** And that ultimately you will become arrogant and aroused against Me with anger and tumult, *estorimir*, in O.F..



### Verse 28

###### Rashi on II Kings 19:28:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/19:28:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/19:28:1)


**וְשַׁאֲנַנְךָ.** כְּמוֹ וּשְׁאוֹנְךָ.


**And your tumult.** [Means] the same as ושאונך [=your tumult].²⁰

footnotes:
²⁰ Alternatively, “your tranquility,” i.e., their rage was on account of their tranquility.—Radak


###### Rashi on II Kings 19:28:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/19:28:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/19:28:2)


**חַחִי.** כְּמִין שִׁיר הוּא, שֶׁמּוֹשְׁכִין בּוֹ בְּהֵמָה שֶׁעֲסָקֶיהָ רָעִים, כְּמוֹ שֶׁשָּׁנִינוּ: כָּל בַּעֲלֵי הַשִּׁיר יוֹצְאִין בְּשִׁיר.


**My ring.** A kind of ring by which they pull an animal whose behavior is bad,²¹ as we learned, ”And all animals that are usually pulled with a ring may go out [on Shabbos] with a ring.”²²

footnotes:
²¹ Alternatively, חח is a fish hook which pierces the fish when it is pulled out of the water.—Radak
²² Maseches Shabbos 51b.


###### Rashi on II Kings 19:28:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/19:28:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/19:28:3)


**וּמִתְגִּי.** תִּרְגֵּם יוֹנָתָן: זְמָם, וְהוּא שֶל בַּרְזֶל, וְתוֹחֲבִין אוֹתוֹ בִּנְחִירֵי הַנָּאקָה וְנִמְשֶׁכֶת בּוֹ, לְפִי שֶׁעֲסָקֶיהָ רָעִים, וְהוּא שֶׁשָּׁנִינוּ: וְּנָאקָה בַּחֲטָם.


**And my bit.** [Targum] Yonoson rendered זמם, which is made of iron, and which is inserted into the nostrils of a female camel,²³ and she is pulled with it, because her behavior is bad. This is what we learned, ”And the female camel [may go out on Shabbos] with a nose ring.”²⁴

footnotes:
²³ Alternatively, מתג is the bit placed in the animal's mouth by which to lead it.—Radak
²⁴ Maseches Shabbos 51b.



### Verse 29

###### Rashi on II Kings 19:29:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/19:29:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/19:29:1)


**וְזֶה לְּךָ הָאוֹת.** לְחִזְקִיָּהוּ אָמַר הַנָּבִיא, וְזֶה שֶׁאָמַרְתִּי לְךָ סַנְחֵרִיב יִפֹּל כָּאן, יְהִי לָךְ לְאוֹת אַף לַיָּמִים הַבָּאִים שֶׁאַתֶּם יְרֵאִים לָמוּת בָּרָעָב, לְפִי שֶׁסַּנְחֵרִיב הֶחֱרִיב אֶת הָאָרֶץ וְגָדַע אֶת הָאִילָנוֹת.


**And this shall be the sign for you.** The prophet said to Chizkiyohu, ”And this prophecy which I said to you that Sancheiriv will fall here, will be a sign for you²⁵ also for the approaching days, for you are afraid of dying from hunger, because Sancheiriv destroyed the land and chopped down the trees.”

footnotes:
²⁵ Alternatively, the sign for the fulfillment of the prophecy is that Sancheiriv will return to his land, mentioned in v. 7 above. The fulfillment of this prophecy serves as a sign that the remainder of the prophecy will likewise be fulfilled.—Radak


###### Rashi on II Kings 19:29:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/19:29:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/19:29:2)


**אָכוֹל הַשָּׁנָה סָפִיחַ.** בַּשָּׁנָה הַזֹּאת יִצְמְחוּ לָכֶם צְמָחִים, וְתִתְפַּרְנְסוּ מֵהֶם.


**This year you will eat what grows by itself.** This year plants will grow for you, and you will be sustained by them.


###### Rashi on II Kings 19:29:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/19:29:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/19:29:3)


**סָחִישׁ.** גְּדוּעֵי אִילָנוֹת שֶׁיִּצְמְחוּ, וּכְשֶׁתִּרְאֶה דְּבָרַי קַיָּם בְּמַפָּלַת הָאוֹכְלֻסִין, יִהְיֶה לְךָ לְאוֹת שֶׁאַף הַבְטָחָה זוֹ תִּתְקַיֵּם.


**What grows from the tree stumps.** The chopped off trees that will sprout, and when you see My word fulfilled concerning the fall of the armies, it will be a sign for you that this promise, too, will be fulfilled.



### Verse 31

###### Rashi on II Kings 19:31:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/19:31:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/19:31:1)


**קִנְאַת ה' צְבָאוֹת.** שֶׁיְּקַנֵּא לִכְבוֹדוֹ וְלִשְׁמוֹ, וְלֹא שֶׁיֵּשׁ זְכוּת בְּיֶדְכֶם, כִּי הַרְבֵּה הִרְשִׁיעַ אָחָז אָבִיךָ.


**The zeal of Adonoy of Hosts.** That He will be zealous for His honor and for His Name, not that you have any merit,²⁶ for Achaz your father committed many wicked deeds.

footnotes:
²⁶ For even the merit of the Patriarchs had been used up, according to Rashi in Yeshayohu 37:32.



### Verse 32

###### Rashi on II Kings 19:32:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/19:32:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/19:32:1)


**וְלֹא יְקַדְּמֶנָּה.** לֹא יֵעָרֵךְ לְפָנֶיהָ מָגֵן.


**He will not advance upon it.** He will not set a shield before it.²⁷

footnotes:
²⁷ The first soldiers attacking a city were the shield bearers.—Radak


###### Rashi on II Kings 19:32:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/19:32:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/19:32:2)


**סֹלְלָה.** צִבּוּר אֲדָמָה לְהַגְבִּיהַּ תֵּל לַעֲמֹד עָלָיו לְהִלָּחֵם עַל הָעִיר, וְעַל שֵׁם שֶׁהוּא כּוֹבְשׁוֹ וְחוֹבְטוֹ בְּעֵצִים וּמַקָּבוֹת כְּדֵי שֶׁיְּהֵא נָדוֹשׁ וְכָבוּשׁ בְּחֹזֶק, קוֹרֵהוּ סוֹלְלָה, לְשׁוֹן: סֹלּוּ סֹלּוּ הַמְסִלָּה.


**A ramp.** A heap of earth to raise a mount upon which to stand to wage war against the city, and because one presses it down and beats it with sticks and sledge hammers, so that it becomes trodden and pressed down with strength, it is called סוללה, an expression of, “Beat down [=סולו], beat down [=סולו] the highway.”²⁸

footnotes:
²⁸ Yeshayohu 62:10. See also II Shmuel 20:15.



### Verse 35

###### Rashi on II Kings 19:35:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/19:35:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/19:35:1)


**וַיְהִי בַּלַּיְלָה הַהוּא.** כְּשֶׁשָּׁב סַנְחֵרִיב מֵעַל כּוּשׁ, וּבָא עַד נֹב שֶׁהָיְתָה סְמוּכָה לִירוּשָׁלַיִם.


**And it was that night.** When Sancheiriv returned from [battling] with Cush, and he came as far as Nov, which was near Yerusholayim.²⁹ of Nov, and it was therefore decreed that his descendants would be liable for punishment for many years. This was the last day of the decree, as stated in Yeshayohu 10:32, “Yet today he will stand in Nov.”

footnotes:
²⁹ Although his astrologers told him that he would be successful in conquering Yerusholayim if he were to attack it on that very day, Sancheiriv in his arrogance decided to camp overnight in Nov and wait for the following day. This ultimately led to his downfall. See Maseches Sanhedrin 95a. Dovid who was Chizkiyohu's ancestor, incurred guilt by causing the deaths of the kohanim


###### Rashi on II Kings 19:35:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/19:35:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/19:35:2)


**מֵאָה שְׁמוֹנִים וַחֲמִשָּׁה אָלֶף.** כֻּלָּם רָאשֵׁי גְּיָסוֹת.


**One hundred eighty-five thousand.** All of them were heads of companies.³⁰

footnotes:
³⁰ Sancheiriv's camp was enormous. Each of these 185,000 officers was in charge of 2,000 men. See Rashi above, in 18:24.



### Verse 36

###### Rashi on II Kings 19:36:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/19:36:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/19:36:1)


**וַיֵּשֶׁב בְּנִינְוֵה.** הִיא רֹאשׁ לְאֶרֶץ אַשּׁוּר.


**And dwelt in Nineveh.** It was the capital of Ashur.



### Verse 37

###### Rashi on II Kings 19:37:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/19:37:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/19:37:1)


**בֵּית נִסְרֹךְ אֱלֹהָיו.** נֶסֶר מִתֵּבָתוֹ שֶׁל נֹחַ.


**In the temple of Nisroch his god.** A board from Noach's ark.³¹ ³²

footnotes:
³¹ נסרת is a contraction of נסר מתיבתו של נח.
³² Sancheiriv believed it was the deity which saved Noach from the Flood, so it will save him also. See Maseches Sanhedrin 96a.


###### Rashi on II Kings 19:37:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/19:37:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/19:37:2)


**הִכֻּהוּ בַחֶרֶב.** שָׁמְעוּ אוֹתוֹ שֶׁאָמַר לְשָׁחְטָן לְפָנָיו אִם יַצִּילוּהוּ, שֶׁלֹּא יַהַרְגוּהוּ שָׂרֵי הַמַּלְכוּת שֶׁמֵּתוּ בְנֵיהֶם עַל יָדוֹ.


**Slew him with a sword.** They heard him that he said he would slaughter them³³ before him if they would spare him, so that they would not kill him---the princes of the kingdom, whose sons perished on account of him.

footnotes:
³³ Sancheiriv asked his wise men what was the merit that the Bnei Yisroel had that God fights their battles. They replied that their forefather Avrohom brought his son for a burnt offering. So he decided that he too will offer his two sons. When his sons heard this, they killed him.—Radak



## Chapter 20


### Verse 1

###### Rashi on II Kings 20:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/20:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/20:1:1)


**בַּיָּמִים הָהֵם.** שְׁלשָׁה יָמִים לִפְנֵי מַפַּלְתּוֹ שֶל סַנְחֵרִיב.


**In those days.** [This occurred] three days before Sancheiriv's downfall.¹

footnotes:
¹ Alternatively, the entire episode is stated in chronological order, and it occurred after Sancheiriv's downfall. The assurance Chizkiyohu requested that he will be saved from Ashur mentioned here refers to future attacks. Although Ashur had been soundly defeated, Sancheiriv's successors might soon try again to attack.—Ralbag


###### Rashi on II Kings 20:1:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/20:1:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/20:1:2)


**חָלָה חִזְקִיָּהוּ.** וְיוֹם שְׁלִישִׁי כְּשֶׁעָלָה בֵּית ה', יוֹם מַפַּלְתּוֹ שֶל סַנְחֵרִיב, וְיוֹם טוֹב שֶל פֶּסַח הָיָה.


**Chizkiyohu became … ill.** On the third day, when he went up to the Beis [Hamikdosh] of A, was the day of Sancheiriv's downfall, and it was on the Pesach holiday.²

footnotes:
² To commemorate this event, reference to Sancheiriv's downfall is mentioned in the Hagaddah on both Seder nights. On the first Seder night it is mentioned in ובכן ויהי בחצי הלילה, and on the second Seder night it is mentioned in ובכן ואמרתם זבח פסח.


###### Rashi on II Kings 20:1:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/20:1:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/20:1:3)


**כִּי מֵת אַתָּה.** בָּעוֹלָם הַזֶּה.


**For you are going to die.** On this world.


###### Rashi on II Kings 20:1:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/20:1:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/20:1:4)


**וְלֹא תִחְיֶה.** לָעוֹלָם הַבָּא, מִשּׁוּם דְּלָא נָסַבְתְּ אִשָּׁה, כִּדְאִיתָא בִּבְרָכוֹת.


**And you will not live.** In the World to Come because you have not married as is [explained] in [Maseches] Berachos.³

footnotes:
³ 10a. Chizkiyohu refused to marry and have children because it was revealed to him that he would have a very wicked son, and a period of extreme corruption would follow.



### Verse 4

###### Rashi on II Kings 20:4:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/20:4:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/20:4:1)


**לֹא יָצָא חָצֵר וְגוֹ'.** מִהֵר הַקָּבָּ"ה לְהוֹדִיעַ רְפוּאָתוֹ, בְּטֶרֶם יֵצֵא קוֹל הֲבָרָה בָּעִיר שֶׁנִּקְנְסָה עָלָיו מִיתָה, כִּדְאִיתָא בִּירוּשַׁלְמִי.


**Had not gone out of the … courtyard, etc.** The Holy One, Blessed Is He, hurried to notify him about his cure before the rumor would spread in the city that he has been sentenced to death, as stated in [Talmud] Yerushalmi.⁴ ⁵

footnotes:
⁴ Maseches Sanhedrin 10:2.
⁵ The כתיב [=it is written] is העיר, the city. The Gemara in Maseches Eiruvin 26a explains that the inner court behind the king's palace was the size of an average city.—Radak



### Verse 6

###### Rashi on II Kings 20:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/20:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/20:6:1)


**וּמִכַּף מֶלֶךְ אַשּׁוּר אַצִּילְךָ.** לָמַדְנוּ שֶׁלִּפְנֵי מַפָּלָתּוֹ שֶל סַנְחֵרִיב חָלָה.


**And I will save you from the hand of the king of Ashur.** We learn that he became ill before Sancheiriv's downfall.⁶

footnotes:
⁶ See note 1 above.



### Verse 7

###### Rashi on II Kings 20:7:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/20:7:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/20:7:1)


**דְּבֶלֶת תְּאֵנִים.** כְּשֶׁהֵם לַחִין קְרוּיִין תְּאֵנִים, וּכְשֶׁנִּדְרָסִים בְּעִגּוּל קְרוּיִין דְּבֵלָה.


**A cake of pressed figs.** When they are fresh, they are called תאנים [=figs], and when they are pressed into a round cake, they are called דבלה.


###### Rashi on II Kings 20:7:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/20:7:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/20:7:2)


**וַיָּשִׂימוּ עַל הַשְּׁחִין וַיֶּחִי.** וְנֵס בְּתוֹךְ נֵס, שֶׁהֲרֵי אַף בָּשָׂר חַי כְּשֶׁאַתָּה נוֹתֵן עָלָיו דְּבֵלָה, נִסְרָח, וְהַקָּבָּ"ה נוֹתֵן דָּבָר הַמְחַבֵּל לְתוֹךְ דָּבָר שֶׁנִּתְחַבֵּל, וּמִתְרַפֵּא.


**And placed it on the boil, and it was healed.** This was a miracle within a miracle, for even healthy flesh, when you place a cake of pressed figs upon it, decays, yet the Holy One, Blessed Is He, [instructs to] put something that is injurious upon something that is vulnerable, and it becomes healed.



### Verse 9

###### Rashi on II Kings 20:9:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/20:9:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/20:9:1)


**הָלַךְ הַצֵּל עֶשֶׂר מַעֲלוֹת אִם יָשׁוּב עֶשֶׂר מַעֲלוֹת.** הֲרֵי זֶה מִקְרָא קָצָר, כְּבָר יָרַד הַצֵּל לְמַעֲלוֹת הָעֲשׂוּיוֹת לִשְׁעוֹת הַיּוֹם, כְּמוֹ אורלויי"ן בְּלַעַ"ז (שָׁעוֹן), שֶׁעוֹשִׂין הָאֲנָשִׁים לִבְחֹן שְׁעוֹת הַיּוֹם, חָפֵץ אַתָּה שֶׁיַּמְתִּין בְּמָקוֹם שֶׁהוּא שָׁם כְּדֵי מַהֲלַךְ עֶשֶׂר שָׁעוֹת, אוֹ יָשׁוּב לְמַעְלָה עֶשֶׂר מַעֲלוֹת, וִיהֵא נִרְאֶה כְּאִלּוּ הוּא שַׁחֲרִית.


**Should the shade advance ten steps or should it go back ten steps?** This is an abbreviated verse. The shade had already advanced the steps that are made [to measure] the hours of the day, like, *oriloje*, in O. F., the clocks that people make to determine the hour of the day. [He asked,] “Do you wish it to stay in the place where it is, for as long as it takes it to walk in ten hours, or should it go back up ten steps so that it will appear as though it is morning?”⁷

footnotes:
⁷ Alternatively, Yeshayohu asked, “Do you wish it to advance or to go back?—Radak



### Verse 10

###### Rashi on II Kings 20:10:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/20:10:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/20:10:1)


**וַיֹּאמֶר יְחִזְקִיָּהוּ נָקֵל לַצֵּל לִנְטוֹת עֶשֶׂר מַעֲלוֹת.** דָּבָר קַל הוּא לִנְטוֹת בִּמְקוֹמוֹ, וְנוֹטֶה וְעוֹמֵד עֶשֶׂר מַעֲלוֹת אֵינוֹ דָּבָר הַנִּכָּר כָּל כָּךְ, לֹא כֵן אֶלָּא יָשׁוּב הַצֵּל, וְיִהְיֶה הַדָּבָר תֵּמַהּ, שֶׁרָאוּ שֶׁפָּנָה הַיּוֹם לַעֲרֹב, וְחוֹזֵר לִהְיוֹת בֹּקֶר.


**And Chizkiyohu said, ”It is easier for the shade to advance ten steps.”** It is easy [for the shade] to remain in its place, and stay for ten steps, it is not something that is so conspicuous. Not so, rather let the shade go back, and the event will be a wonder, for they saw that the day was turning toward evening, and it returns to become morning [again].



### Verse 11

###### Rashi on II Kings 20:11:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/20:11:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/20:11:1)


**אֲשֶׁר יָרְדָה בְּמַעֲלוֹת אָחָז.** יוֹם שֶׁמֵּת אָחָז, נִתְקַצֵּר הַיּוֹם וּמִהֵר הַצֵּל לֵירֵד עֶשֶׂר מַעֲלוֹת, כְּדֵי שֶׁלֹּא יְהֵא נִסְפָּד, וְאוֹתָן עֶשֶׂר שָׁעוֹת חָזְרוּ עַכְשָׁיו לְחִזְקִיָּהוּ.


**That it had gone down on the steps of Achaz.** The day that Achaz died, the day was shortened,⁸ and the shade hurried to advance ten steps, so that he would not be eulogized,⁹ and those ten hours returned now for Chizkiyohu.¹⁰

footnotes:
⁸ Alternatively, it is called the steps of Achaz because he had built this sundial.—Radak
⁹ I.e., there would not be enough time to eulogize him. See Maseches Sanhedrin 96a and Rashi there.
¹⁰ From the day it was created, God prepared the sun for this miracle. Concerning the creation of the sun and other heavenly bodies, in Bereishis 1:14 states, “they will be for signs,” i.e., God used them as signs for Yehoshua and Tzidkiyohu.—Chizkuni



### Verse 12

###### Rashi on II Kings 20:12:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/20:12:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/20:12:1)


**כִּי שָׁמַע כִּי חָלָה.** וְעַד אוֹתוֹ הַיּוֹם לֹא הָיָה אָדָם חוֹלֶה וְחָיָה, וְאֵיךְ שָׁמַע, לָמוּד הָיָה לֶאֱכֹל בְּשָׁלשׁ שָׁעוֹת בַּיּוֹם, וְיָשַׁן עַד תֵּשַׁע, כֵּיוָן שֶׁחָזַר גַּלְגַּל חַמָּה לְחִזְקִיָּהוּ, נֵעוֹר בְּתֶשַׁע שָׁעוֹת וּמָצָא שֶׁהוּא שַׁחֲרִית, בִּקֵּשׁ לַהֲרֹג אֶת עֲבָדָיו, אָמַר: אַתֶּם הִנַּחְתֶּם אוֹתִי לִישֹׁן יוֹם וָלַיְלָה, אָמְרוּ: גַּלְגַּל חַמָּה חָזַר, אָמַר לָהֶם: מִי הֶחֱזִירוֹ, אָמְרוּ לוֹ: אֱלֹהָיו שֶל חִזְקִיָּהוּ, כִּדְאִיתָא בִּפְסִיקְתָּא.


**For he heard that Chizkiyohu was ill.** And until that day no person who had ever been ill, had become well. Now, how did he hear? He was accustomed to eat after three hours [had elapsed] in the day, and he would sleep until the ninth [hour of the day]. Because the cycle of the sun had gone backwards on account of Chizkiyohu, he awoke after nine hours [of the day] and found that it was morning, and he wanted to kill his servants. He said, ”You let me sleep a day and a night!” They replied, “The sun's cycle went backwards.” He asked them, “Who brought it back?” They said to him, “The God of Chizkiyohu,”¹¹ as is [stated] in Pesikta.

footnotes:
¹¹ He then sent messengers to inquire about this great event and eventually heard about Chizkiyohu's miraculous cure. See Maseches Sanhedrin 96a.



### Verse 13

###### Rashi on II Kings 20:13:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/20:13:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/20:13:1)


**בֵּית נְכֹתֹה.** בֵּית גִּנְזֵי בְּשָׂמָיו, כְּמוֹ: נְכֹאת וּצְרִי וָלֹט.


**His treasure house.** The storehouse of his spices,¹² as in, “spices [=נכאת], balm, and lotus.”¹³

footnotes:
¹² נכאת is a term used to describe choice possessions. Accordingly, the Gemara presents different opinions. Some say נכאת is “his treasure house,” others say, “his most powerful weapons,” i.e., his armory. Yet others say that he had his wife [=בית נכתה] wait on them and pour their drinks. See Maseches Sanhedrin 104a and Rashi there
¹³ Bereishis 37:25..


###### Rashi on II Kings 20:13:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/20:13:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/20:13:2)


**וְאֵת שֶׁמֶן הַטּוֹב.** יֵשׁ פּוֹתְרִין שֶׁמֶן הַמִּשְׁחָה, וְיֵשׁ פּוֹתְרִים שֶׁמֶן אֲפַרְסְמוֹן (בלסמון), שֶׁהוּא מָצוּי בְּאֶרֶץ יִשְׂרָאֵל, שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר: יְהוּדָה וְיִשְׂרָאֵל הֵמָּה רֹכְלָיִךְ בְּחִטֵּי מִנִּית וּפַנַּג. וְרָאִיתִי בְסֵפֶר יוֹסִיפוּן: פַּנַּג הוּא אֲפַרְסְמוֹן, וְגָדֵל בִּירִיחוֹ, וְעַל שֵׁם הָרֵיחַ נִקְרָא יְרִיחוֹ.


**The fine oil.** Some interpret this as the anointment oil, and others interpret it as balsam oil, which is found in Eretz Yisroel, as it is said, ”Yehudah and [Eretz] Yisroel, they were your merchants, with wheat of מנית and פנג,”¹⁴ and I saw in the book of Yosephun, that פנג is balsam, and it grows in Yericho, and because of its aroma [=ריח], it is called Yericho [=יריחו].

footnotes:
¹⁴ Yechezkeil 27:17.


###### Rashi on II Kings 20:13:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/20:13:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/20:13:3)


**לֹא הָיָה דָבָר.** אֶת הָאָרוֹן וְהַלּוּחוֹת וְסֵפֶר הַתּוֹרָה.


**There was nothing.** [He even showed them] the Ark, the Luchos, and the Torah Scroll.



### Verse 14

###### Rashi on II Kings 20:14:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/20:14:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/20:14:1)


**מֵאֶרֶץ רְחוֹקָה בָּאוּ.** זֶה אֶחָד מִשְּׁלשָׁה שֶׁבְּדָקָן הַקָּבָּ"ה וּמְצָאָן עָבִיט שֶל מַיִם עֲכוּרִים, קַיִן, יְחִזְקִיָּהוּ, וּבִלְעָם. קַיִן: לֹא יָדַעְתִּי הֲשׁוֹמֵר אָחִי אָנֹכִי, הָיָה לוֹ לוֹמַר רִבּוֹנוֹ שֶל עוֹלָם הֲלֹא כָל הַנִּסְתָּרוֹת גְּלוּיוֹת לְךָ, כִּדְאִיתָא בְּתַנְחוּמָא; בִּלְעָם, שֶׁאָמַר לוֹ הַמָּקוֹם: מִי הָאֲנָשִׁים הָאֵלֶּה עִמָּךְ, הָיָה לוֹ לוֹמַר ה' אֱלֹהִים אַתָּה יָדָעְתָּ, כְּמוֹ שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר בִּיחֶזְקֵאל, וְהוּא הֵשִׁיב בְּגַאֲוָה: בָּלָק בֶּן צִפּוֹר מֶלֶךְ מוֹאָב שָׁלַח אֵלַי, הַרְבֵּה מְבַקְּשִׁים יֵשׁ לִי; חִזְקִיָּהוּ, הָיָה לוֹ לְהָשִׁיב לִישַׁעְיָהוּ, אַתָּה נְבִיאוֹ שֶל מָקוֹם, וְאַתָּה שׁוֹאֵל אֵלַי, הִתְחִיל חִזְקִיָּה מִתְגָּאֶה וְאָמַר: מֵאֶרֶץ רְחוֹקָה בָּאוּ אֵלַי, לְכָךְ נֶעֱנַשׁ, וְעַל שֶׁשָּׂמַח עֲלֵיהֶם וְהֶאֱכִילָם עַל שֻׁלְחָנוֹ.


**”They came from a distant land.”** This is one of the three [men] whom the Holy One, Blessed Is He, tested and found them to be a pot of muddy water: [The three are,] Kayin, Chizkiyohu and Bilam. Kayin [said], “I don't know, am I my brother's keeper?”¹⁵ He should have said, ”Master of the Universe are all hidden things not revealed to You?” as it is [stated] in [Midrash] Tanchuma. Bilam, when the Omnipresent said to him, ”Who are these men with you?”¹⁶ he should have said, “Adonoy God, You know!” as it is stated concerning Yechezkeil,¹⁷ but he replied arrogantly, ”Balak son of Tzippor, king of Moav, sent them to me,”¹⁸ [i.e.,] many people seek me. Chizkiyohu should have replied to Yeshayohu, ”You are the prophet of the Omnipresent, yet you are asking me?” [Instead,] Chizkiyohu began to become haughty, and said, ”They came to me from a distant land.”¹⁹ Therefore, he was punished, and also [he was punished] because he rejoiced over them,²⁰ and served them at his table.

footnotes:
¹⁵ Bereishis 4:9.
¹⁶ Bamidbar 22:9.
¹⁷ Yechezkeil 37:3.
¹⁸ Bamidbar 22:10.
¹⁹ Yeshayohu 39:3.
²⁰ God's wrath was mainly because Chizkiyohu distracted his visitors from God's glory by seeking to impress them with his own wealth. See II Divrei Hayomim 32:25.



### Verse 17

###### Rashi on II Kings 20:17:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/20:17:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/20:17:1)


**לֹא יִוָּתֵר דָּבָר.** מִדָּה כְּנֶגֶד מִדָּה, לֹא הָיָה דָּבָר.


**Nothing will remain.** You shall be paid measure for measure, [as stated above,] “There was nothing.”²¹

footnotes:
²¹ Verse 15 above.



### Verse 18

###### Rashi on II Kings 20:18:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/20:18:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/20:18:1)


**וּמִבָּנֶיךָ.** חֲנַנְיָה מִישָׁאֵל וַעֲזַרְיָה.


**And some of your sons.** Chananyoh, Mishoeil, and Azaryoh.²²

footnotes:
²² Some say that they were eunuchs, because it was the custom to castrate those who served in the palace. Targum maintains that סריסים means ‘officers.’ It is merely a figurative expression used here to symbolize that paganism's impotence was acknowledged in their time. Chananyoh, Mishoeil and Azaryoh would risk their lives by defying the order to worship idols and be miraculously saved. See Maseches Sanhedrin 93b.



### Verse 19

###### Rashi on II Kings 20:19:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/20:19:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/20:19:1)


**טוֹב דְּבַר ה'.** מֵאַחַר שֶׁבְּיָמַי יִהְיֶה שָׁלוֹם.


**”The word of Adonoy … is good.”** Because in my days there will be peace.²³

footnotes:
²³ God's punishment would not be meted out until after his death.



### Verse 20

###### Rashi on II Kings 20:20:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/20:20:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/20:20:1)


**וַאֲשֶׁר עָשָׂה אֶת הַבְּרֵכָה וְגוֹ'.** בְּדִבְרֵי הַיָּמִים.


**And how he made the pool, etc.** In Divrei Hayomim.²⁴

footnotes:
²⁴ See II Divrei Hayomim 32:3-4 and 30. When Sancheiriv began his march on Yerusholayim, Chizkiyohu closed off all the springs and streams outside Yerusholayim as a defensive strategy.



## Chapter 21


### Verse 10

###### Rashi on II Kings 21:10:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/21:10:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/21:10:1)


**בְּיַד עֲבָדָיו הַנְּבִיאִים.** נַחוּם וַחֲבַקּוּק, וּלְפִי שֶׁהָיָה מְנַשֶּׁה רָשָׁע, לֹא נִקְרְאוּ עַל שְׁמוֹ לְפָרֵשׁ שֶׁבְּיָמָיו הָיוּ.


**Through His servants the prophets.** Nachum and Chavakuk, but because Menashe was wicked, they were not referred to with his name to explain that they were [prophesying] in his time.



### Verse 12

###### Rashi on II Kings 21:12:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/21:12:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/21:12:1)


**תִּצַּלְנָה.** לְשׁוֹן מְצִלְתַּיִם, טינטני"ר בְּלַעַ"ז (לִרְעוֹד).


**Will ring.** An expression of cymbals,¹ *tintiner*, in O. F.

footnotes:
¹ See Rashi in I Shmuel 3:11.



### Verse 13

###### Rashi on II Kings 21:13:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/21:13:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/21:13:1)


**מִשְׁקֹלֶת.** הוּא חוּט שֶׁהַבּוֹנִים חוֹמָה קוֹשְׁרִין בּוֹ עוֹפֶרֶת לְכַוֵּן בּוֹ אֶת הַחוֹמָה.


**And the plumb line.** That is the string to which the builders of a wall tie a lead weight to make the wall straight.²

footnotes:
² I.e., so that the following rows of bricks or stones will all be directly above one another.


###### Rashi on II Kings 21:13:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/21:13:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/21:13:2)


**יִמְחֶה אֶת הַצַּלַּחַת.** יְקַנַּח אֶת הַקְּעָרָה.


**As one wipes a dish.** As one wipes a dish.³

footnotes:
³ Just as one wipes a dish and turns it over so that it should not become soiled or be eaten from any more, so will Yerusholayim be cleaned out of its habitants and then it will destroyed so that it would no longer be habitable.—Radak



## Chapter 22


### Verse 8

###### Rashi on II Kings 22:8:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/22:8:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/22:8:1)


**סֵפֶר הַתּוֹרָה מָצָאתִי.** טְמֹן תַּחַת הַנִּדְבָּךְ, שֶׁהִטְמִינוּ שָׂם כְּשֶׁשָּׂרַף אָחָז אֶת הַתּוֹרָה.


**I have found the Torah Scroll.** It was hidden under a layer of stones where they had concealed it when Achaz burned the Torah.¹

footnotes:
¹ According to II Divrei Hayomim 34:14, this was the original Torah Scroll written by Moshe. God had commanded Moshe to write this Scroll at the end of his life. See Devarim 31:24-26.



### Verse 13

###### Rashi on II Kings 22:13:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/22:13:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/22:13:1)


**דִרְשׁוּ אֶת ה' בַּעֲדִי.** רָאָה כָּתוּב יוֹלֵךְ ה' אֹתְךָ וְאֶת מַלְכְּךָ.


**Inquire of Adonoy on my behalf.** He saw written, "Adonoy will lead you and your king."² ³

footnotes:
² Devarim 28:36.
³ When they opened the Torah Scroll it was found rolled to this section. This section deals with the exile of Shomron and Yehudah with its king. Chilkiyoh gave the Torah Scroll to Shofon to read to the king with the hope that perhaps he would come up with a suggestion to alleviate the dreadful situation.—Radak



### Verse 14

###### Rashi on II Kings 22:14:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/22:14:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/22:14:1)


**אֶל חֻלְדָּה.** אָמְרוּ רַבּוֹתֵינוּ: לְפִי שֶׁהָאִשָּׁה מְרַחֶמֶת יוֹתֵר מִן הָאִישׁ, לְכָךְ לֹא שָׁלַח אֶל יִרְמְיָהוּ. וְיֵשׁ אוֹמְרִים: יִרְמְיָהוּ לֹא הָיָה שָׂם, שֶׁהָלַךְ לְהַחֲזִיר עֲשֶׂרֶת הַשְּׁבָטִים.


**To Chuldoh.** Our Rabbis said that because a woman is more merciful than a man,⁴ he, therefore, did not send to Yirmiyohu [to inquire]. Others say that Yirmiyohu was not there because he went to bring back the ten tribes.⁵

footnotes:
⁴ They therefore hoped she would beseech God for mercy, that He change the evil decree to a favorable one.—Maharsha in Maseches Megillah 12b
⁵ See Maseches Megillah 14b and Rashi there.


###### Rashi on II Kings 22:14:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/22:14:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/22:14:2)


**בַּמִּשְׁנֶה.** בְּבֵית אוּלְפָּנָא. שַׁעַר יֵשׁ בָּעֲזָרָה שֶׁשְּׁמוֹ שַׁעַר חֻלְדָּה בְּמַסֶּכֶת מִדּוֹת. וְיֵשׁ שֶׁפּוֹתְרִים 'בַּמִשְׁנֶה', חוּץ לַחוֹמָה, בֵּין שְׁתֵּי הַחוֹמוֹת, שֶׁהִיא מִשְׁנֶה לָעִיר, (בַּמִּשְׁנֶה. הָיְתָה לוֹמְדָה הַתּוֹרָה שֶׁבְּעַל פֶּה לַזְּקֵנִים שֶׁבַּדּוֹר, וְהִיא הִיא הַמִּשְׁנֶה, הג"ה דר"ע). **בַּמִּשְׁנֶה.** (מֵ'אֵלֶּה הַדְּבָרִים' עַד 'לְעֵינֵי כָּל יִשְׂרָאֵל', דְּהַיְנוּ סֵפֶר דְּבָרִים הַקָּרוּי מִשְׁנֵה תּוֹרָה, וְכֵן כָּל הַדְּבָרִים הַנִּכְפָּלִים בַּתּוֹרָה דָּרְשָׁה בָּרַבִּים, וְגִלְּתָה הָעֳנָשִׁים וְהַגָּלֻיּוֹת הַנִּכְפָּלִים לָעוֹבְרִים עַל סוֹדוֹ וְרִמְזֵי הַתּוֹרָה. סוֹד מֵישָׁרִים).


**In the study house.** [Targum Yonoson rendered,] in the study⁶ house. There is a gate in the Temple courtyard, named "the Gate of Chuldoh" [as stated] in Maseches Midos.⁷ Others interpret "במשנה" as "outside the wall/' [i.e.,] between the two walls, which are double⁸ [around] the city. (במשנה [i.e.,] she was teaching the Oral Law to the elders of the generation, i.e., the Mishnah. An annotation of Rabbi Akiva.) **In the study house.** (From, "These are the words,"⁹ until "before the eyes of all Yisroel,"¹⁰ ¹¹ i.e., the Book of Devarim, also known as Misheh Torah, and likewise all the things that are repeated in the Torah, she expounded on in public, and she revealed the punishments and the exiles which were doubled for those who transgress the secrets and the allusions of the Torah. The Secret of Righteousness.)

footnotes:
⁶ שנה[=study]
⁷ 1:3; according to that Mishnah the two Gates of Chuldoh led to the Temple Mount.
⁸ משנה [=double] as in “a double portion [=משנה] of food” in Shemos 16:22.
⁹ Devarim1:1.
¹⁰ Ibid. 33-12.
¹¹ She expounded on Chumash Devarim which is known as Mishnah Torah [=משנה תורה] because many topics from other Chumashim are repeated in it.



### Verse 20

###### Rashi on II Kings 22:20:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/22:20:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/22:20:1)


**וְנֶאֱסַפְתָּ אֶל קִבְרֹתֶיךָ בְּשָׁלוֹם.** וּמַהוּ הַשָּׁלוֹם, וְלֹא תִרְאֶינָה עֵינֶיךָ בְּחֻרְבַּן הַבָּיִת.


**And you will be gathered into your grave in peace.** And what is the peace?¹² "Your eyes will not see," the destruction of the Beis [Hamikdosh].¹³

footnotes:
¹² Although Yoshiyahu was killed in a battle with Paro Necho, he is considered as having died in peace because he died while the Bnei Yisroel were living in peace, before the destruction began.—Radak
¹³ See Maseches Moed Katan 28b.



## Chapter 23


### Verse 3

###### Rashi on II Kings 23:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/23:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/23:3:1)


**עַל הָעַמּוּד.** מְקוֹם מַעֲמַד הַמֶּלֶךְ בְּבֵית הַמִּקְדָּשׁ.


**At his place.** The place where the king stands in the Beis Hamikdosh.¹

footnotes:
¹ See above 11:14. It was a special place in the Beis Hamikdosh where the king would stand when addressing the people.



### Verse 4

###### Rashi on II Kings 23:4:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/23:4:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/23:4:1)


**וְאֶת כֹּהֲנֵי הַמִּשְׁנֶה.** סְגָנֵי כְּהֻנָּה, שֶׁהֵם שְׁנִיִּים לַכֹּהֲנִים גְּדוֹלִים.


**The *kohanim* of the second rank.** The assistants of the priesthood,²who was anointed for war was also included in this group.—Metzudas Dovid who are second in rank³ in the event that he was incapacitated to perform the service in the Beis Hamikdosh.—Radak to the *Kohanim Gedolim*.

footnotes:
² The kohein
³ They would substitute for the kohein gadol


###### Rashi on II Kings 23:4:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/23:4:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/23:4:2)


**שֹׁמְרֵי הַסַּף.** אֲמַרְכָּלִים הַמְמֻנִּים עַל כָּל צָרְכֵי הַבַּיִת, וּמַפְתְּחוֹת הָעֲזָרָה בְּיָדָם.


**And the gatekeepers.** The supervisors in charge over all the necessities of the Beis [Hamikdosh], and the keys of the Temple's courtyard were in their charge.⁴

footnotes:
⁴ See Rashi above, 12:10.


###### Rashi on II Kings 23:4:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/23:4:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/23:4:3)


**בְּשַׁדְמוֹת קִדְרוֹן.** (תַּרְגּוּם:) בְּמֵישָׁר קִדְרוֹן.


**In the plains of Kidron.** In the plains of Kidron.


###### Rashi on II Kings 23:4:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/23:4:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/23:4:4)


**וְנָשָׂא אֶת עֲפָרָם בֵּית אֵל.** אֶל מָקוֹם טָמֵא, אֲשֶׁר טִמֵּא שָׁם אֶת הַמִּזְבֵּחַ שֶׁעָשָׂה יָרָבְעָם, וְשָׂרַף עָלָיו אֶת כֹּהֲנֵי הַבָּמוֹת.


**And he carried their ashes to Beis Eil.** To an unclean place, where he defiled the altar that Yorovom had made,⁵ and he burned upon it the priests of the high places.

footnotes:
⁵ See v. 15 below.



### Verse 5

###### Rashi on II Kings 23:5:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/23:5:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/23:5:1)


**וַיְקַטֵּר בַּבָּמוֹת.** וְאוֹתָן שֶׁקִּטְּרוּ בַּבָּמוֹת בְּעָרֵי יְהוּדָה.


**And who had burned incense on the high places.** And those who burned incense on the high places in the cities of Yehudah.



### Verse 7

###### Rashi on II Kings 23:7:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/23:7:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/23:7:1)


**אֶת בָּתֵּי הַקְּדֵשִׁים.** בָּתֵּי הַזִּמָּה.


**The houses devoted to pagan worship.** The houses of lewdness.⁶ and lived in seclusion.—Metzudas Dovid

footnotes:
⁶ Alternatively, houses designated for those individuals who dedicated their lives to worship the asheiroh


###### Rashi on II Kings 23:7:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/23:7:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/23:7:2)


**אֹרְגוֹת שָׁם בָּתִּים.** יְרִיעוֹת.


**Would weave coverings** Curtains.⁷.—Metzudas Dovid

footnotes:
⁷ The curtains that they wove were used as enclosures for the asheiroh



### Verse 9

###### Rashi on II Kings 23:9:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/23:9:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/23:9:1)


**לֹא יַעֲלוּ.** הַכֹּהֲנִים שֶׁקִּטְּרוּ בַּבָּמוֹת לַעֲבוֹדָה זָרָה, לֹא יַעֲלוּ עוֹד לְשָׁרֵת עַל מִזְבַּח ה'.


**Were not permitted to ascend.** The priests who burned incense on the high places to idols, were not permitted to ascend anymore to serve on the Altar of Adonoy.


###### Rashi on II Kings 23:9:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/23:9:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/23:9:2)


**כִּי אִם אָכְלוּ מַצּוֹת.** כְּלוֹמַר כִּי אִם בְּדָבָר זֶה לֹא יָצְאוּ מִכְּהֻנָּתָם, שֶׁמֻּתָּרִין לֶאֱכֹל בַּקֳּדָשִׁים, הֲרֵי הֵם כְּבַעֲלֵי מוּמִין, חוֹלְקִין וְאוֹכְלִין אֲבָל לֹא מַקְרִיבִין.


**They would eat matzohs.** I.e., but in this thing they would not be disqualified from their priesthood, that they are allowed to eat hallowed things. They are like blemished [*kohanim*] and they share and eat [of the sacrifice], but they may not offer [sacrifices].⁸

footnotes:
⁸ See Maseches Menachos 109a.


###### Rashi on II Kings 23:9:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/23:9:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/23:9:3)


**אָכְלוּ מַצּוֹת.** מְנָחוֹת, וְהוּא הַדִּין לְכָל הַקֳּדָשִׁים.


**They would eat matzohs.** Meal-offerings,⁹ and similarly, all hallowed things.

footnotes:
⁹ All meal offerings were prepared in an unleavened state [=matzohs]. See Vayikra 2:10-11.



### Verse 10

###### Rashi on II Kings 23:10:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/23:10:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/23:10:1)


**הַתֹּפֶת.** הוּא הַמֹּלֶךְ, וְעַל שֵׁם שֶׁהָיוּ הַכֹּהֲנִים מַקִּישִׁים בְּתֻפִּים, שֶׁלֹּא יִשְׁמַע הָאָב אֶת נַהֲמַת הַבֵּן כְּשֶׁהוּא נִכְוָה בִּידֵי הַצּוּרָה שֶל עֲבוֹדָה זָרָה מֹלֶךְ, קְרָאוּהוּ תֹּפֶת.


**The Tophes.** This was the Molech, it is named [תפת] because priests would bang on drums [=תופים] so that the father should not hear the groans of the child when he was burned by the pagan image,¹⁰ Molech, they called it Tophes.

footnotes:
¹⁰ According to Ralbag, the service of Molech involved the passing of a child by priests between two fires.



### Verse 11

###### Rashi on II Kings 23:11:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/23:11:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/23:11:1)


**אֶת הַסּוּסִים.** אוֹתָם הַמִּשְׁתַּחֲוִים לַחַמָּה, הָיוּ לָהֶם סוּסִים מוּכָנִים לָצֵאת לִקְרַאת הַחַמָּה בַּבֹּקֶר.


**The horses.** Those [people] who prostrated themselves to the sun, had horses ready in the morning to go out toward the sun.¹¹

footnotes:
¹¹ The idolatrous kings of Yehudah would ride these horses from the entrance of the Beis Hamikdosh to the chamber of Nesan Melech which was located to the east of the city.—Ralbag and Metzudas Dovid


###### Rashi on II Kings 23:11:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/23:11:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/23:11:2)


**נְתַן מֶלֶךְ.** כָּךְ שְׁמוֹ.


**Nesan Melech.** That was its name.


###### Rashi on II Kings 23:11:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/23:11:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/23:11:3)


**אֲשֶׁר בַּפַּרְוָרִים.** לֹא יָדַעְתִּי מָה הֵם.


**In the outskirts.** I do not know what they are.¹²

footnotes:
¹² ”רורים are the open space areas located outside the city. In Yechezkeil 27:28 the verse states, “the surrounding areas [=מגרשות] will tremble,” and Targum renders “פרורים” for מגרשות.—Radak



### Verse 12

###### Rashi on II Kings 23:12:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/23:12:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/23:12:1)


**וַיָּרָץ מִשָּׁם.** וּבִעֲרָם מִשָּׁם.


**He quickly removed them from there.** And he eliminated them from there.¹³

footnotes:
¹³ Alternatively, וירץ stems from רוץ [=to run], and the phrase means, “he quickly removed them,” or וירץ stems from רצץ [=to smash], and the phrase means, “and he smashed them,” i.e. after he demolished the altars he further smashed the pieces.—Radak



### Verse 13

###### Rashi on II Kings 23:13:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/23:13:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/23:13:1)


**לְהַר הַמַּשְׁחִית.** הַר הַזֵּיתִים, הוּא הַר הַמִּשְׁחָה, וְכֵיוָן שֶׁהִזְכִּיר אֶת שְׁמוֹ עַל יְדֵי עֲבוֹדָה זָרָה, שִׁנָּה שְׁמוֹ לִגְנַאי.


**Of Har Hamashchis.** [I.e.,] the Mount of Olives, which is known as the Mount of Oil. But because its name is mentioned in reference to idolatry, [Scripture] changed its name to a derogatory expression.¹⁴

footnotes:
¹⁴ I.e., from הר המשחה [=Mount of Oil] to הר המשחית [=Mount of Destroyer].



### Verse 17

###### Rashi on II Kings 23:17:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/23:17:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/23:17:1)


**מַה הַצִּיּוּן הַלָּז.** רָאָה קֶבֶר, מִצִּדּוֹ אֶחָד עָלָה קִמְּשׁוֹנִים וַחֲרוּלִים, וְצִדּוֹ אֶחָד הֲדַסִּים וּבְשָׂמִים, וְתָמַהּ עָלָיו וְאָמַר מַה זֶּה. וְהֵשִׁיבוּ:


**”What is this marker?”** He saw a grave, one side of which was overgrown with thorns and thistles, and whose other side was graced with myrtles and spices. He wondered about it and said, “What is this?”¹⁵ And they replied.

footnotes:
¹⁵ Alternatively, he saw a grave with a large and distinctive marker that was unlike the markers of other graves. The old [=false] prophet was certain that the prophecy of the prophet from Yehudah would be fulfilled and that Yoshiyahu would spare the bones of that prophet. He hoped that his bones would also be spared because of their proximity and he therefore instructed that a large and distinctive marker be erected on that prophet's grave.—Radak


###### Rashi on II Kings 23:17:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/23:17:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/23:17:2)


**הַקֶּבֶר אִישׁ הָאֱלֹהִים.** וּנְבִיא הַשֶּׁקֶר שֶׁצִּוָּה לְבָנָיו שֶׁיִּקְבְּרוּהוּ אֶצְלוֹ.


**”It is the grave of the man of God.”** And the false prophet, who commanded his sons that they bury him next to him.¹⁶

footnotes:
¹⁶ See I Melochim 13:31-32. Although they were buried over 350 years ago, the people knew about it by tradition handed down from father to son.—Radak



### Verse 19

###### Rashi on II Kings 23:19:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/23:19:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/23:19:1)


**אֲשֶׁר בְּעָרֵי שֹׁמְרוֹן.** שֶׁהָיָה יֹאשִׁיָּהוּ מֶלֶךְ עַל כָּל יִשְׂרָאֵל, מַה שֶּׁחָזְרוּ מֵעֲשֶׂרֶת הַשְּׁבָטִים עַל יְדֵי יִרְמְיָהוּ.


**In the cities of Shomron.** For Yeshayohu was king over all Yisroel, of those who returned from the ten tribes through Yirmiyohu.



### Verse 22

###### Rashi on II Kings 23:22:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/23:22:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/23:22:1)


**לֹא נַעֲשָׂה כַּפֶּסַח הַזֶּה.** לֹא נִתְקַבְּצוּ רֹב עַם בְּכָל פֶּסַח כְּמוֹ שֶׁנִּתְקַבְּצוּ בָּזֶה.


**Such a Pesach sacrifice had not been offered.** So many people had not assembled at any Pesach sacrifice as had assembled at this one.¹⁷

footnotes:
¹⁷ The magnitude of the participation by the vast number of people was unprecedented. Alternatively, since the time of the Judges, the people had never celebrated Pesach so devotedly.—Ralbag


###### Rashi on II Kings 23:22:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/23:22:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/23:22:2)


**מִימֵי הַשֹּׁפְטִים.** מִימֵי שְׁמוּאֵל, כָּתוּב בְּדִבְרֵי הַיָּמִים (דה"ב לה יח), לְפִי שֶׁאַף שְׁמוּאֵל הֶחֱזִיר יִשְׂרָאֵל לְמוּטָב, וְקִבְּצָם הַמִּצְפָּתָה. דָּבָר אַחֵר: לֹא נַעֲשָׂה כַּפֶּסַח הַזֶּה בִּירוּשָׁלַיִם, (אֲבָל) מִשֶּׁמָּלְכוּ מַלְכֵי יִשְׂרָאֵל וּמַלְכֵי יְהוּדָה, לֹא עָשׂוּ פֶּסַח כָּל יִשְׂרָאֵל בִּירוּשָׁלַיִם, לְפִי שֶׁנֶּחְלְקָה הַמַּלְכוּת לִשְׁנַיִם מִיָּרָבְעָם, וְהָיוּ הוֹלְכִים לָעֵגֶל שֶׁבְּבֵית אֵל וּבְדָן, עַד עַכְשָׁיו שֶׁגָּלוּ עֲשֶׂרֶת הַשְּׁבָטִים, וְהֶחֱזִירָם יִרְמְיָהוּ וּמָלַךְ עֲלֵיהֶם יֹאשִׁיָּהוּ, וּבָאוּ כֻּלָּם לִירוּשָׁלַיִם.


**Since the time of the Judges.** ”Since the time of Shmuel,”¹⁸ In Divrei Hayomim, it is written, because Shmuel, too, led Yisroel to improve their ways,¹⁹ and he assembled them to Mitzpoh. Alternatively, such a Pesach had not been offered to God in Yerusholayim, [i.e.,] (but) when the kings of Yisroel and the kings of Yehudah reigned, all Yisroel did not perform a Pesach sacrifice in Yerusholayim, because the kingdom had been divided in two since Yorovom, and they would go to the calf in Beis Eil and in Dan until now, that the ten tribes were exiled, and Yirmiyohu brought them back, and Yoshiyahu reigned over them, and they all came to Yerusholayim.

footnotes:
¹⁸ II Divrei Hayomim 35:18.
¹⁹ Alternatively, Shmuel is mentioned because he was the last of the Judges.—Metzudas Dovid



### Verse 24

###### Rashi on II Kings 23:24:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/23:24:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/23:24:1)


**הַתְּרָפִים.** צוּרוֹת שֶׁמְּדַבְּרִים עַל יְדֵי כְשָׁפִים, וְהָעוֹשֶׂה אוֹתָן צָרִיךְ לְכַוֵּן שָׁעָה אַחַת בְּשָׁנָה, וְשָׁנָה אַחַת רְאוּיָה לְכָךְ.


**The *teraphim*.** Images²⁰ as being made in the likeness of a human figure. that speak through sorcery, and the one who make them must determine a certain time in the year, and a certain year that is appropriate for this.

footnotes:
²⁰ In I Shmuel 19:16, Rashi described teraphim



### Verse 30

###### Rashi on II Kings 23:30:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/23:30:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/23:30:1)


**וַיִּמְשְׁחוּ.** לֹא הָיָה צָרִיךְ מְשִׁיחָה, אֶלָּא מִפְּנֵי שֶׁהָיָה יְהוֹיָקִים אָחִיו גָּדוֹל מִמֶּנּוּ שְׁתֵּי שָׁנִים, כְּמוֹ שֶׁכָּתוּב בָּעִנְיָן.


**Anointed.** He did not require anointment,²¹ nonetheless [he was anointed] because Yehoyakim his brother was two years older than he, as is explained in this section.²²

footnotes:
²¹ Ordinarily, a king's son does not need to be anointed unless there is a dispute over his successor. See Maseches Kerisos 5b.
²² Verse 31 below states that Yehoachaz was twenty three years old when he became king and v. 36 states that his brother Yehoyakim was twenty-five years old when he became king. Being that Yehoachaz reigned for only three months as indicated in v. 31, it follows that there is a two year age difference between them.



### Verse 35

###### Rashi on II Kings 23:35:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/23:35:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/23:35:1)


**אִישׁ כְּעֶרְכּוֹ.** כְּפִי עָשְׁרוֹ.


**Each one according to his assessment.** According to his wealth.



## Chapter 24


### Verse 3

###### Rashi on II Kings 24:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/24:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/24:3:1)


**אַךְ עַל פִּי ה' הָיְתָה.** כָּל הָרָעָה הַבָּאָה בִּיהוּדָה עַל יְדֵי הַגְּדוּדִים הָאֵלֶּה.


**Indeed, it was by the order of Adonoy.** All the calamity that befell Yehudah through these bands.¹

footnotes:
¹ I.e., all the calamity had been preordained from the time of Menashe's reign.—Radak



### Verse 6

###### Rashi on II Kings 24:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/24:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/24:6:1)


**וַיִּשְׁכַּב יְהוֹיָקִים.** לֹא עַל מִטָּתוֹ, כִּי אֲסָרוֹ נְבוּכַדְנֶאצַּר בִּנְחֻשְׁתַּיִם לְהוֹלִיכוֹ בָּבֶלָה, וְהָיוּ מְגָרְרִין אוֹתוֹ וּמֵת בְּיָדָם, כְּמוֹ שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר: קְבוּרַת חֲמוֹר יִקָּבֵר סָחוֹב וְהַשְׁלֵךְ. וּבְדִבְרֵי הַיָּמִים כָּתַב שֶׁאֲסָרוֹ נְבוּכַדְנֶאצַּר לְהוֹלִיכוֹ בָּבֶלָה.


**Yehoyakim lay with his forefathers.** [He did] not die in his bed, for Nevuchadnetzar bound him with chains to take him to Bavel, and when they were dragging him he died in their hands,² as it is stated, ”With the burial of a donkey will he be buried, dragged and thrown,”³ and in Divrei Hayomim it is written⁴ that Nevuchadnetzar bound him to take him to Bavel.

footnotes:
² Scripture therefore, does not mention his burial. There are several different opinions surrounding the circumstances of Yehoyakim's death and burial. See Vayikra Rabboh 19:6.
³ Yirmiyahu 22:19.
⁴ II Divrei Hayomim 36:6. The verse there states, “Nevuchadnetzar king of Bavel attacked him [Yehoyakim] and bound him in chains to take him to Bavel.”



### Verse 7

###### Rashi on II Kings 24:7:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/24:7:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/24:7:1)


**לָצֵאת מֵאַרְצוֹ.** לַעֲזֹר לִיהוֹיָקִים.


**Left his land.** To aid Yehoyakim.


###### Rashi on II Kings 24:7:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/24:7:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/24:7:2)


**כִּי לָקַח מֶלֶךְ בָּבֶל וְגוֹ'.** שֶׁנִּצְּחוֹ בַּמִּלְחָמָה בִּשְׁנַת הָרְבִיעִית לִיהוֹיָקִים, בְּכַרְכָּמִישׁ עַל נְהַר פְּרָת, כַּכָּתוּב בְּסֵפֶר יִרְמְיָהוּ.


**For the king of Bavel had taken, etc.** For he defeated him in battle in the fourth year of Yehoyakim in Charchemish on the Euphrates River, as it is written in the Book of Yirmiyohu.⁵

footnotes:
⁵ 46:2.



### Verse 12

###### Rashi on II Kings 24:12:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/24:12:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/24:12:1)


**וַיֵּצֵא יְהוֹיָכִין מֶלֶךְ יְהוּדָה עַל מֶלֶךְ בָּבֶל.** לַעֲשׂוֹת לוֹ כִּרְצוֹנוֹ, וְלֹא לְמִלְחָמָה.


**Yehoyachin king of Yehudah, came out to the king of Bavel.** That he do to him as he wishes, and not for war.⁶

footnotes:
⁶ Nevuchadnetzar, at this time, did not come to destroy Yerusholayim. He only demanded that Yehoyachin be handed over to him. See Vayikra Rabboh 19:6.



### Verse 14

###### Rashi on II Kings 24:14:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/24:14:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/24:14:1)


**הֶחָרָשׁ וְהַמַּסְגֵּר.** תִּרְגֵּם יוֹנָתָן: אֻמָּנַיָּא וְתַרְעַיָּא. וְרַבּוֹתֵינוּ אָמְרוּ: חֲכָמִים גְּדוֹלִים בַּתּוֹרָה, שֶׁכְּשֶׁאֶחָד פּוֹתֵחַ הַכֹּל שׁוֹתְקִין, כְּמוֹ שֶׁכָּתוּב: הַחֲרִישׁוּ אֵלַי אִיִּים, מַסְגֵּר, הַכֹּל יוֹשְׁבִין לְפָנָיו וּלְמֵדִין הֵימֶנּוּ, כְּמוֹ שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר: וּפָתַח וְאֵין סוֹגֵר וְסָגַר וְאֵין פּוֹתֵחַ.


**Craftsmen and gatekeepers.** [Targum] Yonoson rendered the craftsmen and the sentries of the gates. But, our Rabbis said that these were great Torah scholars,⁷ that when one would begin [to speak], all remained silent [=חרש], as it is written, “Be silent before Me, O islands.”⁸ ”מסגר” [is one who] all sit before him and learn from him, as it is stated, ”And he will open, and no one will close, and he will close, and no one will open.”⁹ ¹⁰

footnotes:
⁷ I.e., “craftsmen and gatekeepers” is a metaphoric allusion to Torah scholars. See Maseches Sanhedrin 38a.
⁸ Yeshayohu 41:1.
⁹ Ibid 22:22.
¹⁰ See Maseches Gittin 88a.



### Verse 15

###### Rashi on II Kings 24:15:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/24:15:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/24:15:1)


**וְאֶת אֵילֵי הָאָרֶץ.** אֵלּוּ חֹרֵי יְהוּדָה וּבִנְיָמִין, וְצַדִּיקִים הָיוּ, עֲלֵיהֶם הַכָּתוּב אוֹמֵר: כַּתְּאֵנִים הַטּוֹבוֹת הָאֵלֶּה כֵּן אַכִּיר אֶת גָּלוּת יְהוּדָה.


**And the dignitaries of the land.** These are the dignitaries of Yehudah and Binyomin, who were righteous men. Concerning them Scripture states, ”Like these good figs, so will I recognize the exiles of Yehudah.”¹¹

footnotes:
¹¹ Yirmiyahu 24:5.



### Verse 16

###### Rashi on II Kings 24:16:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/24:16:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/24:16:1)


**שִׁבְעַת אֲלָפִים.** וּלְמַעְלָה הוּא אוֹמֵר עֲשֶׂרֶת אֲלָפִים, בָּא הַכָּתוּב הַשְּׁלִישִׁי וְהִכְרִיעַ בְּסֵפֶר יִרְמְיָהוּ: זֶה הָעָם אֲשֶׁר הִגְלָה נְבוּכַדְנֶצַּר בִּשְׁנַת שֶׁבַע יְהוּדִים שְׁלשֶׁת אֲלָפִים, אֱמֹר מֵעַתָּה שְׁלשֶׁת אֲלָפִים הָיוּ מִשֵּׁבֶט יְהוּדָה, וְשִׁבְעַת אֲלָפִים מִבִּנְיָמִין וּשְׁאָר שְׁבָטִים, וְאַף בְּסֵדֶר עוֹלָם לָמַדְנוּ כֵּן.


**Seven thousand.** But above he states, “ten thousand.”¹² The third verse comes to reconcile [the difference], in the Book of Yirmiyohu, ”This is the people whom Nevuchadnetzar exiled in the seventh year, three thousand Jews.”¹³ Jews.” We deduce from here that three thousand were of the tribe of Yehudah and seven thousand were of Binyomin and the other tribes. Also in Seder Olam we learned this.

footnotes:
¹² Above, v. 14.
¹³ Yirmiyahu 52:28. The verse there states, “three thousand and twenty-three



### Verse 17

###### Rashi on II Kings 24:17:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/24:17:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/24:17:1)


**וַיַּסֵּב אֶת שְׁמוֹ צִדְקִיָּהוּ.** יָהּ יַצְדִּיק עָלֶיךָ אֶת הַדִּין, אִם תִּמְרֹד בִּי.


**And changed his name to Tzidkiyohu.** May God justify the judgment upon you if you rebel against me.¹⁴

¹⁴ See Maseches Horiyos 11b.



### Verse 20

###### Rashi on II Kings 24:20:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/24:20:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/24:20:1)


**כִּי עַל אַף ה' הָיְתָה וְגוֹ'.** לְפִיכָךְ: וַיִּמְרֹד צִדְקִיָּהוּ בְּמֶלֶךְ בָּבֶל, נָתַן הַקָּדוֹשׁ בָּרוּךְ הוּא בְּלִבּוֹ לִמְרֹד בּוֹ, כְּדֵי שֶׁיִּגְלֶה.


**Indeed the wrath of Adonoy was, etc.** Therefore, Tzidkiyohu rebelled against the king of Bavel. The Holy One, Blessed Is He, gave him the desire to rebel against him in order that he would be exiled.¹⁵

¹⁵ God incited Tzidkiyohu to rebel in order to exile the Bnei Yisroel from Eretz Yisroel.



## Chapter 25


### Verse 1

###### Rashi on II Kings 25:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/25:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/25:1:1)


**בִשְׁנַת הַתְּשִׁיעִית לְמָלְכוֹ.** שֶל צִדְקִיָּהוּ.


**In the ninth year of his reign.** Of Tzidkiyohu.¹

footnotes:
¹ Which corresponds to Nevuchadnetzar's seventeenth year.—Radak


###### Rashi on II Kings 25:1:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/25:1:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/25:1:2)


**דָּיֵק.** תִּרְגֵּם יוֹנָתָן: כַּרְקוּם.


**Siege towers.** [Targum Yonoson] rendered כרקום [=works of siege].



### Verse 4

###### Rashi on II Kings 25:4:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/25:4:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/25:4:1)


**דֶּרֶךְ שַׁעַר בֵּין הַחֹמֹתַיִם.** מְעָרָה הוֹלֶכֶת מִבֵּיתוֹ עַד עַרְבוֹת יְרִיחוֹ, וּבָרַח לוֹ דֶּרֶךְ הַמְּעָרָה, וְהַקָּבָּ"ה זִמֵּן צְבִי הוֹלֵךְ עַל גַּג הַמְּעָרָה חוּץ לָעִיר, וְרָדְפוּ כַּשְׂדִּים אַחֲרֵי הַצְּבִי, וּכְשֶׁהִגִּיעוּ לְפֶתַח הַמְּעָרָה בְּעַרְבוֹת יְרִיחוֹ רָאוּהוּ וּלְכָדוּהוּ, הוּא שֶׁאָמַר: וּפָרַשְׂתִּי רִשְׁתִּי עָלָיו וְנִתְפַּשׂ בִּמְצוּדָתִי.


**By way of the gate between the two walls.** A cave went from his house until the Plains of Yericho, and he fled through the cave. The Holy One, Blessed Is He, ordained a deer walking on the roof of the cave. and the Kasdim pursued the deer, and when they reached the cave's entrance in the Plains of Yericho, they saw him and captured him. This is what [Scripture] states, ”And I will spread My net over him, and he shall be caught in My snare.”²

footnotes:
² Yechezkeil 12:13.



### Verse 6

###### Rashi on II Kings 25:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/25:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/25:6:1)


**וַיְדַבְּרוּ אִתּוֹ מִשְׁפָּט.** נִתְוַכַּח עִמּוֹ עַל הַשְּׁבוּעָה.


**And called him to account.** He debated with him concerning the oath.³ ⁴

footnotes:
³ Tzidkiyohu violated his oath of allegiance to Nevuchadnetzar.—Radak
⁴ The Gemara in Maseches Nedarim 65a states that Tzidkiyohu had discovered Nevuchadnetzar eating a live, or according to other opinions, an uncooked rabbit. He had him swear that he would never reveal what he had seen. However, Tzidkiyohu had his oath nullified and revealed Nevuchadnetzar's uncivilized behavior. When Nevuchadnetzar found out that people were making fun of him, he realized that Tzidkiyohu had broken his promise.



### Verse 9

###### Rashi on II Kings 25:9:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/25:9:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/25:9:1)


**וְאֶת כָּל בֵּית גָּדוֹל.** בָּתֵּי כְּנֵסִיּוֹת שֶׁמְּגַדְּלִין בָּהֶן תּוֹרָה וּתְפִלָּה וּבָתֵּי הַשָּׂרִים.


**And all the houses of the dignitaries.** [This includes] synagogues wherein Torah and prayer are aggrandized, and the houses of the princes.⁵

footnotes:
⁵ See Maseches Megillah 27a.



### Verse 11

###### Rashi on II Kings 25:11:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/25:11:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/25:11:1)


**וְאֶת הַנֹּפְלִים.** שֶׁשָּׁמְעוּ לְקוֹל נְבוּכַדְנֶצַּר וְיָצְאוּ אֵלָיו מִן הָעִיר, כְּמוֹ: בְּלֶכְתּוֹ אֶל צִקְלָג נָפְלוּ עָלָיו מִמְּנַשֶּׁה וְגוֹ'.


**And the defectors.** [Those] who obeyed Nevuchadnetzar and went out to him from the city, as in, “When he went to Tziklag, [members] of Menashe defected to him, etc.”⁶

footnotes:
⁶ I Divrei Hayomim 12:21.



### Verse 12

###### Rashi on II Kings 25:12:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/25:12:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/25:12:1)


**וּלְיוֹגְבִים.** חוֹפְרֵי הָאָרֶץ, לְשׁוֹן יְקָבִים, שֶׁהֵם חֲפִירוֹת בַּקַּרְקַע.


**And farmers.** Diggers of the earth, an expression of wine vats [=יקבים]⁷ which are diggings in the ground.⁸

footnotes:
⁷ The ‘ג’ of ‘יגבים’ and the ‘ק’ of ‘יקבים’ are interchangeable.
⁸ Alternatively, יקבים are wine pressers.—Metzudas Tzion Or יקבים refer to fishermen who catch the חלזון which was pressed to extract its blood from which the blue dye [for תכלת] is obtained. Nevuchadnetzar used the dye for his robes and therefore allowed these people to remain.



### Verse 17

###### Rashi on II Kings 25:17:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/25:17:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/25:17:1)


**וְקוֹמַת הַכֹּתֶרֶת שָׁלשׁ אַמּוֹת.** וּבִתְחִלַּת הַסֵּפֶר הוּא אוֹמֵר: חָמֵשׁ אַמּוֹת. וְשָׁם יִשַּׁבְתִּי אֶת הַמִּקְרָאוֹת.


**The height of the crown was three *amohs*.** And in the beginning of the Book [of Melochim] it states, ”five *amohs* [high].”⁹ I reconciled the verses there.¹⁰ high but its two lower *amohs* are not counted because the column was inserted into the crown two *amohs* deep and it served as a base for the crown.

footnotes:
⁹ Melochim 7:16.
¹⁰ Rashi reconciled the difference as follows: The crown was actually five amohs


###### Rashi on II Kings 25:17:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/25:17:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/25:17:2)


**וּשְׂבָכָה.** קופיי"א בְּלַעַ"ז, כְּמִין כּוֹבַע.


**And a netting.** (*Coife*, in O.F.) a type of hat.


###### Rashi on II Kings 25:17:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/25:17:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/25:17:3)


**וְכָאֵלֶּה.** רִמּוֹנִים.


**Had the same.** Pomegranates.



### Verse 19

###### Rashi on II Kings 25:19:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/25:19:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/25:19:1)


**הַסֹּפֵר שַׂר הַצָּבָא.** שֶׁהָיָה יוֹדֵעַ מִנְיַן כָּל עִיר וָעִיר, כַּמָּה אֲנָשִׁים מוֹצִיאָה לַצָּבָא.


**The scribe of the general of the army.** Who knew the number of each city, how many men it enlists into the army.



### Verse 23

###### Rashi on II Kings 25:23:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/25:23:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/25:23:1)


**כָּל שָׂרֵי הַחֲיָלִים.** שֶׁיָּצְאוּ מִן הָעִיר לְהֵחָבֵא בַּמְּצוּדוֹת וּבַטִּינָרִים וּבַצּוּרִים וּבַיְּעָרִים.


**All the officers of the armies.** Who had left the city to hide in the strongholds, boulders, rocks and forests.



### Verse 27

###### Rashi on II Kings 25:27:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/25:27:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/25:27:1)


**נָשָׂא אֱוִיל מְרֹדַךְ.** שֶׁמֵּת נְבוּכַדְנֶצַּר, וּמָלַךְ אֱוִיל מְרוֹדַךְ תַּחְתָּיו.


**Evil Merodach … elevated.** For Nevuchadnetzar died, and Evil Merodach reigned in his place.


###### Rashi on II Kings 25:27:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/25:27:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/25:27:2)


**בְּעֶשְׂרִים וְשִׁבְעָה לַחֹדֶשׁ.** וּבְיִרְמְיָהוּ הוּא אוֹמֵר: בְּעֶשְׂרִים וַחֲמִשָּׁה, אֶלָּא בְּעֶשְׂרִים וַחֲמִשָּׁה מֵת נְבוּכַדְנֶאצַּר, וְנִקְבַּר בְּעֶשְׂרִים וְשִׁשָּׁה, בְּעֶשְׂרִים וְשִׁבְעָה הוֹצִיאוֹ אֱוִיל מְרוֹדַךְ מִקִּבְרוֹ וּגְרָרוּהוּ, בִּשְׁבִיל לְבַטֵּל גְּזֵרוֹתָיו, כְּמוֹ שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר בִּישַׁעְיָהוּ: וְאַתָּה הָשְׁלַכְתָּ מִקִּבְרְךָ כְּנֵצֶר נִתְעָב וְגוֹ'.


**On the twenty-seventh day of the month.** But in Yirmiyohu it states, “On the twenty-fifth.”¹¹ Rather, on the twenty-fifth Nevuchadnetzar died and he was buried on the twenty-sixth. On the twenty-seventh, Evil Merodach removed him from his grave and dragged him about,¹² in order to nullify his decrees, as it is stated in Yeshayohu, ”and you have been cast out of your grave like a despised sapling, etc.”¹³

footnotes:
¹¹ Yirmiyahu 52:31.
¹² Evil Merodach was afraid that his father was not really dead and would return to claim his throne. This happened before when Nevuchadnetzar became insane. He left his throne and lived as a wild beast for seven years. See Daniel 4:29-34. In the interim one of his sons was appointed in his place. When Nevuchadnetzar came back he imprisoned his son. Evil Merodach wanted to make sure that would not happen again, and he therefore had Nevuchadnetzar's body removed from his grave and dragged along the ground, thus fulfilling Yeshayohu's prophecy.—Radak
¹³ Yeshayohu 14:19.



### Verse 28

###### Rashi on II Kings 25:28:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/25:28:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Kings/r/25:28:1)


**וַיְדַבֵּר אִתּוֹ טֹבוֹת.** דִּבְרֵי נִחוּמִים.


**He spoke kindly with him.** Words of consolation.
